# Development log

## First implementation batch — validated locally, 23 September 2026

User authorised development after the review and handover. Base commit `638ba87`; see the release record below for subsequent commits and PRs. Preserve unrelated untracked `docs/audit/` and `docs/ops/user-lookup-queries.sql`. No database reset has been performed.

### Work in this batch

- **W10/A4 — implemented:** shared editable-money formatting uses the selected decimal separator without losing stored precision. Whole/split transaction editing now uses the same helper instead of `String(amount)`. Regressions cover all five formats and unchanged positive/negative account saves. Tests reproduced 1.234 becoming 1234 before the fix. Other monetary input initializers were checked; percentage/count fields remain unchanged.
- **W02/J3, rule62 — partial:** browser preview amount and due date share one anchored, date-aware recurrence calculation. Short months clamp only that occurrence, including leap February, and later dates restore the anchor day. Preview uses the server's existing UTC date basis; tests cover local midnight/month-boundary differences in Malta and New York. Backend date logic already clamps correctly. This does not implement the separately agreed missed-deadline lifecycle; the existing automatic rollover model still requires replacement under W03.
- **W08/A2, rule63 — partial:** allocation commits validate source currency against the selected owned budget before touching prior transfers. Existing source/destination currency validation remains. Missing owned budget is rejected, not assigned an inferred currency. This closes the EUR-budget-to-GBP-accounts posting bug, but broader automatic FX removal/approval and account/jar linkage enforcement remain open.
- **W09/A3, rules52/64 — implemented:** shared rounding floors calculated shares to cents, then distributes the remainder by fractional size and displayed order. Equal, income-ratio and balance-tilt splits conserve the exact total without negative shares. Stored sub-cent amounts are conserved without silently changing precision policy. Handler regression verifies a 0.02 remainder across four people records only two 0.01 transfers; no extra money is committed.
- **W01:** traced actual-account/jar model and all relevant write paths. Not implemented by merely dropping plans from a sum; see foundation notes below.

### Test maintenance

Meaningful existing tests were retained. `JarLifecycleTests.EffectiveDate_PreservesMonthEndDay_AcrossRolls` was replaced by an eight-case theory, preserving its protection without a redundant standalone example. New regressions cover real observed failures and independent accounting invariants. No further obsolete tests belonged to the behaviours removed in this batch, so no blanket deletion was made. Automatic-FX and plan-as-money tests remain for replacement with their future corresponding implementations, not silent acceptance of the old rules.

### W01 foundation notes for the next developer

`Transaction` records account movement; `TransactionSplit` records budget attribution. Transfers currently cannot be split across budget lines. Add a distinct jar-allocation model rather than allowing transfers to masquerade as income or expenses.

The candidate model is signed, dated allocations associated with an operation, jar and account; transaction-backed entries also identify the relevant transaction/account leg. Initial allocations and same-account reallocations divide existing account money and create no bank movement. Unallocated funds reconcile account actuals minus signed jar balances. This is a technical design proposal, not yet deployed schema or a new product rule.

Implementation must cover:

1. Dated account openings (the current `Account` has no opening date), jar designation and ownership/currency validation, and persistent operation identity.
2. Initial allocation, transaction funding/withdrawal splits and same-account reallocation; outgoing funds identify the jar/unallocated sources.
3. Completed cross-account transfers with explicit user confirmation and idempotent pairing of imported statement sides.
4. One shared dated balance service used by JarBalances, BudgetActuals savings availability, CreateJar responses and account reconciliation. Planned amounts must never enter actual sums.
5. All write paths: create/update/delete transaction, transfers, both imports, allocation commits and account-opening edits. A protected manual-transfer path alone leaves bypasses.
6. Automatic savings-line jars in AddBudgetItem, templates and registration seeding must obtain a savings-account selection. Do not create valid-looking unbacked jars or assign an arbitrary account.
7. Account deletion must not detach jar/transaction history. Additive schema changes must preserve development data until handling is agreed; no invented opening dates or planned-as-actual backfill.
8. SQL transaction/concurrency protection for withdrawals. Read-then-save sufficiency checks alone can overdraw savings under concurrent requests.

Questions still relevant before affected foundation paths are activated: handling existing local sample data with missing account/opening date (O12), import reconciliation/order (O1), changing a jar's designated account after money/history exists (O3). Account/budget currency policy was answered in rule63; do not ask it again. Questions on unrelated features do not block the bounded fixes above.

### W01 actual calculation foundation — 23 September 2026

Added domain-only `ActualAccountLedger` and `ActualSavingsLedger`. The first derives dated account balances from the explicit opening date and authoritative account-currency transactions; the second reconciles signed actual jar/unallocated postings to those movements. Initial and same-account allocations sum to zero; transaction allocations exactly match their account leg, date and direction. Duplicate transaction/operation identities and missing allocations are rejected rather than silently counted or assigned. Archived jars remain in reconciliation; legacy jar opening fields and budget plans do not fund jars.

Rule 67 now confirms that dates before opening show an unavailable balance, represented by null monetary values. Negative jars are retained; account and unallocated deficits are exposed for write validation and review, not accepted as permission to post. Source limits for reallocations remain a pending user question, distinct from the agreed permission for actual spending to make a jar negative.

This is a calculation foundation, **not activation of the new ledger in the product**. No database schema, account/transaction handler or screen has switched to it. The old jar bug therefore remains until persistence, dated openings, every transaction/import writer, concurrency guards and the API/UI switch are completed together. Current account/jar definitions must not be reused blindly for historical periods after a designation change. Transfer matching, separately posted bank-leg dates and correction policies remain the documented O1/O3 questions; this calculation consumes the dates and identities it is explicitly given.

The affected test review and one redundant-assertion removal are recorded in TEST-PLAN. First targeted run: 35 passed after correcting a test constructor ambiguity. Full backend run: 844 passed. An additional deficit-distinction case was then added; final targeted validation and CI results belong in the PR record. No existing tests were disabled to obtain these results. No user database reset or migration occurred.

### W01 reallocation source limits — 23 September 2026

The user rejected moving 150 from a jar holding 100: rule 68 resolves the source-limit question above. `JarReallocationRules` checks the selected jar's available actual balance (or explicitly selected unallocated funds), preserves stored precision and never borrows from other jars implicitly. Cross-account moves also require enough bank funds. A same-account move changes ownership only and therefore does not debit bank money or impose that second cap. Negative actual jar balances remain visible but cannot fund reallocations. Unknown sources, unavailable dates, account deficits and non-positive requests are rejected.

The guard is separate from read calculations, which must still represent actual spending and candidate deficits truthfully. It remains part of the inactive ledger foundation: persistence must bind snapshots to the authorised account/date, validate destination and currency, obtain completed-transfer confirmation, recheck under concurrency protection and save atomically. No bank movement is recorded by preview or validation. Existing product endpoints are unchanged in this step.

Sixteen new tests use calculated account/jar snapshots, including the user's exact 500/100/400 example and the distinction between jar availability and bank money after another jar overspends. Negative source fixtures are produced by actual payments rather than by an invalid reallocation. Forty targeted reallocation/savings-ledger tests pass. Existing read-engine tests remain relevant; none was removed or disabled for this addition. Full-suite/build and release results are recorded in the PR.

The next pending O1 question concerns transfers whose departure and arrival bank dates differ: whether to retain each bank date and show the money in transit between them. Do not silently choose a posting-date or in-transit policy before the answer.

The user requested mainstream-app evidence before deciding the departure-only workflow. Official documentation checked on 24 September 2026 shows different approaches:

- [YNAB transfers](https://support.ynab.com/transfer-transactions-a-guide-HJOsZz4Jj?mobile-help=true) create entries in both account registers and later match imported transactions. Its [balance documentation](https://support.ynab.com/getting-started-with-reconciling-accounts-an-overview-Sy3JWx4Js?mobile-help=true) distinguishes cleared balances from a working balance that includes uncleared entries and underlies the plan.
- [Actual Budget transfers](https://actualbudget.org/docs/transactions/transfers/) link both entries while permitting independent dates and cleared/reconciled statuses, explicitly addressing money arriving days later.
- [Monarch transfers](https://help.monarch.com/hc/en-us/articles/360048393292-Understanding-transfers-categories) are excluded from budget, cash-flow and spending totals to prevent double-counting. This page does not establish the account-specific jar funding policy being designed here.

Recommendation presented for this project's already agreed account-specific jars: record confirmed departure, show the same amount in transit, and fund the receiving jar only on confirmed receipt; match statement evidence without another monetary effect. This is a proposal awaiting the user's answer, not a new confirmed rule or a claim that every app uses an identical transit workflow. Rule 70's negative-jar example also remains unanswered. Documentation-only follow-up checked rule numbering (1–70), local Markdown links and `git diff --check`; unchanged code was not retested locally.

The user subsequently answered rule 70's example: **"Require an explicit €50 reallocation to cover the deficit."** This resolves the account-backing question and supersedes the pending references above. `JarReallocationRules.ValidateResult` now checks the fully calculated result for every affected account: positive jars must not exceed actual account money, account balances must remain non-negative, and allocations must reconcile exactly. `Validate` remains a source check; it cannot stand in for resulting-account validation, since a covering move must be possible from an initially deficient allocation. Read calculations retain the genuine payment/deficit and the guard never selects another jar automatically. The new assertions and retained-test rationale are in TEST-PLAN. PR #255 is being updated and must pass fresh CI before merge. The separate departure-only confirmation question remains pending after the mainstream-app comparison.

After this clarification, targeted reallocation/savings-ledger tests pass **49/49** and the full backend suite passes **870/870**, with no skipped tests. `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx --no-restore -m:1 -nr:false -p:UseSharedCompilation=false --verbosity minimal` passes with zero warnings/errors. Both test commands used the same build flags; the targeted filter was `FullyQualifiedName~JarReallocationRulesTests|FullyQualifiedName~ActualSavingsLedgerTests`. `git diff --check` is clean. The PR now contains 25 new reallocation/backing cases in total. No product endpoint, database schema or user data was changed in this guard step; fresh CI and deployment evidence belong to the PR record.

The next calculation step adds explicit `TransferPostingDates` for each transfer identity. `ActualAccountLedger` uses the relevant account's own bank date; no receipt date means no destination movement. `ActualSavingsLedger` validates each jar operation against that same dated account leg, preventing a departure-date or legacy-date allocation from prematurely funding the receiving jar. `TransferLedger` preserves authoritative amounts/currencies and derives the unsettled amount from debited minus credited money; it does not initiate, match or persist transfers.

Seventeen new test cases cover the agreed 1000/200 accounts and 100 transfer before departure, while in transit and on arrival, including account/jar backing and the unchanged combined total. Additional cases protect unknown receipts, history, per-account opening cutoffs, duplicate or premature allocations and owner/currency integrity. Remaining validation and release evidence will be recorded with the PR.

Preserve both statement references on one transfer when implementing persistence and check every writer before switching readers. The low-level signed unsettled amount can faithfully represent explicit bank dates with receipt posted first; this is not a decision about accepting a receipt-first import without known departure details.

### W01/W07 pending receipt review — 25 September 2026

[PR #258](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/258) merged as `1b39cd6320b3beb26fc297d56e2f3cae2db7474e` after functional [CI run 36035212805](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36035212805), and staging/production deployment [36067422598](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36067422598) succeeded. This supersedes its earlier pending-release snapshot. Production health and home returned HTTP 200 at release. The unchanged dependency scan remains the separately documented non-required failure.

The user asked how an incoming transfer is identified. Read inspection confirmed that the current importer only hints from descriptions; the user selects the statement account, marks the row as a transfer and chooses the other account. Parsed statement entries do not supply a mapped counterparty account, and Account has no bank-identifier mapping. Existing content-level possible-duplicate hints do not implement the agreed two-sided transfer matching. Do not mistake an equal amount or a generic transfer description for proof of an owned-account transfer. Confirmed identities, distinct account-specific bank references, candidate selection and atomic idempotent matching still need persistence/API/UI work.

The user then approved holding an identified receipt for review until departure is supplied (rule 72). This supersedes the pending receipt-first question above. `TransferPostingDates` now accepts an unknown departure when a receipt is known. `TransferLedger` returns the preserved receipt as review evidence with no posted debit, credit or transit. `ActualAccountLedger` and `ActualSavingsLedger` expose the review details on both relevant accounts and keep their posted balances unchanged. Jar or unallocated postings cannot attach to the held receipt. Supplying departure resolves the review and activates the independently dated movements through the existing calculation. Completely missing bank dates are rejected; no date is inferred from the legacy transaction field.

This is still the **inactive calculation foundation**, not a delivered import review screen. A balance flagged `RequiresReview` is the posted ledger balance, not a claim that it reconciles with the bank. Callers must persist the pending statement evidence and both bank references, retain the selected accounts, display the discrepancy/action, obtain user confirmation and save the complete resolution with actual jar allocations under concurrency protection. No database schema/reset, bank execution, automatic matching, new access policy or correction policy was implemented here. Before-opening balances remain unavailable; a pending receipt known before opening remains review evidence once an account view is available, without crediting the opening amount twice.

### W01/W07 activation slice 1 — dated account openings, transfer bank legs and pending receipts — 25 September 2026

[PR #259](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/259) merged as `9b2504715ddf414b292f4a1aff45f78bfe54801e`; functional [CI run 36069574086](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36069574086) and staging/production deployment [36070284735](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36070284735) succeeded, production `/health` and `/` returned HTTP 200, and its PR description now carries that release record (the earlier update was blocked by a tooling approval failure, not by CI). The unchanged dependency scan remains the separately documented non-required failure.

This slice is the first change that switches product paths to the actual-money foundation. Scope: **accounts and transfers only**. Jars still read the legacy plan-based `JarBalances`/`BudgetActuals` figures; the planned-as-saved defect (J1/J2) is therefore **still open** and belongs to the next slice.

**Schema** (`20260925205235_AccountOpeningDatesAndTransferLegs`): `Accounts.OpeningDate` (date, nullable in the schema, required by the API); `Transactions.DepartedOn`, `Transactions.ReceivedOn`, `Transactions.ReceiptBankReference` (the destination statement's reference; `BankReference` is now the source/own-statement reference, rule 51); unique filtered index `(OwnerId, TransferAccountId, ReceiptBankReference)`; check constraint `CK_Transactions_TransferLegs` (a transfer carries at least one bank leg). Existing rows: no opening date is invented — such accounts read as *balance unavailable* until their owner records the date (rule 67); existing single-date transfers get `DepartedOn = ReceivedOn = Date`, which is exactly what the legacy model recorded, so nothing new is inferred. The project development database has **not** been reset here (the LocalDB `ZeroBudget` target is on the developer's Windows machine); rule 65 still authorises that reset when this schema is applied locally. Migrations run at API startup on deploy, as before.

**Domain/Application:** `Account.OpeningDate`; `Transaction.DepartedOn/ReceivedOn/ReceiptBankReference` and derived `TransferStatus` (Completed / InTransit / PendingReview); `TransferLedger.PostingDates`. New `AccountLedgerReader` is the single account read path (replaces `AccountBalances`): opening balance at the start of the opening date, income/expense/transfer legs posted from that date to the as-of date (today), in-transit out/in, and pending receipts on both accounts. It feeds `GetAccounts`, `GetAccountReconciliation`, `Create/UpdateAccount` responses and `AllocationPlanner`'s savings-balance tilt. `AccountDto` gains `openingDate`, nullable `currentBalance`, `balanceAsOf`, `openingDateMissing`, `inTransitOut/In`, `pendingReceiptCount/Total`, `requiresReview`; reconciliation `currentBalance`/`float` are nullable.

**Writers traced and changed:** `CreateAccount`/`UpdateAccount` require the opening date. `CreateTransfer` records the confirmed departure (`DepartedOn`, required) and an optional receipt; receipt before departure is rejected on manual entry. New `PUT /api/transactions/{id}/transfer-legs` (`ConfirmTransferLegsCommand`, allowed for Limited like transfers) fills only unknown legs and statement references — repeats are no-ops, a different value against a confirmed leg is refused (corrections remain an O1 question). `UpdateTransaction` clears the legs when a transfer is re-typed. `DeleteAccount` refuses while the account is a side of any transfer (clearing a pointer would leave a half-movement and discard a statement reference). `CommitImport`: a debit row marked as a transfer records the departure leg; a credit row records the receipt only and is held for review (rule 72); the user-selected `matchTransferId` confirms the complementary open leg of a recorded transfer instead of inserting a row (rules 8/50/51), validated for accounts, direction, amount, currency and leg state; dedup and race recovery cover both reference indexes through the shared `StatementReferences`. `PreviewImport` lists `transferMatches` per row (same statement account on the open leg, same amount and currency; all such candidates regardless of date, nearest first — the user must choose; one candidate is never auto-selected). `ImportStatement` (direct CAMT/HSBC) uses the same known-reference definition. `AllocateIncome` now requires the "I've completed these transfers" confirmation and the departure date (rules 7/29), records departures only (receipt optional), retains unchanged prior transfers, withdraws superseded in-transit ones and refuses to discard a transfer whose receipt is confirmed; the result carries `transfersRetained`. `FxRateResolver` never rates or flags a transfer (rule 42).

**Client:** Accounts page (opening date on add/edit, *Not available* balances, in-transit and pending-review badges, hero footnotes, server message on refused delete), account detail (unavailable balance, transit/pending tiles, status badges), Transactions (transfer form: departure date, optional receipt date, confirmation checkbox; register status badges with inline *Confirm receipt* / *Add departure date*), Import (candidate select that also fixes the counterparty, held-for-review / departed notes, `matchTransferId` in the commit, Matched / Pending review result tiles), Allocation (record modal with departure date and confirmation; unchanged-re-run banner; unavailable savings balance shown as such).

**Implementation choices made within the agreed rules (object if wrong):** legacy transfers backfilled as same-day (their recorded meaning); accounts without an opening date read *unavailable*, never zero, and are not counted in the hero total; candidate suggestions apply no date tolerance; manual receipt-before-departure rejected while statement-supplied dates are accepted as given; account deletion blocked by transfers; allocation re-run keeps unchanged transfers rather than deleting and recreating them.

**Still inactive / next:** jar allocations (`JarLedgerOperation` persistence, designated-account FK, initial allocation, same-/cross-account reallocation, `ActualSavingsLedger` readers for jars, budget savings-available and reconciliation), rule 10/11 savings-account non-negative enforcement with SQL-level concurrency protection, import review holding (O1 batch/missing-history), correction of confirmed legs (O1), and the undesignated-jar question raised below.

## Jar allocation slice (W01 jars, W05 jar cutoff; rules 1–6, 9, 26, 40, 48, 67, 68, 70, 73–81) — 26 September 2026

**Decisions taken first.** Rules 73–81 were agreed in conversation on 26 September (see AGREED-RULES): every jar has a designated account of any non-credit-card type; no account may go negative; credit cards are not accounts; optional bank identifiers; "saved so far" is a dated initial allocation; incoming money prompts for allocation when recorded (deferred to the next slice); outgoing money identifies its jar through the budget line; a jar's account changes only at zero balance; accounts-first onboarding with no sample data and no template savings lines, after research on EveryDollar, YNAB, Monarch, Goodbudget, Actual Budget, Monzo Pots and Starling Spaces (accounts-first apps ask for balances first because only money on hand can be assigned; none pre-loads sample data; goal forms are the same fields everywhere).

**Schema** (`20260926153122_JarLedgerAllocations`): `Jars.FundingAccountId` NOT NULL with a Restrict foreign key to Accounts; `Jars.OpeningBalance`/`OpeningAsOf` dropped; new `JarAllocations` (OwnerId, AccountId, Kind InitialAllocation/Reallocation/Movement, Date, TransactionId cascade, Note, CreatedUtc) and `JarAllocationPostings` (JarId Restrict, Amount 18,4); unique filtered index `(TransactionId, AccountId)`; `Accounts.Iban/Bic/SortCode/AccountNumber`. The migration first deletes jars with no (or a dangling) account: pre-launch data only (rule 65); their savings lines become plain lines through the existing SetNull key and their transactions are untouched. Nothing is invented.

**Domain.** `ActualSavingsLedger.Calculate` accepts any non-credit-card account, keeps a re-designated jar's zero-sum history reconcilable, and exposes `PositiveAllocations`/`HasBackingDeficit` (rule 70). `ActualAccountLedger.Entries` is public so the application reader can attribute movements. `BudgetItem.Jar` navigation added.

**Application.** `JarLedgerReader` is the single jar read path: per jar-backed account it reads the account ledger, takes the stored allocations (a transaction-linked allocation whose postings no longer match its transaction is set aside and counted as unreconciled rather than failing the read), derives rule-79 attribution for every other movement (expense on a jar's line from the jar's account → that jar, split slices likewise, anything else → unallocated) and calls the domain ledger; `Apply` produces the candidate ledger for rule 68/70 checks. `CreateJar` requires the account (owner, not credit card) and records the initial allocation as a zero-sum operation validated against unallocated funds as of its date (not future, not before the account opening) under `BeginLedgerWriteAsync` — a ReadCommitted transaction holding `UPDLOCK, HOLDLOCK` on the account row on SQL Server, null on InMemory. `ReallocateJarMoney` (POST `/api/jars/reallocations`) moves money between one account's jars/unallocated with rules 68/70 under the same lock; cross-account is refused with a pointer to transfers (rule 7). `GET /api/jars/ledger` returns each account's balance, jars, unallocated, positive allocations and deficit flag. `UpdateJar` allows an account change only at zero balance (rule 80); `ArchiveJar` requires zero (rule 26). `GetJars`, `BudgetActuals.SavingsAvailable` (as of month end, today for the current/future month; null when unavailable), `CreateBudgetMonth` seeding (balance as of the day before the month) and account reconciliation (In jars, Unallocated, `HasBackingDeficit`) read the ledger; `JarBalances` is deleted. `TransactionInvariants.EnsureJarAccount` refuses attributing a payment (whole or slice) to a jar's line unless it leaves the jar's account, on every path (create, assign, update incl. account change, split, import). `AddBudgetItem` refuses savings-group lines (jars come from the jar form, which plants the line). `DeleteAccount` refuses accounts holding jars or allocation history. Accounts: `BankIdentifiers` normalises and format-checks the optional fields; validators refuse credit cards (rule 75) and negative openings (rule 74). Onboarding: `BudgetSeeder`, `StarterSavingsKindSeeder`, the startup backfill and `RegisterRequest.IncludeSampleData` are removed; `HouseholdBootstrap` seeds nothing; templates carry no savings groups.

**Choices within the rules (object if wrong):** initial allocation date defaults to today, backdatable to the account opening date; a stored allocation that drifts from its transaction is set aside for review rather than blocking every read of the account; a re-designated jar keeps its zero-sum history in the old account; expenses from a jar-backed account assigned to a non-jar line draw from unallocated for now (the rule-79 prompt arrives with the next slice, together with rule 78 incoming prompts, cross-account "Move money" via transfers, and rule 74 enforcement on manual entry/import holds).

**Client.** One shared `JarFormModal` (Jars page and Budget-page Savings groups, which now offer "Add jar" instead of a bare line): "Held in" required with no "No account" option and no credit cards, "Already set aside" + "Set aside on" on create only, server messages shown; `MoveMoneyModal` on each jar card (same-account moves from `GET /jars/ledger`, `POST /jars/reallocations`); jar cards show "Not available" and the account they are held in; a per-account "Unallocated" summary with the rule-70 "Jars exceed account money" badge; the Budget page's "Set up your budget" first-run checklist (accounts → month → jar); registration without the sample-data checkbox; Accounts page without "Credit card", with optional IBAN/BIC/sort code/account number (shown on the detail page), "Unallocated" in reconciliation with the deficit badge, and a client-side refusal of negative openings.

**Test maintenance** is itemised in TEST-PLAN (W01 jar allocation slice review). **Validation (this machine, .NET SDK 10.0.112; no SQL Server or Docker daemon):** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx` — 0 warnings, 0 errors; `dotnet test tests/AllotJar.Application.Tests` — **978 passed**, 0 failed, 0 skipped (928 before; 61 new cases, some legacy cases consolidated); the integration project compiles (its new `JarLedgerSqlTests`, including the concurrent-reallocation race, run in CI only). From `client`: `npm run test:quiet` — **717 passed in 56 files** (691 before); `npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` passed; ESLint on every new file clean and no new findings on changed files (the 9 pre-existing `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`/`only-export-components` errors remain); `git diff --check` clean. **Not run locally:** the SQL integration tests and the real-backend journeys (updated `budget-lifecycle`, `register`, `session-security`, `households-roles`, `import-lifecycle`, `invite` specs — registration seeds nothing, so they start months from the Essentials template — and the new `jars-ledger.spec.ts`); CI supplies those and their results are recorded on the PR.

**Review fixes (28 September, same PR).** Christopher's review of the PR found two skip-arithmetic bugs, both fixed before merge. (1) A skipped month lost the money that had already arrived this month: a €300 goal holding €200 today still planned €300 after skipping, not €100. `JarSchedule` now carries `BalanceNow` past a skipped first month in both the deadline and the fixed-monthly plan (`Rule61_MoneyThatArrivedThisMonth_StillCounts_WhenThisMonthIsSkipped`). (2) Creating a month that did not exist ignored skips in later months: with €300 due in December and November skipped, September and October previewed €150 each, yet creating October afterwards planned €75, leaving the schedule €75 short. `JarPlanner.PlanMonthAsync` now reads the skipped months of every existing month (`SavingsLines.SkippedMonthsByJarAsync`) and passes them into every figure it computes, and the Jars page card reads the same skips so its horizon agrees (`Rule61_CreatingAMissingMonth_PlansAroundTheMonthsSkippedAfterIt`, `Rule61_CreatingTheCurrentMonth_PlansAroundALaterSkippedMonth_Too`). All three tests fail on the previous head and pass on the fix.

## Money movement slice (W01/W07; rules 5, 7, 10, 11, 68, 70–72, 74, 78–80) — 26 September 2026

**Scope.** The slice the jar release left open: rule 74 on every write that moves money, the rule 78 incoming-money prompt on every path money reaches a jar-backed account, the rule 79 "which jar does this come from?" prompt for a payment on a line that is not a jar, and cross-account "Move money" recorded as a confirmed bank transfer (rule 80). No schema change: the `JarAllocations` table's `Movement` kind, reserved by the jar slice, is now written.

**Domain.** `AccountBalanceRules.FirstShortfall` walks an account's movements day by day from its opening balance and returns the first end-of-day balance below zero with its date (rule 74; a receipt still pending review is evidence, not money; rows before the opening date never count). `Transaction.JarAllocations` navigation added (the stored jar split of each account leg).

**Application.** `Accounts/AccountBalanceGuard` composes the candidate state of every affected account (stored rows, plus the caller's unsaved additions, edits and deletions) and refuses a write whose result would dip below zero on any date, stating the account, the balance and the date. It runs in `CreateTransaction` (expense), `UpdateTransaction` (both the previous and the new account, so shrinking or moving an income that later payments relied on is refused), `DeleteTransaction` (the income or receipt being removed), `CreateTransfer` and `ConfirmTransferLegs` (the departure), `AllocateIncome` (the source, with superseded in-transit transfers no longer counted) and `UpdateAccount` (a smaller opening balance or later opening date). Each of those takes the per-account ledger lock (`BeginLedgerWriteAsync`) on the account that can lose money, so two racing payments validate against committed money. `CommitImport` and the direct `ImportStatement` path evaluate rows in date order against the account's stored money plus the rows accepted so far: a debit that would dip the balance is returned in `ImportStatementResult.Held` (reference + reason) and not posted, later rows are judged without it, credits and pending-review receipts are never held; the import commit keeps its existing collision-retry save and takes no ledger lock (a concurrent payment during a commit is the one remaining race, recorded in HANDOVER). `Jars/JarMovements` writes the stored split of one account leg (`JarAllocationKind.Movement`): `ApplyIncomingAsync` turns the prompt's answer (`JarAllocationInput` rows: jar, amount) into postings plus the unallocated remainder, refusing amounts over the movement, jars of another account and archived jars; `ApplyOutgoingAsync` attributes jar-line portions (whole line or slices) to their jars and the rest to `PaidFromJarId` or unallocated, refusing a jar for a payment already on a jar's line; a leg whose answer equals the derived default stores nothing, and an existing row is updated in place (the `(TransactionId, AccountId)` index is unique). Commands gained the optional fields `JarAllocations`/`PaidFromJarId` (create, update, assign, split, import item), `FromJarId`/`JarAllocations` (create transfer, confirm legs — a receipt's answer applies once the receipt is posted) and per-member `JarAllocations` (allocation commit); an update re-answers the prompts, so an edit that omits them returns the leg to its default. `TransactionDto.JarAllocations` carries the stored postings with jar names on every transaction endpoint (`TransactionReads.ReadDtoAsync` is the shared re-read). Validators reject the wrong direction (jar amounts on an expense, a "paid from" jar on income or without a line, a receipt split without a receipt date).

**Client.** Shared `useJarLedgers` hook (GET `/jars/ledger`: which accounts back jars), `JarAllocationFields` (rule 78: one amount per jar, remainder shown as "Stays unallocated", may be left empty) and `PaidFromJarSelect` (rule 79: "Unallocated money" default plus the account's jars). TransactionsPage: the add and edit forms show the income fields and the paid-from select for jar-backed accounts, the inline assignment to a non-jar line opens the "Which jar does this come from?" prompt, the split editor asks once for the non-jar slices, the transfer form offers "From jar" and (with an arrival date) the destination fields, "Confirm receipt" asks the destination question, and register rows show the stored answer ("→ Holiday 300.00, Car 200.00", "from Holiday"); rule 74 refusals and jar validation messages are shown verbatim. ImportPage: per-row prompts for credits and non-jar-line debits into a jar-backed statement account; held rows keep the review open with the reason and a "Commit held rows" action, unticking discards. MoveMoneyModal: a destination in another account turns the move into a confirmed transfer (checkbox, departure date, clearable arrival date) posted to `/transactions/transfer` with `fromJarId`/`jarAllocations`. AllocationPage: per-member jar fields for savings accounts that back jars, sent as `jarAllocations` on commit.

**Behaviour proposals recorded in HANDOVER (P6–P11)** and applied unless the user objects: a transfer out of a jar-backed account may name the jar it leaves; edits re-answer the prompts; held import rows live in the review table only; cross-account Move money requires the completed-transfer confirmation with a clearable arrival date; rule 74 applies to every date and to income edits/deletions and opening-balance edits; the import commit takes no ledger lock.

**Test maintenance** is itemised in TEST-PLAN (money movement slice review). **Validation (this machine, .NET SDK 10.0.112; no SQL Server or Docker daemon):** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx` — 0 warnings, 0 errors; `dotnet test tests/AllotJar.Application.Tests` — **1033 passed**, 0 failed, 0 skipped (978 before; 55 new cases); the integration project compiles (its new `AccountBalanceSqlTests` run in CI only). From `client`: `npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` clean; `npm run test:quiet` — **753 passed in 61 files** (717 before; 36 new); `npm run build` passed; ESLint on every file changed on the branch adds no finding (the 5 findings in `TransactionsPage.tsx` and 2 in `AllocationPage.tsx` reproduce on the `main` versions of those files); the mocked a11y and 375px-overflow suites pass locally (47 cases, run with the container's Chromium 1194 through a local config); `git diff --check` clean. **Not run locally:** the SQL integration tests and the real-backend journeys (the new `money-movement.spec.ts`, the funded `import-lifecycle.spec.ts`); CI supplies those and their results are recorded on the PR.

The first run on `cb0eeb2` passed every job but the journeys: two existing journeys opened an account at zero and spent from it (now refused by rule 74; they open "Joint" with 500.00), one asserted the generic transfer error the modal now replaces with the server's verbatim message, and the new journey selected an option by a regex label Playwright does not accept. **Released.** PR #264 was squash-merged as `d3132d28a014521f711b5df96dcde0cb1b426f59` on 27 September 2026 (CI green again on the final docs-only head `b0a6cf3`, [run 36273857262](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36273857262)). ## W06 People and allocation income (rules 28–29; REVIEW P1/P2) — 27 September 2026

**Scope.** The income fix the review defined: People and Allocation read the selected month's budgeted income and nothing else; the saved per-person default is a helper, never a pool or fallback (rule 28); the People and Allocation pages use the same month and figures as the Budget (rule 29); per-person received income and spending are base-currency, month-scoped and flag pending exchange rates (P2). The optional-feature placement (O7) and the treatment of income not assigned to people beyond the existing equal share (O4) are not decided here.

**Application.** `MemberIncome.SharesAsync` is the one derivation every figure uses: each active member's attributed income lines plus an equal share of the unattributed lines (last member absorbs rounding, the `IncomeAllocator.Split` convention); archived members 0; a month with no budget yields zero for everyone. `AllocationPlanner` takes its pool from it and the `NetMonthlyIncome` fallback is gone (`IncomeFromBudget` is always true; `UnattributedIncome` is always reported). `GetHouseholdMembersQuery(IncludeArchived, Year?, Month?)` returns each member's `BudgetIncome`, `IncomeYear`/`IncomeMonth`, `HasBudget` and an `IncomeSharePct` computed from that month; the create/update/archive member commands return the same shape for the current month (`HouseholdMemberReads`). `GetMemberIncome` converts received rows to the base currency, leaves `FxPending` rows out (`FxPendingCount`) and adds `Income`/`SharePct` per member. `GetMemberSpendingQuery(Year?, Month?)` scopes to a month when asked (`GET /members/spending/{year}/{month}`), converts by `ExchangeRate` and counts pending rows per member; the all-time route stays for other consumers. `HouseholdMember.NetMonthlyIncome` is kept as the saved default (nothing populates a new budget from it yet — recorded in HANDOVER as a question).

**Client.** People page: reads the app-wide selected month (`useMonth`), shows "Income this month" from the budget always, each person's month income and budget-based share, month-scoped spending, "+N pending rate" hints, and labels the saved figure **Default income** ("A starting figure for a new month's budget. Calculations use the income lines in the month's budget."). Allocation page: period from `useMonth` (no longer `/budget/current`), the "default incomes from the People page" banner replaced by a no-income notice, and linking a savings account no longer rewrites the person's default income.

## W06 follow-up ([PR #266](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/266)) — the Default income seeds a blank month (rule 28; HANDOVER Q1 (a), Q2 keep) — 27 September 2026

**Scope.** The two questions PR #265 left for the user, answered on 27 September 2026: Q1 (a) — the saved per-person **Default income** populates a new budget, the one purpose rule 28 gives it; Q2 — a "Whole household" income line stays shared equally among the active people (no code change; O4's unassigned-income part closed in HANDOVER). Also the release record for PR #265 above.

**Application.** `CreateBudgetMonthCommandHandler`'s blank branch (no template, and no earlier month to copy) now builds the Income group with one line per active household member whose `NetMonthlyIncome` is above zero, in People-page order (`DisplayOrder`, then name): the line is named after the person, planned at the default and attributed to them (`MemberId`), so `MemberIncome.SharesAsync` reads the month's income for People and Allocation without a manual step. A person with a default of 0 gets no line (**P12**, HANDOVER), so a solo household whose owner never set one keeps the empty Income group; archived people and other households never seed. Template months keep the template's lines (all 0.00, unattributed) and copied months keep last month's lines — neither path changes. No schema change.

**Client.** People page: the Default income hint now reads "Becomes this person's income line when you start a blank month. Calculations use the income lines in the month's budget." No other UI change: the "Start a blank budget" button and the create-month card are as before. USER_GUIDE (Creating a month, Whose income is whose) and the test catalogs (BUDG-001 precondition, new BUDG-057, PPL-079 hint) are updated.

## W11 Pocket-money identity and archive ([PR #268](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/268); rules 53–54; REVIEW A6) — 27 September 2026

**Scope.** The pocket-money line of a person is identified by the person, not by their display name (rule 53), and archiving a person stops their pocket money from the next month on, asking what to do with the current month and leaving every payment in place (rule 54). No schema change: `BudgetItem.MemberId` already existed for income lines ("who earns") and is now also the pocket-money link on an Expense line, written only by the sync.

**Application.** `SyncPocketMoneyLinesCommandHandler` finds each active member's line by `MemberId` first and only then by name among the unlinked lines (adopting and linking it — P16); new lines carry the link; a rename therefore reuses the existing line instead of leaving the old amount and adding a duplicate. `UpdateHouseholdMemberCommandHandler` renames the linked lines whose name is the person's old name in every month (P15). New `Household/PocketMoneyLines`: `ReadFromAsync` lists a person's linked Expense lines from a month onward with planned and paid (`BudgetActuals` on a detached graph, base currency, pending-rate rows left out) and `CancelAsync` removes a line nothing was recorded against or drops its planned amount to what was paid (P13). `ArchiveHouseholdMemberCommand(Id, Archived, PocketMoneyThisMonth?)`: when archiving an active person the handler reads their lines from the current UTC month (P14); if the current month still has a remainder and no `PocketMoneyDecision` (Keep/Cancel) was sent it refuses with a 400 on `PocketMoneyThisMonth` ("{Name} still has {amount} of pocket money planned for {Month Year}. Choose whether to keep or cancel it before archiving."); otherwise it cancels every later month and, on Cancel, the current one, then archives. Owner scoping is checked before any line is read. Restoring recreates nothing (P17). New `GetMemberArchivePreviewQuery` (`GET /members/{id}/archive-preview` → `MemberArchivePreviewDto`: `ThisMonth` planned/paid/remaining or null, `FutureMonths`) lets the People page ask before it archives. `CreateBudgetMonthCommandHandler`'s copy path drops an archived person's pocket-money lines next to the archived jars' lines. Income lines and other people's lines are never touched.

**Client.** People page: the ✕ first reads the archive preview; when this month's remaining pocket money is above zero it opens **"Archive {name}"** — "{name} still has €X of pocket money planned for {Month Year} (€Y already paid). Keep it for this month, or cancel what remains? Either way {name}'s payments stay recorded, and the pocket money already planned for {later month(s)} is cancelled." — with **Keep this month** (decision 0), **Cancel remaining** (decision 1) and **Don't archive**; otherwise it archives on the one click with the unchanged `{ archived: true }` body. A server 400 is shown verbatim (`serverErrors`). `types.ts` gains `MemberArchivePreviewDto`, `PocketMoneyMonthDto` and `PocketMoneyDecision`. USER_GUIDE (People & sharing: renaming and archiving; Income allocation: pocket money) and the test catalogs (PPL-022/023/024 rewritten, PPL-084–087, ALLOC-068 updated and ALLOC-075, BUDG-058) are updated.

**Behaviour proposals recorded in HANDOVER (P13–P17)** and applied unless the user objects: cancel drops the plan to what was paid and removes an untouched line; the current month is the UTC month; a rename follows only lines named after the person; unlinked legacy lines are adopted by name once; restoring recreates nothing and a copied month drops an archived person's line.

## W12 Reports and refunds ([PR #270](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/270); rules 37–39, 41; REVIEW concerns 5/6) — 27 September 2026

**Scope.** The report fixes the review defined: uncategorised spending is included in every report as its own row so the month's total is complete and does not move when a transaction is categorised later (rule 37); a refund is linked to the purchase it refunds, reduces that category's net spending, is kept as its own row and is never income (rule 38); every figure uses the transaction's actual date, so a refund reduces the month it was received and is labelled (rule 39); the annual table shows planned and actual income with a signed **Variance** (rule 41). O9's edges (a refund into a different account, unmatched refunds, completed jar cycles) were the agreed stop and stay open in HANDOVER.

**Schema.** Migration `20260927150929_TransactionRefunds`: one nullable `RefundOfTransactionId` column on `Transactions`, indexed, with a self-referencing foreign key (`Restrict`, so the database also refuses deleting a refunded purchase). Additive; runs on the deploy; no local reset needed.

**Application.** A refund is an Income-typed `Transaction` with `RefundOfTransactionId` set (`IsRefund`), so it credits its account like any receipt. `TransactionInvariants.LineDirection` maps a refund to the Expense direction, and every assignment path (create, assign, link, update, splits) uses it: a refund sits only on expense/savings lines. `BudgetActuals` groups assigned rows by `(line, type, isRefund)`: income lines read income rows that are not refunds; expense/savings lines read `spent + slices − refunded`, with the refunded figure kept on the new transient `BudgetItem.RefundedAmount` (→ `BudgetItemDto.RefundedAmount`). New `Transactions/RefundRules`: `ValidateOriginalAsync` (owned, an Expense, not a transfer, not itself a refund, not dated after the refund, and the purchase's refunds never total more than was paid — same currency compares face amounts, otherwise base amounts) and `DefaultLineAsync` (P19). `CreateTransactionCommand` gains `RefundOfTransactionId` (validator: Type must be Income and no splits); the handler validates the line in the refund's direction, defaults it through `RefundRules`, skips the auto-categorizer and returns the re-read DTO. New `LinkRefundCommand` (`PUT /transactions/{id}/refund` `{ refundOfTransactionId, budgetItemId?, acknowledgeCrossMonth }`) marks money received as a refund — moving it off any income line onto the given expense/savings line or the default — or unlinks it back into plain income (clearing its expense line). `Update` keeps the pair coherent (types locked, amount ≥ refunded, purchase date ≤ first refund) and re-validates a moved refund; `Delete` refuses a purchase with refunds; `Split` refuses a refund; `GetMemberIncome` leaves refunds out and `GetMemberSpending` counts them negative. `TransactionDto` carries `RefundOfTransactionId/Payee/Date/Amount`, `RefundCount` and `RefundedAmount`. Reports: new `Reports/ReportFigures.ForMonthAsync` is the one derivation for a calendar month — the budget's non-income groups (gross, refunds, net, display index) after `BudgetActuals`, plus the month's transaction-dated layer of rows and slices on no line (uncategorised spending, uncategorised income, unplaced refunds; pending-FX rows left out and counted); `IncomeReceived` = income-line actuals + uncategorised income, `Variance` = received − budgeted. New `GetMonthReportQuery` (`GET /reports/month/{year}/{month}` → `MonthReportDto`, the breakdown's source; 400 for an impossible month); `GetBudgetTrends` and `GetAnnualSummary` map from the same figures (`BudgetTrendPointDto`/`AnnualMonthDto` gain received, variance, uncategorised, refunds and counts; annual categories accumulate by `(Name, Kind)` including an `Uncategorised` row; a month with no budget carries its transaction-derived figures).

**Client.** Transactions page: the add form's Type offers **Refund** with a **Refund of** picker (the user's purchases, newest first; not refunds, not split rows) and a Category that defaults to *Same category as the purchase* and offers only expense/savings lines (no split toggle); the POST is `type: Income` + `refundOfTransactionId`. Register rows: a teal **Refund** badge with "Refund of {payee} · {date}", an "€X refunded" tag on the purchase, the ↩ control to **Mark as refund** (inline purchase picker, `Save refund link`) or **Not a refund**, the type select locked on a refund and on a refunded purchase, the split control hidden on a refund, expense-only inline assignment; the hero's Money in excludes refunds and Money out nets them. Reports page: the breakdown reads `/reports/month/{y}/{m}` (page currency from its `baseCurrency`), lists an amber **Uncategorised** row with a *Review N transactions* link to the register, shows each group net of refunds with "after €X refunded", keeps a negative-net group last without a share; the annual table reads **Month | Budgeted | Received | Variance | Spent | Net** with a signed, coloured Variance (plain at zero), Net = received − spent, and a no-budget month showing what was received and spent with dashes for Budgeted and Variance; the yearly-averages card marks Uncategorised with a **Review** badge and adds "after €X refunded". `types.ts` gains `MonthReportDto`/`MonthReportCategoryDto` and the new fields. USER_GUIDE (Transactions: Refunds; Reports rewritten to the current page) and the test catalogs (RPT-015 and RPT-023 rewritten, RPT-038–041, TX-099–102, PPL-006 note) are updated.

**Behaviour proposals recorded in HANDOVER (P18–P23)** and applied unless the user objects: a refund is Income-typed money received linked to its purchase and left out of every income figure; its default line continues the purchase's line in the refund's month; the pair stays coherent (partial refunds up to the purchase, dates ordered, types locked, no split, no deleting a refunded purchase); reports add a transaction-dated layer to the line-derived figures; the annual table reads Budgeted · Received · Variance · Spent · Net; a negative-net group stays on the breakdown, labelled, without a share.

**Test maintenance** is itemised in TEST-PLAN (W12 reports and refunds review): one backend test and two client cases whose premise the rules removed were rewritten, not duplicated; the breakdown's unused `BudgetMonthDto` fixture builders were deleted; nothing else was removed. **Validation (this machine, .NET SDK 10.0.112; no SQL Server or Docker daemon):** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx` — 0 warnings, 0 errors; `dotnet test AllotJar.slnx` (unit project) — **1081 passed**, 0 failed, 0 skipped (1057 before; 24 new: 16 `RefundTests`, 8 `ReportFiguresTests`; 1 rewritten); the integration project compiles. From `client`: `npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` clean; `npm run test:quiet` — **771 passed in 61 files** (763 before; 8 new, 2 rewritten); `npm run build` passed; ESLint on the six changed client files adds no finding (the 1 `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` error in `ReportsPage.tsx` and the 5 errors in `TransactionsPage.tsx` reproduce on `main`); `git diff --check` clean. **Not run locally:** the SQL integration tests (the only place the `Restrict` foreign key runs) and the real-backend journeys (none records a refund); CI supplies those and their results are recorded on the PR.

The first run ([36331684063](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36331684063)) failed only the a11y job's UX-017 h1 check on `/reports` — the mocked API answered the new month-report endpoint with the catch-all `[]` — and was fixed by the e2e-mock commit (TEST-PLAN, W12 review). **Released.** PR #270 was squash-merged as `30b503f20198932c9c7baf3b3aeed4b9ff7038f2` on 27 September 2026 at 16:21 UTC (CI on `main` [run 36332947944](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36332947944) green at 16:28 UTC). ## W02 Schedules and previews ([PR #271](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/271); rules 12–18, 35–36, 46, 58–59, 62, 82, 86; REVIEW J3/J4, concerns 1/4/8) — 27 September 2026

**Scope.** A jar's contributions are planned by one engine, on the server, and every surface reads it: a jar saves **by a deadline** — the remaining amount split exactly over the contribution months up to the month before the deadline, the rounding remainder in the last (rules 12, 13, 35), the deadline month only by explicit opt-in when the deadline is not the 1st (rule 36), a recurring deadline clamped to a shorter month without losing its intended day (rule 62) — or at a **fixed monthly amount**, ongoing without a target (rule 16) or capped in the final month with an estimate that is worded as an estimate when there is one (rules 18, 58). A jar with a target needs one or the other; the twelve-month default is gone (rule 17). The Jars page and the dashboard show this month's plan, what has gone in since the month began and what is still to go as three figures (rule 14). The New/Edit form previews the server's schedule and flags the months the contribution would push over their income (rule 59). Changing a schedule input replants the current and later months' lines (rule 46, W04). The answers to the six pre-W02 questions are rules 82–86; StraightLine, DailyRate and ProportionalPool are retired (O6). W03's persistent **Deadline missed** state is not part of this slice: a recurring jar whose date passes still rolls silently to its next cycle.

**Schema.** Migration `20260927170434_JarSchedules`: `UPDATE [Jars] SET [Accrual] = 1 WHERE [Accrual] <> 1` (every existing jar saves by its deadline; there is no equivalent for the retired modes), drop `CoverStart`/`CoverEnd`, add `IncludeDeadlineMonth` (bit, default 0) and `MonthlyContribution` (decimal(18,4), null). Runs on the deploy; no local reset needed.

**Domain.** `AccrualMethod` is `TargetByDate = 1 | FixedMonthly = 2`. `Jar` gains `MonthlyContribution` and `IncludeDeadlineMonth` and loses the cover window. New `Services/JarSchedule` replaces `JarAccrualCalculator`: `HasSchedule`, `IsOngoing`, `EffectiveDeadline` (the old effective-date roll, rule 62 clamp), `FundingCompletionMonth` (rule 36), `MonthsBetween`, and `Plan(jar, asOf, balance)` → `JarPlan` (the listed `Months` with their `Planned` amounts, `Remaining`, `Deadline`, `FundingCompletesOn`, `EstimatedMonths`, `IsOngoing`; `First`/`Total`). Deadline plans: count = months from `asOf`'s month to the completion month inclusive, floored at one (a deadline this month or passed plans the whole remainder now); each month = floor(remaining ÷ count, cent), last = remaining − the rest. Fixed plans: the fixed amount each month, the last capped at what remains, at most 120 listed months (the estimate covers the whole span); with no target a single ongoing entry. No plan → `JarPlan.Empty`.

**Application.** New `Jars/JarPlanner`: `PlanMonthAsync` seeds a month for many jars (0 for an unscheduled jar; a month at or before the current one plans from the ledger balance the day before it starts; a later month projects forward from the current month's opening balance through the saved planned lines of the months in between, or the schedule's own figures where a month has no budget); `ScheduleAsync` is the preview from the current month; `ReplantAsync` plants a new jar's line or rewrites the planned amount of the current and later existing months (gap months advance the balance by the schedule) and appends the line where missing (`SavingsLines.AppendJarLine`). New `Jars/JarFigures` reads the DTOs from two ledger snapshots (as of the date and as of the month start) plus the current month's saved lines. `JarDto` now carries `Accrual`, `MonthlyContribution`, `IncludeDeadlineMonth`, `PlannedThisMonth`/`ContributedThisMonth`/`RemainingThisMonth`, `Deadline`, `FundingCompletesOn`, `EstimatedMonthsToTarget` and `Status` ∈ Unavailable | Overspent | Ongoing | FullyFunded | Unscheduled | Behind | OnTrack (`RequiredMonthlyContribution`, `ProjectedFullyFundedDate` and `Unfunded` are gone). New `Jars/JarDefinition` (`IJarDefinition` + the shared FluentValidation rules: a recurring jar is by deadline with a target and a date; a by-deadline goal needs a target and a date; a fixed plan needs a monthly amount above zero and no date; amounts in range; cycle 1–120) used by `CreateJarCommandValidator`, `UpdateJarCommandValidator` and the new `GetJarScheduleQueryValidator`. `CreateJarCommand`/`UpdateJarCommand` (and the API requests) gain `MonthlyContribution` and `IncludeDeadlineMonth`; the create handler plants through `JarPlanner.ReplantAsync` with the initial allocation; the update handler compares the schedule inputs before and after and replants the current and later months when they differ (rule 46), never on a rename or an account change. New `GetJarScheduleQuery` (`POST /jars/schedule` → `JarScheduleDto`: `Accrual`, `IsOngoing`, `HasSchedule`, `Balance`, `Remaining`, `Months` [`Year`, `Month`, `Planned`, `HasBudget`, `Shortfall`], `Total`, `Deadline`, `FundingCompletesOn`, `EstimatedMonths`) plans a new definition from what is set aside, or an existing jar (ownership checked) from its balance, and computes each existing month's shortfall (rule 59). `CreateBudgetMonthCommandHandler` seeds only jars with a schedule, through `PlanMonthAsync`; an unscheduled jar's copied line is carried flat.

**Client.** `types.ts`: `AccrualMethod { TargetByDate, FixedMonthly }`, the new `JarDto` shape, `JarScheduleDto`/`JarScheduleMonthDto`/`JarScheduleRequest`. `JarFormModal` rewritten: Type (Recurring | Goal), for a Goal **Save** = *By a deadline* | *A fixed amount each month*; Target (optional for the fixed method), Next due + Repeats | Deadline | Each month, Held in, **Include the deadline month** (only when the deadline is after the 1st, with the explanation rule 36 asks for), Already set aside + Set aside on (create only). The preview asks `POST /jars/schedule` 250 ms after typing stops whenever the definition can be planned (never while the edited jar's balance is unavailable) and renders the server's answer: "Sets aside €100.00 a month from Sep 2026 to Nov 2026, funded by 30 Nov 2026 for the deadline on 1 Dec 2026, then it repeats every year.", "… €100.00 in total …" with the month list when the months differ, "Sets aside €50.00 every month, ongoing.", "Sets aside €50.00 a month — about 4 more months to reach the target (an estimate, not a deadline).", "Already funded — nothing more to set aside.", and a rose **Over budget: Oct 2026 by €20.00** status for rule 59; twelve months are listed, the rest counted. Client-side validation mirrors the server's (a recurring jar needs its target and due date; a goal needs a deadline or a monthly amount). `lib/jarStatus.ts`: `jarStatusChip` (Behind, Overspent, **Needs a plan**, Funded, Unavailable), `jarMonthLabel` ("Plan €125.00 · in €50.00 · to go €75.00", "Fully funded · €100.00 above target", "Add a deadline or a monthly amount to plan contributions", "Balance not available", "Nothing to set aside this month"), `jarHorizonLabel` ("Funded by Nov 2026 · due 1 Dec 2026, then it repeats", "About 6 more months at this rate (an estimate, not a deadline)", "Ongoing", "Due …"). The Jars hero's tile is **This month** (Σ planned) and the dashboard's jar shelf reads `plannedThisMonth`. The client's own recurrence/date mirror in `lib/jarForm.ts` is gone (only the account filter and two date helpers remain). USER_GUIDE (Savings groups; Jars rewritten) and the catalogs (13-jars: header, JAR-003/005/006/007/013/014/016/017/027/034/035/036/041/042/054/055 rewritten, JAR-083–087 added; BUDG-016; TIME-018/019) are updated.

**Behaviour proposals recorded in HANDOVER (P24–P31)** and applied unless the user objects: exact cents with the remainder last and the whole remainder when due now or passed; the deadline day is never a contribution day and the deadline month is an opt-in only after the 1st; this month's figures from the month's start; later months projected through the saved plans; the preview is the planner; a schedule edit replants the current and later months (manual overrides unprotected, O3); no plan means no contribution, not ÷12; retired modes migrate to the deadline method.

**Left in place.** `tools/AllotJar.Importer` (not in `AllotJar.slnx`, not built by CI, already referencing removed members) is the only code that wrote the retired modes; deleting the folder needs the owner's say-so, so it stays for now.

**Test maintenance** is itemised in TEST-PLAN (W02 schedules and previews review): the calculator's tests and the client's date-mirror preview cases went with the code they tested; the duplicated `EffectiveDate_*` cases were folded into `JarScheduleTests`; assertions that encoded the twelve-month default or the ÷months rounding were rewritten under rules 17 and 35; the six real-backend journeys that created Goal jars with no deadline (`helpers.ts` `createGoalJar`, `budget-lifecycle.spec.ts`, `allocation-commit.spec.ts`, and through the helper `jars-ledger.spec.ts` and `money-movement.spec.ts`) now give a goal a deadline twelve months out — or a fixed monthly amount, for the target-less pot — and the copy journey checks that the next month is re-planned through the current month's saved line rather than carried flat (P27). **Validation (this machine, .NET SDK 10.0.112; no SQL Server or Docker daemon):** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx` — 0 warnings, 0 errors; `dotnet test` (unit project) — **1084 passed**, 0 failed, 0 skipped (1081 before: 31 cases removed, 34 new, the rest rewritten in place); the integration project compiles. ## W03 Recurring lifecycle (rules 19–24, 57, 83; REVIEW concern 3) — 27 September 2026

**Scope.** A Recurring jar now has a persisted **open cycle** and a history of closed ones, and a missed deadline is a state the user must act on, never a silent roll. When a cycle's deadline arrives with the jar holding its target (measured at the end of the day before, rule 82), the cycle closes as **Funded** and the jar advances to the next deadline, its balance untouched (rules 19, 21). When it is short, the jar enters **Deadline missed** with the shortfall in view, plans the whole shortfall now and stays there (rule 20); reaching the target afterwards reads **Funded late** and the notice stays (rule 57). The user ends a cycle by **marking it paid** and choosing the payment that settled the bill — money out from the jar's account, or any expense on request; the app never infers which payment belongs to which cycle (rule 83) — or by **dismissing** the missed notice, which closes the cycle as missed (or funded late) and saves toward the next deadline without recording a payment or manufacturing money (rule 20). A payment before the deadline that empties the jar counts as met on time once marked paid (rule 22); only the actual remainder carries into the next cycle (rule 23); the target never changes on its own (rule 24). A cycle that rolled forward funded but whose payment was not recorded can still be given one from the card ("Paid it? Record the payment"). Money never moves on any of this. Goals are untouched.

**Schema.** Migration `20260927203644_JarCycles`: adds `Jars.CycleDeadline` (date, null); for every legacy Recurring jar sets it to the cycle the old silent roll showed — the anchor, stepped by whole cycles while in the past, then one more step while still in the past (the retired `EffectiveDeadline` closed form, in T-SQL) — so no jar reads Deadline missed for a cycle it never showed; creates `JarCycles` (`Id`, `OwnerId`, `JarId` → `Jars` cascade, `Deadline`, `TargetAmount`, `Outcome`, `BalanceAtDeadline`, `PaymentTransactionId` → `Transactions` set-null, `PaidOn`, `PaidAmount`, `ClosedOn`, `CreatedUtc`; unique `(JarId, Deadline)`; unique filtered `PaymentTransactionId` so a payment settles one cycle; index `OwnerId`). Runs on the deploy; no local reset needed.

**Domain.** New `Entities/JarCycle` and `Enums/JarCycleOutcome` (`Paid | Funded | Missed | FundedLate`; `PaidOnTime` = paid and the payment dated before the deadline). `Jar.CycleDeadline` is the open cycle (Recurring only). `Services/JarSchedule`: `EffectiveDeadline` is gone; `CurrentDeadline(jar)` is the open cycle for a Recurring jar (falling back to the anchor) and the deadline for a Goal; `NextCycleDeadline(jar, after)` steps from the stored anchor, so a 31st clamps only in shorter months and never drifts (rule 62); `PlanByDeadline` plans toward the open cycle, and a passed cycle plans the whole remainder now (the missed state is visible in the plan, rule 20).

**Application.** New `Jars/JarCycles`: `SettleAsync` (rule 21, lazily on every read and plan path — `GetJars`, `GetJarSchedule`, `UpdateJar`, `CreateBudgetMonth`: each open Recurring cycle whose deadline has passed and whose balance the day before was at or above target closes as Funded and the jar advances, replanting this month and later ones; a short cycle stays open and unsettled; several passed cycles settle in order), `CloseOpenCycleAsync` (records the balance at the deadline when it has passed, links the payment, advances, replants), `Link`, `DeadlinesFor` (a form that echoes the open cycle's date, or the anchor, keeps both — a rename never resets an advanced cycle; any other date is a new anchor and a new open cycle; a Goal has none) and `BalanceBeforeAsync`. New commands `MarkJarCyclePaid` (`POST /jars/{id}/cycles/paid` — the cycle's deadline and the payment; refuses a Goal, income/transfers/refunds, a future-dated payment, a payment already fulfilling a cycle, an unknown cycle and a closed cycle already paid; the open cycle closes as Paid and advances, a closed Funded cycle is simply linked) and `DismissJarCycle` (`POST /jars/{id}/cycles/dismiss` — the open cycle's deadline, so a stale page cannot dismiss the wrong one; refuses a cycle not yet passed; closes as FundedLate when the balance today is at target, else Missed; advances and replants). New query `GetJarPayments` (`GET /jars/{id}/payments[?all=true]` → `JarPaymentDto`: expenses not yet linked to a cycle, newest first — those drawn from the jar (rule 79's `SavingsId` line or a stored allocation), or every expense with `all`; at most 100). `JarDto` gains `Shortfall`, `LastCycle` (`JarCycleDto`) and the statuses `DeadlineMissed` (before Overspent; a passed open cycle with money still to save) and `FundedLate` (a passed open cycle held in full); `JarFigures` loads each jar's latest closed cycle. **Planning balance (amends P26/P27):** this month's plan and the preview now start from `min(balance entering the month, balance now)` — money leaving the jar this month (a bill paid from it) counts at once, while a contribution never shrinks the month's plan (rule 14); later months project from the month's expected end (`JarPlanner.PlanningBalance`, `ProjectedMonthEnd`, `BalancesAsync`).

**Client.** `types.ts`: `JarDto.shortfall`/`lastCycle`, `JarCycleDto`, `JarPaymentDto`. `lib/jarStatus.ts`: chips **Deadline missed** (rose) and **Funded late** (amber); month line "Deadline 1 Sep 2026 missed · €150.00 short" / "Funded after the deadline on 1 Sep 2026"; horizon "Mark the cycle paid, or dismiss it to save toward the next one"; new `jarLastCycleLabel` ("Last cycle · due 1 Sep 2026 · paid 3 Sep 2026 (€580.00), after the deadline" / "· funded on time, payment not recorded" / "· funded late" / "· missed"). `JarsPage`: every Recurring jar card with a balance offers **Mark paid**; a missed or late one also **Dismiss**; a last cycle that rolled forward funded without a payment shows "Paid it? Record the payment". New `components/jars/MarkCyclePaidModal` (the jar's own payments as radios with "From this jar", **Show payments from other accounts** re-asks with `all=true`, the empty state points at the Transactions page, Confirm disabled until a payment is chosen, server messages shown verbatim) and `DismissCycleModal` (says what dismissing does and does not do, with a link into Mark paid for a bill that was in fact paid). `JarFormModal` shows a Recurring jar's open cycle's date as **Next due** (the server keeps the cycle when that date is echoed). `e2e/mocks.ts` answers `GET /jars/{id}/payments` and carries a Deadline-missed jar. USER_GUIDE §6 (recurring cycles) and the catalog (13-jars: header, JAR-037/056 rewritten, JAR-088–093 added) are updated.

**Behaviour proposals recorded in HANDOVER (P32–P39)** and applied unless the user objects: the open cycle is persisted and settled lazily; four outcomes and a balance snapshot per closed cycle; the jar's own payments first, every expense on request, a payment settles one cycle; a passed open cycle plans the whole shortfall now; dismissing records Missed or Funded late by the balance today; the planning balance is `min(entering the month, now)`; a form that echoes the open cycle's date keeps the cycle; legacy jars start on the cycle they were showing.

**Test maintenance** is itemised in TEST-PLAN (W03 recurring lifecycle review): the three `JarScheduleTests` cases that pinned the silent roll (`ARecurringJar_PlansTowardItsNextCycle_OnceTheDateHasPassed`, `Rule62_AShorterMonth_ClampsOnlyThatOccurrence_AndKeepsTheIntendedDay`, `EffectiveDeadline_DueToday_DoesNotRoll_ButALaterDayThisMonth_Does`) went with `EffectiveDeadline` and are replaced by four cases against `CurrentDeadline`/`NextCycleDeadline`; `JarLifecycleTests.Status_PastDueRecurring_ReadsOnTrack_NotBehind` and the month-create seeding case were rewritten for rules 20/57; `JarCycleTests` (17) and one `JarPlannerTests` case are new; the client fixtures carry the two new fields, JAR-037 asserts the cycle-date echo, `jarStatus.test.ts` and the new `JarsPage.cycles.test.tsx` (9) cover the states, the two modals and the gating. **Validation (this machine, .NET SDK 10.0.112; no SQL Server or Docker daemon):** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx` — 0 warnings, 0 errors; `dotnet test` (unit project) — **1102 passed**, 0 failed, 0 skipped (1084 before: 3 removed, 21 new); the integration project compiles. From `client`: `npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` clean; `npm run test:quiet` — **780 passed in 62 files** (766 before: 14 new); `npm run build` passed; ESLint on the changed client files adds no finding; `npx playwright test e2e/a11y.spec.ts e2e/ux-states.spec.ts` against the mocked API — 51 passed; `git diff --check` clean. **Not run locally:** the SQL integration tests (where the `JarCycles` migration's `UPDATE`s run against real rows) and the real-backend journeys; CI supplies those and their results are recorded on the PR.
