## W04 Edits, deletion, archive, completion, skip (rules 25–27, 46–47, 55–56, 60–61, 84; REVIEW J5, concern 7) — 27 September 2026

**Scope.** What happens to a jar's planned lines when the jar stops, and how a month's contribution is skipped. **Archiving** (rule 25; still only at a zero balance, rule 26) now cancels the jar's plan from this month on: the current month's line is cut to what has actually gone into the jar since the month began and every later month's plan line is removed (kept at 0 where a transaction sits on it, so history keeps its place); restoring replants the current and later months. A Goal is **marked completed** (rule 55): its plan stops the same way, the money stays in the jar until the user moves or spends it (rule 56), and the jar stays listed with a **Completed** chip and "€X left to move"; a recurring jar is never completed (its cycle is marked paid), an overdrawn or unreadable balance is refused, and a completed goal cannot become recurring. A jar that has **never held money** — no allocation posting ever, no transaction ever assigned to one of its lines — can be **deleted** with every planned line it had in any month and its cycle rows (rules 27, 84); any other jar is refused with a pointer to Archive, and the Edit form offers Delete only when the server says it applies. A jar's monthly line **cannot be deleted from a month** (rule 60): `DELETE /budget/items/{id}` refuses it, and the Budget page's ✕ opens a dialog offering **Skip this month** — the line stays in the month at 0, flagged, and the rest of the plan spreads over the months that are left, split exactly (rule 61: €300 by December with September skipped → €150 October, €150 November), with the server's preview shown before the choice — or **Archive the jar**. The last eligible month may be skipped; the dialog then says what stays unplanned. **Plan it again** (or typing an amount on the line) puts the contribution back. Past months cannot be skipped. Rule 46 had shipped with W02 (P29) and rule 47's excess shows on the card. Nothing here moves money or touches transactions.

**Schema.** Migration `20260927224100_JarCompletionAndSkips`: adds `Jars.CompletedOn` (date, null) and `BudgetItems.IsSkipped` (bit, not null, default 0). No data changes; runs on the deploy.

**Domain.** `Jar.CompletedOn`/`IsCompleted`; `BudgetItem.IsSkipped`. `Services/JarSchedule`: `IsPlanning(jar)` (neither archived nor completed — the one predicate month creation, replanting and the DTO use); `Plan(jar, asOf, balance, JarPlanOptions?)` — a stopped jar plans nothing but still reports `Remaining`; new `JarPlanOptions(SkippedMonths, BalanceNow, ListThrough)`: deadline plans split the remainder in whole cents over the eligible (non-skipped) months, remainder last (rule 35 holds around a skip), a skipped month is listed at 0 with `Skipped`, and `JarPlan.Unplanned` reports the part no month carries when every eligible month is skipped (rule 36: never a month after the deadline); fixed-monthly plans leave a skipped month at 0 and carry on (the estimate counts contributions); `ListThrough` lets an ongoing plan list every existing month so each can take its figure. P37's `BalanceNow` moved into the options.

**Application.** `Jars/SavingsLines`: `SkippedMonthsAsync` (the flagged months from a date on) and `CancelPlansAsync` (P40: current line := max(0, now − planning balance), later lines removed unless a transaction or split references them, then 0). New `Jars/JarUsage`: `EverHeldMoneyAsync`/`HasHeldMoneyAsync` (postings or transactions on lines — rule 84's eligibility, read from the ledger). `JarPlanner.ScheduleAsync(…, alsoSkip)` and `ReplantAsync` now derive the skipped months (from the stored lines, or the loaded ones mid-change) and pass `BalanceNow`/`ListThrough`, so the preview, the skip preview and every replant are one derivation; the old gap-filling `Advance` logic went. New commands `CompleteJar` (`POST /jars/{id}/complete` → `JarDto`; refuses non-Goal, completed, archived, unavailable or negative balance; sets `CompletedOn` = today and cancels the plans), `DeleteJar` (`DELETE /jars/{id}` → 204; refuses a jar that has held money; removes its lines in every month, its cycles and the jar), `SkipJarContribution` (`PUT /budget/items/{id}/skip {skipped}` → `BudgetMonthDto`; settles cycles, sets the flag and 0, replants) and query `GetJarSkipPreview` (`GET /budget/items/{id}/skip-preview` → `JarScheduleDto` with the month added to the skips); `Budgets/JarLineLoader` shares their checks (a jar's line, the jar planning, the month not over). `ArchiveJar` cancels plans on archive and replants on restore; `UpdateJar` refuses Recurring for a completed goal and replants only a planning jar; `CreateBudgetMonth` drops and skips the lines of archived **or completed** jars; `DeleteBudgetItem` refuses a jar's line; `UpdateBudgetItem` clears `IsSkipped` when an amount is typed. `JarDto` gains `CompletedOn`, `CanDelete`, `SkippedThisMonth` and the status `Completed` (after Unavailable); `JarFigures` reads the skip flag and the held-money set per jar; `JarScheduleDto` gains `Unplanned` and each month `Skipped` (`JarScheduleDtos.BuildAsync` factors the rule 59 shortfall logic out of the schedule query); `BudgetItemDto.IsSkipped`.

**Client.** `types.ts` carries the new fields. `lib/jarStatus.ts`: **Completed** chip (neutral), "Completed · €X left to move", "Skipped this month", "Completed on 27 Sep 2026 · no more contributions planned". `JarsPage`: **Mark completed** on a Goal with a known, non-negative balance; the hero's Active jars leaves completed goals out; new `components/jars/CompleteJarModal` (says what stays and what stops; server refusals verbatim). `JarFormModal`: **Delete jar** (two clicks, like Archive) when `canDelete`; the schedule preview lists skipped months as "Skipped" and says what stays unplanned. `BudgetItemRow`/`SavingsGroup`/`DashboardPage`: a jar line's ✕ ("Remove X this month") opens the new `components/jars/RemoveJarLineModal` (loads the skip preview, **Skip this month**, **Archive the jar** in two clicks, Cancel; the unplanned warning; rule 26 verbatim); a skipped line shows a **Skipped** tag and **Plan it again**; a past month shows neither. `budgetModel` carries `savingsId`/`isSkipped`. Analytics events `fund_completed`, `fund_deleted`, `fund_month_skipped`. `e2e/mocks.ts` answers the four new routes and carries the new fields. USER_GUIDE §4 (skipping a month) and §6 (Completed chip; "Finishing with a jar": complete, archive, delete) and the catalogs (13-jars: JAR-044/045/047/067/071 rewritten, JAR-094–099 added; 07-month-lifecycle: BUDG-017/032/034 rewritten, BUDG-059–062 added; 08-categories: CAT-042/044) are updated; the testing maps carry the new files.

**Behaviour proposals recorded in HANDOVER (P40–P46)** and applied unless the user objects: stopping a jar cancels its plan from this month on and only its plan; completing is a Goal's ending and an overdrawn or unreadable balance refuses it (O3's negative-balance completion stays open); deletion eligibility is "never held money", read from the ledger; a jar's line cannot be deleted from a month — the ✕ asks skip or archive; a skipped month is a flagged line at 0 and the flag protects it from every replant; the last eligible month can be skipped and the preview says what stays unplanned; a past month is history.

**Test maintenance** is itemised in TEST-PLAN (W04 review): `CCategoriesLinesCoverageTests.DeleteSavingsLine_LeavesTheBackingJar` (CAT-044, the behaviour rule 60 forbids) is rewritten as `DeleteSavingsLine_IsRefused_TheLineAndItsJarStay`; the month-copy case now covers a completed goal; `JarScheduleTests` (+6) pins the skip arithmetic, the balance carried past a skipped month and the stopped-jar plan; the new `JarLifecycleW04Tests` (14) covers complete, delete, archive/restore and skip through the real handlers, and month creation planning around a later skipped month; the client fixtures carry the three new fields; `jarStatus.test.ts` (+2), the new `JarsPage.lifecycle.test.tsx` (7) and `DashboardPage.skip.test.tsx` (5) cover the card action, the two dialogs, the gating and the past-month rule; the new real-backend journey `e2e-journey/jar-lifecycle.spec.ts` marks a recurring cycle paid (closing the W03 gap), skips and restores a month, completes a goal and deletes a never-used jar. **Validation (this machine, .NET SDK 10.0.112; no SQL Server or Docker daemon):** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx` — 0 warnings, 0 errors; `dotnet test` (unit project) — **1122 passed**, 0 failed, 0 skipped (1102 before: 1 rewritten, 20 new); the integration project compiles. From `client`: `npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` clean; `npm run test:quiet` — **794 passed in 64 files** (780 before: 14 new); `npm run build` passed; ESLint on the changed client files adds no finding (the five `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` errors in `BudgetItemRow.tsx`, `SavingsGroup.tsx` and `DashboardPage.tsx` reproduce on `main`); `npx playwright test e2e/a11y.spec.ts e2e/ux-states.spec.ts` against the mocked API — 51 passed; `git diff --check` clean. **Not run locally:** the SQL integration tests (the `JarCompletionAndSkips` migration) and the real-backend journeys, the new one included; CI supplies those and their results are recorded on the PR.

## Review fixes after W04 (rules 21, 39, 51, 74, 79–80) — 28 September 2026

**Scope.** Five findings from an external review of PRs #260–#273, each verified against `main` at `13be67e` before it was changed, each fixed with a regression test that fails on that head. No schema change, no client change, no new endpoint.

- **Changing an empty jar's account rewrote the old account's history (rule 80; PR #261).** `JarLedgerReader.Derive` attributes a jar-line payment to the jar only when the jar's *current* account is the one the payment left, so once the jar named another account its past payments fell to unallocated in the old account while the allocations that funded them stayed: "jar 100, unallocated −100" and a backing deficit though no money moved. `UpdateJar` now calls the new `JarMovements.KeepLineAttributionAsync` before re-designating: every payment the jar's lines drew from the old account that has no stored answer is written down there as a stored operation, exactly as the reader derived it (`JarLedgerReader.Derive`/`LineJarsAsync` are shared with the writer). The old account then reads as before; nothing is stored on the new one. *Added on 28 September from Christopher's review of the PR:* editing that payment afterwards (payee, date, amount, a re-assignment) re-answers the jar prompt through `JarMovements.ApplyOutgoingAsync`, which looked the line's jar up in its current account and so replaced the kept operation with nothing — the old account read "jar 100, unallocated −100" again. `ApplyOutgoingAsync` now treats a line whose jar has since left the account as still drawing from that jar when the leg's stored operation says it did, and writes the attribution down again (a new amount is drawn from the jar too: 80 → jar 20, unallocated 0).
- **Editing a transfer into an expense could leave its destination negative (rule 74; PR #264).** The rule 74 guard checked the old and new source accounts only; the former destination lost its receipt unchecked. The handler now loads the transfer's destination, includes it in the accounts the guard checks, and drops the receipt leg's stored jar split with the receipt.
- **A source bank reference could hide the first receipt import (rule 51; PR #260).** `StatementReferences.KnownAsync` lent every inbound transfer's source reference to the destination account, so when both banks print the same end-to-end reference the destination's first receipt row was skipped as already imported and the transfer stayed in transit. The fallback now applies only to a transfer whose receipt is recorded (`ReceivedOn` set) — the legacy rows the `AccountOpeningDatesAndTransferLegs` migration marked received on their date — never to a transfer still in transit.
- **Editing a refunded purchase compared amounts in different currencies (rule 39, O5; PR #270).** `UpdateTransaction` summed the refunds' recorded amounts and compared them with the edited amount; `RefundRules` (create/link) compared base-currency values. The comparison now lives once in `RefundRules.Totals` (same currency: amounts as recorded; otherwise each amount × its own stored rate, the converted figure O5 has the user confirm) and both paths use it: a €150 purchase with USD 60 back at 2 (€120) can shrink to €120, not €80; a €100 purchase with USD 90 back at 0.5 (€45) can shrink to €80.
- **A future-dated jar read settled cycles early (rule 21; PR #272).** `GET /api/jars?asOf=` passed the as-of date to `JarCycles.SettleAsync`, which writes closed cycles and advances the deadline. Settlement now always uses the real date; the as-of figures stay a projection. (The client never sends `asOf` to this endpoint; the fix closes the API path.)

**Not changed.** `GetJarPayments` reads its `asOf` only as a filter; the other settlement callers already use today. The review's note on PR #273's red CI was GitHub Actions refusing to start jobs, not a failure.

**Test maintenance** is itemised in TEST-PLAN (review fixes after W04): six new backend tests; one fixture updated (`ImportPreviewCommitTests`' legacy inbound transfer now carries the bank legs the migration gave every such row, since a transfer with neither leg cannot exist); nothing removed — the existing rule 80, rule 74, re-import, refund-edit and future-as-of cases all still hold and stay. **Validation (this machine, .NET SDK 10.0; no SQL Server or Docker daemon):** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx -c Release` — 0 warnings, 0 errors; `dotnet test` (unit project) — **1128 passed**, 0 failed, 0 skipped (1122 before: 6 new); the integration project compiles; each of the six new tests fails on `13be67e` with the fixes stashed. No client file changed, so the client suites were not rerun. **Not run locally:** the SQL integration tests and the real-backend journeys; CI supplies those and their results are recorded on the PR.

The push to `main` triggered CI [run 36450038581](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36450038581) and Deploy [run 36450038603](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36450038603) at 16:18:11 UTC. ## W05 Period consistency and refresh ([PR #275](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/275); rules 40, 48–49; REVIEW J6, concern 2) — 28 September 2026

**Scope.** The Budget page's **Your jars** shelf becomes the viewed month's view of the jars instead of a current-month-only snapshot of today's all-time balances (rule 48; REVIEW J6): a past month's balances are read as of its last day, its plan is that month's Savings line, "in" is what went into the jar during that month and "to go" what was still short at its end; the current month reads as before; a month that has not begun reads today's balances with nothing contributed yet and its own line as the plan. The shelf says which ("€300.00 saved as of 30 Sep 2026", "balances as of today") and agrees with the Savings lines' Available on every month, since both read one ledger at one date. It is re-read whenever the month changes and after every change on the page that moves a jar's figures (a jar line's planned amount, creating the month, skip / plan again / archive from a line, a new jar) — J6's staleness. Rule 40 (the opening balance already includes everything before its date) and rule 49 (a correction dated in a past month changes that month and carries forward) were already the dated ledger's behaviour and are pinned by the new tests; nothing about them changed. The Jars page keeps reading today (HANDOVER P48). No schema change, no migration.

**Application.** `GetJarsQuery` gains `Month` (any day in the budget month wanted; `AsOf` stays the injectable "today"). `JarFigures.ReadAsync(…, month)` derives the balance date — the month's last day when that is before today, else today — reads the ledger as of it and as of the day before the month (never after the balance date, so a month that has not begun contributes nothing), takes the month's own stored lines and skips, and settles nothing itself (`GetJars` settles cycles by today, as before). `JarMapping.ToDto` takes the month so a later month's skip flag and plan are its own. `GET /jars?year=&month=` (both or neither; 400 otherwise) passes the month through. `BudgetActuals.ApplySavingsBalancesAsync` already read the same date (rule 48) and is unchanged.

**Client.** `DashboardPage`: the shelf's fetch carries `{ year, month }` and re-runs when the viewed month changes; the current-month-only gate is gone; the header reads "… saved as of 30 Sep 2026" on a past month (the server's `balanceAsOf`) and "… saved balances as of today" on a later one; `commitItem` re-reads the shelf after a jar line's planned amount is saved (an expense line's edit does not), and `createMonth`/`createFromTemplate` re-read it after the month is created (its seeded lines are the plan); the skip / plan-again / archive / jar-created paths already did. USER_GUIDE §4 (Savings groups) says the shelf follows the month; the catalog (06-dashboard: DASH-027/028/030 rewritten, DASH-073–075 added; 13-jars: JAR-060/061 rewritten; 20-journeys CP4) and the testing maps carry the change.

**Behaviour proposals recorded in HANDOVER (P47–P50)** and applied unless the user objects: the shelf is the viewed month's view (a past month as of its end, a later month as of today with nothing contributed); the Jars page always reads today; looking ahead settles no cycle and a past month keeps the status it had; what re-reads the shelf.

**Test maintenance** is itemised in TEST-PLAN (W05 review). One journey pinned the old rule: `e2e-journey/budget-lifecycle.spec.ts` asserted that a non-current month hides the shelf, failed on the PR's first CI run, and is rewritten to assert the month-scoped shelf (the copied next month reads "balances as of today" with the pot's re-planned 250.00); the current-month-only catalog cases (DASH-028, JAR-060/061) are rewritten; `JJarsCoverageTests.JarAndGridReads_AgreeOnAllocatedMoney_AndIgnoreThePlan` still pins that the plan counts nowhere and stays. New `Application/JarMonthViewW05Tests.cs` (7) pins rules 48 and 49 on the jar read (a past month as of its end with that month's plan · in · to go; a later month as today with nothing contributed; a later month's read closes no cycle; a past month's status is what it was; a backdated correction changes the month and carries forward without touching earlier months; the grid's Available agrees with the shelf on last month, this month and next month against the real clock; rule 67 before the account opened) and new `pages/DashboardPage.jars.test.tsx` (5) covers DASH-028 and DASH-073–075. **Validation (this machine, .NET SDK 10.0.112; no SQL Server or Docker daemon):** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx` — 0 warnings, 0 errors; `dotnet test` (unit project) — **1129 passed**, 0 failed, 0 skipped (1122 before: 7 new); the integration project compiles. From `client`: `npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` clean; `npm run test:quiet` — **799 passed in 65 files** (794 before: 5 new); `npm run build` passed; ESLint on the changed client files adds no finding (the five `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` errors in `BudgetItemRow.tsx`, `SavingsGroup.tsx` and `DashboardPage.tsx` reproduce on `main`); `npx playwright test e2e/a11y.spec.ts e2e/ux-states.spec.ts` against the mocked API — 51 passed; `git diff --check` clean. **Not run locally:** the SQL integration tests and the real-backend journeys; CI supplies those and their results are recorded on the PR.

**Review fixes (28 September, same PR).** Christopher's review of head `d35a68a` found two P2 bugs in the month-scoped read, both fixed before merge. (1) *Completion changed past months' status:* the mapper tested today's `IsCompleted`, so a goal completed on 5 October read **Completed** when September was viewed. Completion is dated (rule 55): `JarSchedule.IsCompletedOn(jar, asOf)` and the dated `IsPlanning(jar, asOf)` now decide the status and the plan against the month's balance date, so September keeps the status, deadline and horizon it had then and only October reads Completed (`Rule55_AGoalCompletedThisMonth_StillReadsAsItWas_InThePastMonth`; the undated `IsPlanning(jar)` the planner and the month-create path use is unchanged). (2) *Changing accounts lost the displayed historical balance:* `JarLedgerSnapshot.BalanceOf` looked the jar up only in its current account, so a jar that held €100 in account A at August's end, was emptied and moved to B in September, read €0 for August. The old account's ledger keeps the jar's postings (rule 80), so `BalanceOf` now sums the jar's balance across every readable account in the snapshot — the old account's postings sum to zero today and to what it held there on a past date; null still means the designated account is unavailable (`Rule80_AJarMovedToAnotherAccount_KeepsTheBalanceItHeld_InThePastMonth`, which also checks the grid's Available agrees). Both tests fail on `d35a68a` and pass on the fix; unit suite **1131 passed** (1129 before). The expense-drawn side of the same history — a payment from the jar's budget line derived against the jar's current account — is [PR #274](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/274)'s `JarMovements.KeepLineAttributionAsync`, which writes it down before the jar moves; the two fixes compose, and the test here empties the jar by reallocation so it does not depend on that PR's order of merge.

**Adversarial review (28 September, same PR; Christopher's standing rule for every PR).** An independent review of head `5eeb72f` (after the #274 merge) found no P1, five P2s — each reproduced by a probe test that fails on that head — and two P3s; all seven are fixed here, with the probes folded into `JarMonthViewW05Tests` and `DashboardPage.jars.test.tsx` as regression tests. (P2-1) *A month that is over with no line for the jar invented a plan:* the mapper fell back to a freshly computed plan, so a jar created in October showed "+€120.00/mo" on September's shelf beside a grid with no such line; `JarFigures` now hands a month that is over its stored line or 0 (rule 46; `Rule46_AMonthThatIsOver_WithNoLineForTheJar_PlansNothingForIt`). (P2-2) *History read Unavailable when the jar's current account had not opened yet:* `BalanceOf` returned null before the cross-account sum whenever the designated account was not open on the date, so a jar moved to an account opened on 1 October read null for August though account A held its 100; null now means the designated account has no opening date, or no account's ledger carries the jar on that date (rule 67 keeps a month before any account held it unavailable; `Rule80_AJarMovedToAnAccountOpenedLater_StillReadsWhatTheOldAccountHeld_ForTheEarlierMonth`). (P2-3) *A past month's Recurring status was today's cycle re-judged:* after a missed September cycle was marked paid in October, September read OnTrack toward 15 September 2027; `JarFigures` now passes the mapper the cycle that was still open at the balance date (the earliest `JarCycle` closed after it, from the cycles it already loads — no new query), whose deadline and target the month is judged against, and the history line shows only the cycles closed by then (`Rule20_AMonthThatIsOver_IsJudgedAgainstTheCycleOpenThen_NotTodays`; P49 amended). (P2-4, client) *On month navigation the previous month's shelf showed, unlabelled, under the new month's grid* while the new month's `/jars` read was in flight (the month is cleared on navigation, the jars were not); `DashboardPage` now clears the jars in the same effect (`DASH-076`). (P2-5) *A month that is over omitted a jar archived since, while the grid still showed its line:* `GetJarsQueryHandler` now also lists, for a month that is over, the archived jars that have a line in it (`Rule27_AMonthThatIsOver_StillListsAJarArchivedSince_WhoseLineItCarries`); today's reads are unchanged. (P3-1) A month that has not begun read the whole ledger twice for the same date; `JarFigures` reuses the first snapshot when the two dates coincide. (P3-2) A month that has not begun was planned from today's month when it had no line; the mapper now plans it from that month (its own share of what is left, from today's balance — 1,200 over November–June is 150 a month, not a share computed from October; asserted in the rule 46 test). Every new test fails on `5eeb72f`; unit suite **1141 passed** (1137 before), client **800 passed in 65 files** (799 before). The review's clean findings (no N+1, `AsNoTracking` throughout, owner scoping, endpoint validation, React dependency arrays, idempotent settlement) stand. The re-review of head `da0024c` confirmed all seven fixed and raised one more, (P2-6, client) *the first-run checklist flashed "add a jar" on month navigation:* clearing the shelf for P2-4 left `jars` empty while the new month's read was in flight, and the checklist (rule 81) reads an empty shelf as no jar yet; `DashboardPage` now tracks whether the viewed month's shelf has been read (`jarsLoaded`, reset with the jars, set when the read settles) and the checklist waits for it (`DASH-077`, fails on `da0024c`); client **801 passed**.

**Adversarial review, round 3 (28 September, same PR).** A third independent review, of head `5dbaf02`, found eight more P2s that W05 introduced or exposed (P2-7–P2-14), three older P2s (P2-15–P2-17) and five P3s, each reproduced by a probe that fails on that head; Christopher's own review of the same head confirmed three of them (P2-7, P2-9, P2-12) and he decided the one open question: **rule 22 wins** — a cycle paid before its deadline but marked paid after its month ended reads paid on time on that month, not Deadline missed. The eight P2s and rule 22 are fixed here, with Christopher's three reproductions as regression tests; P2-15–P2-17 and the P3s follow in a separate PR. (P2-7) *Archiving rewrote a past month's status:* a Recurring jar that missed its September deadline with 200 short read **Behind** on September once archived in October, because the mapper tested today's `IsArchived`. Archiving is now dated (`Jar.ArchivedOn`, set by `ArchiveJar`, cleared on restore) and `JarSchedule.IsArchivedOn(jar, asOf)` / the dated `IsPlanning` decide a past month's status, so September stays **Deadline missed** (`Rule27_AJarArchivedAfterAMonthItMissed_StillReadsDeadlineMissedThere`); an archive recorded before this change has no date and counts on every date, as before. (Rule 22) `JarCycle.ClosedForHistoryOn` is the payment day for a cycle paid on time and its close day otherwise; the cycle open at a month's end and the history line use it (`Rule22_ABillPaidBeforeItsDeadline_ButMarkedPaidLater_ReadsPaidOnTimeOnThePastMonths`). (P2-8) *A past month was judged against today's target and deadline:* raising a target or moving a date after a month ended changed that month's status and the target it reported. Every edit that changes a jar's definition (kind, target, date, saving method, monthly amount, deadline-month option, cycle) is now dated in `JarRevisions` (`JarHistory.RecordEditAsync`: the first edit also stores the definition it replaces from the earliest date; a second edit on the same day overwrites that day's row), and a month that is over reads the definition dated on or before its last day (`JarHistory.DefinitionOn`), with a Recurring jar's deadline taken from the cycle open then; the `cycleThen` special case in the mapper is gone and `TargetAmount` is the target its status used (seven tests in `JarMonthViewW05Tests`, `JarLedgerSqlTests.A_jar_edit_is_dated_so_last_month_keeps_the_target_it_had`). (P2-9–P2-11) *One jar balance, two readings:* after a jar changed account, single-jar reads, previews and cycle records read only the designated account while `GET /jars`, month seeding and the grid read every account, so the card a save returned disagreed with the next list read (Christopher's case: emptied in A, moved to B, 50 added: the save said 50 contributed, the list 0; the month's plan and the preview differed), a residue left in the old account by correcting a pre-move payment showed on the card but not to the zero checks (archive, move, complete), and a payee-only edit of a pre-move income dropped its jar share. Every reader and zero check now reads one balance, the jar's postings in every account on the date (a restricted snapshot throws); moving a jar records a **Departure** operation (`JarAllocationKind.Departure`, zero-sum) dated the move day in the account it left, and `JarDepartures.SettleAsync` adjusts it whenever a kept pre-move entry is corrected, deleted or moved off the jar's line, so the corrected history reads on the dates before the move and the difference returns to the old account's unallocated money on the move day, never to the new account (rule 80: no transfer is inferred). An edit without a jar answer keeps a departed jar's income share (reduced only when the corrected amount can no longer hold it) and draw; a kept entry can't be re-dated past the move; the move waits for any transfer into the jar still in transit; a move recorded before this change is settled on the last day the old ledger touched the jar. Recorded as HANDOVER **P51** (applied unless Christopher objects); `JarDepartureTests` (15), `JarLedgerSqlTests.A_correction_to_a_kept_payment_is_settled_on_the_day_the_jar_left`; `JarLedgerActivationTests`' account-change case now reads jar 0 / unallocated 20 after the amount edit (the 20 is settled on the move day) instead of jar 20 / unallocated 0. (P2-12, client) *A slow save could paint the wrong month:* a jar-line save still in flight when the user changed month re-read `/jars` for the old month after the new month's read, so August's grid showed September's shelf; `refreshJars` now checks the month on screen (a ref set in a layout effect) before reading (`DASH-078`). (P2-13, client) *A failed jars read showed the first-run checklist:* `jarsLoaded` is now set only on success, and a failed read shows "Could not load your jars for this month" instead (`DASH-079`). (P2-14) *Deleting the Savings group bypassed rule 60:* the group's delete removed a live jar's monthly line, and the shelf then rewrote the month without it; `DeleteBudgetCategory` refuses a group that holds a jar's line (skip the month or archive the jar instead) and `SavingsGroup` hides the delete control there; an empty Savings group still deletes (`CCategoriesLinesCoverageTests.DeleteSavingsGroup_ThatHoldsAJarsLine_IsRefused_AndAnEmptyOneDeletes` replaces the old CAT-023 test; client CAT-023). **Schema:** migration `JarHistory` adds the nullable `Jars.ArchivedOn` date and the `JarRevisions` table (cascade from its jar, unique on `JarId, EffectiveFrom`); nothing is backfilled — a jar with no revision reads its current definition. `e2e/mocks.ts` needs no change (no new endpoint). **Validation (this machine, .NET SDK 10.0; no SQL Server or Docker daemon):** `dotnet build` of the unit and integration projects — 0 warnings, 0 errors; `dotnet ef migrations has-pending-model-changes` — none; unit suite **1165 passed** (1141 before: 24 new, one replaced, one expectation changed, two extended); from `client`: `npx tsc -b --noEmit` clean; `vitest` — **804 passed in 65 files** (801 before: 3 new, DASH-076/077 moved onto a shared harness); ESLint's findings are the ones `5dbaf02` already has; `npm run build` passed; `npx playwright test e2e/a11y.spec.ts e2e/ux-states.spec.ts` against the mocked API — 51 passed; `git diff --check` clean. **Not run locally:** the SQL integration tests (two new) and the real-backend journeys; CI runs them.

**Adversarial review, round 4 (29 September, same PR).** Christopher moved `49cbc96` onto the PR at 07:48 UTC; PR CI [run 36538841662](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36538841662) was green on all seven jobs (07:48–07:55 UTC). The fourth independent review, of that head, confirmed P2-7–P2-14 and rule 22 fixed and found one more must-fix, (P2-18) *an entry dated after a jar's move day survived the move:* rule 80's zero check read today only, so a jar could change account while an income dated ahead of payday in the old account was still put into it; from payday the old account held the jar's 200 again, and the user could neither edit that income (the kept-entry date guard refused it) nor archive or empty the jar. `UpdateJar` now refuses the account change while the old account has a stored entry that moves money in or out of the jar dated after today (`JarDepartures.EntryDatedAfterAsync`, beside the in-transit check): "Holiday has an entry dated 2 Oct 2026 in A. Change or remove it first, then change the account." (`JarDepartureTests.UpdateJar_IsRefused_WhileTheOldAccountHasAJarEntryDatedAfterToday`, fails without the fix; JAR-111; P51 amended). The round's other findings go to the follow-up PR with P2-15–P2-17 and P3-3, P3-5–P3-7: (P2-19) restoring a jar clears its archive date, so the months it spent archived read it as live; (P2-20, pre-existing on `main`) moving a jar into an account re-derives that account's earlier payments on the jar's line onto the jar; (P3-8) residues left by moves made before this PR block the archive until settled (backfill); (P3-9) the departure's read-modify-write takes no lock; (P3-10) each jar write reads the full ledger about five times. Unit suite **1166 passed** (1165 before); the solution builds with 0 warnings; no client change.

## W13 Joint accounts and privacy (rules 30–34; HANDOVER O8, P52–P60) — 29 September 2026

**Scope.** Accounts gain **holders** and per-login **privacy** (rules 30–34). An account lists its holders from People: none is the household's own account, one a personal account, two or more a joint account with one balance and one history (rule 30). A personal account whose holder has a login can be private to that login: the account, its balance and the transactions only it can see are not there for any other login — the owner included — and no one else can change them (rule 31). An account the holder adds for themselves starts private; one someone else adds for them starts shared and flags their sharing review; a login newly linked to a person who holds accounts gets a one-time **Review sharing** prompt that changes nothing until answered (rule 32). An account a jar uses — archived jars and jar allocation history included — can't be private, and choosing a private account for a jar shows the rule 34 warning with **Share the account and continue** or **Pick another account** (rules 33/34). Private money counts in household figures only on a budget line, where the others see it as "Private account", read-only; a private↔shared transfer shows on the shared side (P56/P57). O8's open questions were answered by the proposals P52–P59, posted to Christopher in the W13 thread before the build, and recorded in HANDOVER with P60 (deleting a private account), all applied unless he objects.

**Schema.** Migration `JointAccountsAndPrivacy`: table `AccountHolders` (AccountId, MemberId; composite key, cascade from the account and from the person, index on MemberId), table `AccountPrivacy` (AccountId key, cascade from the account; `UserId` the login it is private to; `SinceUtc`), and the bit `HouseholdMemberships.SharingReviewPending`. Nothing is backfilled: every existing account starts as the household's own, shared, and no review is pending.

**Enforcement, once for every handler (`ApplicationDbContext`).** The context takes `ICurrentUser` and reads as its login (`ViewerId`; null — background work, design time, tests that build it bare — filters nothing). Two global query filters: an `Account` private to another login is not there; a `Transaction` is there when any account it moves money in is visible, when it has no account, or when it (or one of its slices) is on a budget line. The filters read the unfiltered `AccountPrivacy` table, so the transaction filter does not inherit the account filter through navigation, and a hidden account's navigation reads null (the DTO then names it "Private account"). The write side is `SaveChanges`: any added, changed or deleted `Account`, `AccountHolder`, `Transaction`, `TransactionSplit` (through its transaction), `JarAllocation` or `Jar` that touches, before or after the change, an account private to another login is refused with 403 "This belongs to a private account. Only the login it is private to can change it."; the only exception is the visible side's bank leg of a private↔shared transfer (source: `DepartedOn`, `BankReference`, `Date`; receipt: `ReceivedOn`, `ReceiptBankReference`). A privacy row can be added, changed or removed only by the login it names.

**Application.** `AccountSharing` (holders from the household's active people, each once; `EnsureCanBePrivateAsync` — one holder, the caller's own person, no jar now or in its history; `BlockedReasonAsync`; `SetPrivateAsync`; `FlagReviewForHolderAsync`; `DecorateAsync` fills the DTO's `Holders`, `IsPrivate`, `CanChangeSharing`, `SharingBlockedReason`). `CreateAccount` takes `HolderIds` and `IsPrivate` (default: private when the caller holds it alone); `UpdateAccount` takes `HolderIds` (null keeps them; a private account's holders change only after it is shared). New `SetAccountSharing` (`PUT /accounts/{id}/sharing`), `GetSharingReview` / `CompleteSharingReview` (`GET`/`POST /accounts/sharing-review`), all open to any role since they concern the caller's own accounts; `/auth/me` carries `sharingReviewPending` (`SharingReview.IsDueAsync`). Accepting an invite linked to a person, or linking a login to a person, sets the review flag. `JarAccounts.EnsureVisibleForJarAsync` refuses a private account for a new jar or a jar's move with the `accountPrivate` error (the rule 34 wording) unless the request says `ShareAccount`, which removes the privacy first. `HouseholdMoney.Counts` keeps off-budget private money out of the month report's uncategorised figures, pending-FX counts and People income/spending for everyone. `AllocateIncome` names the person when their savings account is private to them; `UpdateHouseholdMember` keeps an unchanged savings-account id without re-validating it (the editor may not see it); `DeleteAccount` refuses a private account with money off the budget (P60).

**Client.** Accounts page: a **Held by** picker in the add and edit forms, **Keep it private** (ticked) when you hold the new account alone, **Private** / **Joint** badges and "Held by …" on the cards, and the holder's **Share with household** / **Make private**, or why a jar keeps it visible. `SharingReviewBanner` (in the shell): **Review sharing** → a dialog of the person's accounts as they are → **Save choices**. `JarFormModal`: a note when the chosen account is private and the rule 34 warning panel (`role="alert"`) with its two actions. Transactions page: another login's private entry reads "Private account" with no edit, assign or delete controls, and an in-transit transfer from a private account can still have its shared receipt confirmed. People page: a savings account private to the person reads "Private account" and an edit keeps it. `e2e/mocks.ts` answers the two new endpoints. USER_GUIDE §5 (Joint and private accounts) and §8 (Reviewing who sees your accounts); catalog ACCT-065–078, JAR-112, ALLOC-076, PPL-088; the automated-test map.

**Found and fixed during the build.** (1) A re-split of another login's private entry already split across lines changed only its slices, so the save guard — which looked at transactions, not slices — let it through; slices are now checked against their transaction's accounts (`OthersCannotReSplitAPrivateAccountsEntry_OnlyItsSlicesWouldChange`, fails without the fix). (2) Deleting an account keeps its transactions without an account, which would have shown a private account's off-budget money to everyone; the delete is refused while any is left (P60). (3) The People page named a hidden savings account "—" and its select showed "None".

**Review fixes (adversarial review of PR #277 at `eaf9f5e`, `/mnt/project-files/reviews/pr-277/`).** Each P1/P2 was reproduced by the reviewer's probes and has a regression test that fails on `eaf9f5e`. **P1-1:** `AutoCategorizer.ApplyAsync` put a private entry left off the budget on a line from the payee's history (entering it, or every row of a statement imported into the account), which made it visible to everyone; it now skips transactions in a private account, so only the holder puts private money on the budget (P56). `PreviewImport` suggestions stay: the holder confirms them. **P1-2:** the allocation planner treated a savings account private to another login as no account, so the owner's run reported success and moved nothing for that person, and the person's own re-run failed with 403. The planner now flags those people (`MemberAllocationDto.SavingsPrivate`); `AllocateIncome` refuses up front, before anything moves, when such a person has a share or an earlier run's transfer ("Sam's savings account is private, so only Sam can record a transfer into it…"), and a profile whose source is private to another login is refused with its own message; the Allocation page shows "Private savings account" with that note instead of "No savings account" (P53). **P2-1:** the refund cap and the purchase's edit and delete checks counted only the refunds the caller could see; `RefundRules.AllRefundsAsync` reads them all (a hidden one by amount and date only) and the messages don't name what the caller can't see. **P2-2:** the rule 34 warning named one account and **Share the account and continue** shared whichever the select held; the warning now belongs to its account and goes when the account changes. **P2-3:** `UpdateAccount` validated the holder list before seeing it was unchanged, so an archived holder blocked even a rename; an unchanged list is kept, and a holder already on the account may stay while archived. **P2-4:** making an account private and putting a jar on it could both pass their checks at once; `ApplicationDbContext.SaveChanges` now locks the account row (`UPDLOCK, HOLDLOCK`, in the save's transaction) of every jar added or moved and every privacy row added, and re-checks committed plus pending state: the second writer is refused (`IsPrivate`, naming the jar; or the rule 34 warning to a jar's own holder). **P2-5:** P59 as built let only the same login ever see the account again, unlike the proposal as posted ("hidden until that person has a login again"). The first fix (`47126a6`) handed a removed login's private account to whichever login was then linked to its holder; the second review showed that let the owner read it (below), so that hand-over is gone and P59 is "the same verified email only", which today is the same login. HANDOVER P53, P55, P56 and P59 say so. **Folded-in P3s:** `DecorateAsync` reads what blocks privacy in two queries for the whole list, not two per account (P3-1); a transaction or jar payment in another login's private account carries no account id (P3-2); a shared→private transfer waiting for its departure offers **Add departure date** to the others (P3-4); a login created by a direct invite for a person now flags the sharing review, as the other ways of linking do (rule 32).

**Second review (at `47126a6`, `round2-review-47126a6.md`).** Every fix above held. **R2-P1-1:** the P59 hand-over let the owner read any member's private account: remove the person's login, link the owner's own login to that person, and the account passed to the owner — and stayed with them after linking back; linking another existing login, or inviting a new one the owner controls, did the same. The owner decides who is linked to whom, so "the person's next login" can't be told from the owner. The hand-over is removed (`HandOverOrphanedPrivacyAsync`, `HeirAsync` and the guard's exception for it): a privacy row changes only by its own login, and the account passes only to a login with the same verified email as the one that chose privacy. An email belongs to one login (`RequireUniqueEmail`) and the app neither deletes logins nor changes their email, so that is the same login id and needs no stored email; a feature that deletes logins or changes emails must add one. `ARemovedLoginsPrivateAccount_PassesToNoOtherLogin_ButThatLoginSeesItAgain_P59` covers the owner's own login, another existing login and a new one (it fails on `47126a6`). Christopher chose this ("Same email only") over keeping the hand-over on 29 September 2026.

**Follow-ups from the review (not in this PR).** P3-3: deleting a budget line or category that holds another login's private entry takes the entry off the budget through the database's `SET NULL`, without the guard seeing it; refuse, or ask, when the line holds someone else's private entries. P3-5: the People page shows "Private account" for any savings id it can't find, including a deleted account's dangling id, which used to read "—". P3-6: the guard's 403 text ("This belongs to a private account") can reach flows that never mention the account; map those flows to validation messages as `AllocateIncome` now does. R2-P3-1: the refund checks run before anything is written, so another login can try refund amounts, or purchase amounts and dates, and narrow down a hidden refund's total and earliest date; the messages hide the figures, and this is inherent to enforcing P20 across logins. P59: an account private to a login removed for good can't be reached by anyone; an owner-side way to erase it and its off-budget entries without seeing them needs the user's word first.

**Test maintenance** is itemised in TEST-PLAN (W13 review): nothing obsolete or duplicated was found in the touched areas; 25 backend unit tests, 2 SQL integration tests and 10 client tests are new (the review fixes added 8, 1 and 3 of them and rewrote the P59 test). **Validation (this machine, .NET SDK 10.0; SQL Server 2022 in Docker):** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx` — 0 warnings, 0 errors; `dotnet ef migrations has-pending-model-changes` — none; unit suite **1191 passed** (1166 before: 25 new); SQL integration suite against SQL Server 2022 **79 passed** (77 before: 2 new). From `client`: `npx tsc -b` clean; `vitest` — **814 passed in 66 files** (804 before: 10 new); `npm run build` passed; ESLint on the new client files is clean and the changed pages keep only the findings `main` has; `npx playwright test` (all mocked specs) — 101 of 102 passed, the one failure `keyboard-a11y.spec.ts` UX-005 (focus ring on the skip link) fails 3 of 6 runs on `main` too; `git diff --check` clean. **Not run locally:** the real-backend journeys; CI runs them. The #275 release record is [PR #276](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/276)'s.

## W08 Monetary units and conversion (rules 41–44, 63, 85; HANDOVER O5, P61–P67) — 29 September 2026

**Scope.** The app stops converting money by itself (REVIEW A2/A5/P2). A household has one **budget currency** (its first month's, else the owner's chosen currency, else EUR — `BudgetCurrency.ResolveAsync`), fixed once the household holds an account, a month or a transaction (`IsFixedAsync`, which counts private ones too); every new account, month and hand-entered transaction takes it and an account in another currency is refused (rule 63). A row in another currency — a statement row with no account, or one on an older account kept in another currency — is recorded in its own currency and **waits** (`FxPending`): it is in no figure until the user enters its budget-currency amount (rules 43/85). **Suggest** gives the ECB reference rate for the row's date and the amount it gives, rounded to cents, and records nothing (P63); **Confirm amount** restates the row in the budget currency and keeps the foreign figure as `OriginalAmount`/`OriginalCurrency` (rule 42, P64). No fee is inferred anywhere (rule 44). Christopher chose **Keep counting** for existing data (P67).

**Removed.** `FxRateResolver` (import-time conversion), `FxReconciliationService` (the hosted worker that converted waiting rows when a rate appeared — the unapproved conversion A2 found), `Transaction.ExchangeRate` and `Transaction.BaseAmount`, `TransactionDto.ExchangeRate/BaseAmount`, `JarPaymentDto.BaseAmount`, `RefundRules.Totals`/`RefundLeg`. Every reader that multiplied by a rate (`BudgetActuals`, `ReportFigures`, member income and spending, `JarCycles.Link`, jar payments, refund totals) now sums `Amount`, since every row that counts is in the budget currency; waiting rows stay excluded as before. The Frankfurter client stays, for suggestions only.

**Schema.** Migration `ForeignAmounts`: adds `Transactions.OriginalAmount` (decimal(18,4)) and `OriginalCurrency` (nvarchar(3)), and `BudgetAmount` (decimal(18,4)) on `Transactions` and `TransactionSplits`, then brings existing rows to the new rule before dropping `ExchangeRate`. It first stops with an error, changing nothing, if a household has budget months in more than one currency (its budget currency would be ambiguous; the API then does not start, so run census Q2 before deploying). Per household (first month's currency, else the owner's normalised preference, else EUR), for each non-transfer row in another currency that counted: the old resolver converted into the row's month's currency, or into EUR when that month had no budget, so the row keeps its figure only when that currency is the budget currency, and otherwise waits for the user's amount. A kept row with no account becomes `ROUND(Amount × ExchangeRate, 4)` in the budget currency with the foreign figure kept (none at rate 1: the amount was typed as the budget figure), its split slices scaled the same way with the largest slice taking the rounding difference. A kept row on an account keeps its amount, the account's money, and gets the figure as `BudgetAmount`, its slices sharing it the same way (Christopher's "Support them"). A waiting row already in the budget currency now counts; transfers are untouched; months already in another currency are not changed. `Down` drops the new columns and re-adds `ExchangeRate` at 1; it is lossy (budget and reference figures are lost).

**Application and API.** `ForeignAmounts` (`MarkWaiting`, `SuggestAsync`, `Counted`, `ShareOverSlices`). Budget, report, People and refund figures read `BudgetAmount ?? Amount`; account and jar balances read `Amount`. `ConfirmConvertedAmountCommand` (`PUT /api/transactions/{id}/converted-amount`, `[AllowLimited]`): refused when the row doesn't wait or is split; on an account it stores the figure as `BudgetAmount` and leaves the amount, otherwise it restates the row; a refund must still fit its purchase and a purchase still cover its refunds (P66). `GetConversionSuggestionQuery` (`GET /api/transactions/conversion-suggestion?currency&amount&date`, refused for a future date). `CreateTransaction` stores the budget currency and an optional `OriginalAmount`/`OriginalCurrency` (P65). Imports mark waiting rows (`ImportStatementResult.AwaitingAmount`). `UpdateTransaction` no longer clears the waiting flag on an amount edit (A5: the edited amount is still in the row's own currency); changing the amount of a row with a `BudgetAmount` makes it wait again. `SplitTransaction` shares a `BudgetAmount` over the slices. `MarkJarCyclePaid` refuses a waiting payment, and a cycle paid from an account kept in another currency records the payment's budget figure. `LinkRefund` and `RefundRules` refuse a link to a waiting purchase. `CreateAccount`, `CreateJar`/`UpdateJar` (via `JarAccounts`) refuse another currency; `CreateBudgetMonth` takes the budget currency, including for a month backfilled before the first. `/auth/me` returns `budgetCurrency` and `currencyLocked`; `PUT /auth/preferences` refuses a currency change once it is fixed.

**Client.** `budgetCurrency`/`currencyLocked` in `AuthContext` label all budget money. Transactions: a waiting row shows its own figure ("GBP 12.50"), a **Needs EUR amount** tag and **Enter amount** (not on a split row) opening `ConvertedAmountModal` (Suggest, editable amount, Confirm amount, the server's refusal; on an account row it says the account keeps its amount); a confirmed row shows its foreign figure in small print, and a row with a budget figure shows "Counts as €…"; the totals leave waiting rows out and count budget figures; the add form's **Paid in another currency** (behind the `MultiCurrency` flag) sends the foreign figure. Accounts: no currency picker, totals count budget-currency accounts only and name the others; an account's page shows "Counts as … in the budget" under such rows. Jars: **Mark paid** lists a waiting payment but it can't be chosen. Settings: the currency is read-only once fixed. Dashboard and People wording follows ("waiting for their amount in EUR"). `e2e/mocks.ts` answers the two new endpoints.

**Review fixes (adversarial review of PR #278 at 11d6ae0).** P1-1: the first build set rows on an account kept in another currency waiting and refused to confirm them, so they (and every later import onto that account) could never count; Christopher chose **Support them**, built as `BudgetAmount` above. P2-2: a waiting payment could fulfil a jar cycle; refused. P2-3: a purchase and its refund restated at different days' rates could leave the refund larger than the purchase and then refuse every edit of either; the checks now allow any edit that doesn't push them further apart, and compare counted figures. P2-4: a household with months in more than one currency was silently re-based on its earliest month; the migration now stops instead. P2-5: rows the old resolver had converted into its EUR default were read as budget-currency figures; the migration now keys on the currency the old figure was in. P3: the waiting-purchase refusal names the purchase; **Confirm amount** can't be sent twice; suggestions for a future date are refused; the migration keys on the row's currency, not the account's.

**Follow-ups (not in this PR).**
- Before deploying: run census Q2 (`/mnt/project-files/reviews/pr-278/pre-deploy-census.sql`) against production; a household with months in more than one currency stops the migration and the API with it.
- Data where a refund already exceeds its purchase after restating (census Q4) stays as it is; whether to restate such refunds at their purchase's rate is Christopher's call.
- The suggestion cache stores Frankfurter's answer under the date asked for. For a date the ECB has not published yet (today before about 16:00 CET, or a weekend), Frankfurter answers with the latest published day's rate, so yesterday's rate is saved as today's and every later Suggest for today shows it until the API restarts. The cache also has no size bound (P3-2).
- A row taken off an account kept in another currency without changing its amount keeps its `BudgetAmount` and its foreign currency with no account. It counts the same figure, so nothing is wrong on screen, but `Transaction.BudgetAmount`'s doc says only a row on such an account has one (R2-P3-1).
- `/auth/me` mixes two households for a member: `budgetCurrency` is the viewed household's, while `currencyLocked` is the login's own (P3-5). The locked currency field in settings also shows the owner's chosen currency while its note says "Your budget currency", which is the first budget month's; on older data the two can differ.
- Months created in another currency before W08 keep their currency.

**Test maintenance** is itemised in TEST-PLAN (W08 review): 17 backend unit tests that pinned automatic conversion were removed with it and 6 rewritten; 27 backend unit tests, 3 SQL integration tests and 10 client tests are new (9, 2 and 1 of them from the review fixes).

**CI and release of [PR #278](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/278).** Round 2 of its review was clean on `544d0b6`, and PR CI [run 36586546443](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36586546443) was green on its last head `70047ca` (all seven jobs). **Before merging**, Christopher ran the census in `/mnt/project-files/reviews/pr-278/pre-deploy-census.sql` on production at 16:43 UTC: Q1 returned one informational row ("UK Account" in GBP, with no transactions and no jars), and Q2 and Q3 returned no rows, so the migration had no mixed-currency household to stop on and no non-EUR household whose figures would move. **Released.** PR #278 was squash-merged by Christopher as `4b549fe8a9064b786e3fa66ee28003507f0bdfdb` on 29 September 2026 at 16:45:14 UTC. The push to `main` triggered CI [run 36600020423](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36600020423) and Deploy [run 36600020198](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36600020198) at 16:45:17 UTC. Deploy succeeded end to end: the release builds (staging 16:45:21–16:46:42 UTC, production 16:45:21–16:46:46), the automatic staging deploy (16:46:44–16:47:30 UTC; "Extract and deploy on VPS" 16:47:02–16:47:28) and the production deploy, which needs approval (16:47:33–16:48:18 UTC; "Extract and deploy on VPS" 16:47:45–16:48:16). The release carries migration **`ForeignAmounts`** (columns `Transactions.BudgetAmount`, `OriginalAmount`, `OriginalCurrency` and `TransactionSplits.BudgetAmount`; existing rows brought to the one rule as described above; `Transactions.ExchangeRate` dropped), applied at API start on staging and then production; the deploy's health check passed. CI on `main` succeeded (16:45:17–16:51:44 UTC; all seven jobs green: backend 16:45:23–16:47:03, integration 16:45:21–16:47:11, journey 16:45:21–16:51:43, frontend 16:45:21–16:48:51, a11y 16:45:21–16:49:24, deploy-scripts 16:45:21–16:45:31, security-scan 16:45:21–16:46:07). Times are from the GitHub Actions API. The two follow-up lines above on the suggestion cache (P3-2) and `/auth/me` (P3-5) were corrected by PR #276 when it merged `main`, from the #278 review's notes.

## W05 follow-up: the findings the #275 reviews deferred (P2-15–P2-17, P2-19, P2-20, P3-3, P3-5–P3-10) — 29 September 2026

**Scope.** The findings rounds 3 and 4 of the #275 reviews left for a follow-up PR. Each was reproduced on `main` at `05329cd` before it was changed (the round 3 and round 4 probes, re-run), and each fix has a regression test that fails without it. One finding did not reproduce and is skipped: **P3-3** (a past month's history line showing a cycle that had not closed by the month's end) was fixed by #275's round 3 commit, whose history-line filter keeps only cycles before the one open then; the round 3 reproduction (a dismissed cycle followed by lazily settled ones) passes on `main`, on both months involved. One new migration, **`JarArchivePeriodsAndArrival`** (table `JarArchivePeriods`, nullable `Jars.FundingAccountSince`), no endpoint change, and no new endpoint the client calls.

- **(P2-15) A month before a moved jar's first posting in its old account read Unavailable.** `JarLedgerSnapshot.BalanceOf` counted an old account as holding the jar only from its first posting there, so before it (with the jar's current account opened later) no available account held it. Each account's view now carries the jars its ledger carries on any date (`JarLedgerView.CarriedJars`), and an open account that carries the jar holds 0 of it before its first posting. Only a date before every account that held the jar opened reads unavailable (rule 67). Test: the July case added to `JarMonthViewW05Tests.Rule80_AJarMovedToAnAccountOpenedLater_…` (read null on `main`).
- **(P2-16, client) Typing an amount on a skipped jar line kept its Skipped tag.** `withItemPlanned` now clears `isSkipped` when the typed amount is not 0, as `UpdateBudgetItem` does on the server (rule 60). DASH-080.
- **(P2-17, client) An answer that returned after the user changed month repainted the new month's grid.** Creating a month, "Plan it again", a save's reconcile and a failed save's rollback set the month after an await. `DashboardPage` now keeps the viewed month in a ref set on every month change (the one DASH-078 added, moved up), each handler takes `stillOn()` when it starts, and an answer only lands while its month is still viewed; `refreshMonth` and the create handlers check the month they were made for, and the skip dialog's answer is checked against its own month. DASH-081 (a create, and "Plan it again").
- **(P2-19) Restoring a jar rewrote the months it spent archived.** Restore cleared `ArchivedOn`, so a month that ended while the jar was archived read it live (a Recurring jar with no line there read "Deadline missed, short 300"). Restore now records the stretch in a new `JarArchivePeriod` (archived on, restored on; cascade on jar delete and removed explicitly by `DeleteJar`). `JarHistory.ArchiveOn` marks a month's definition archived when a period covers its end, `JarFigures` applies it to a month that is over, and `GetJars` no longer lists a jar on a past month it spent archived with no line there. An archive made before archives were dated has no date, so restoring it keeps no stretch: its months read the jar as live again, as they did on `main`.
- **(P2-20, pre-existing) Moving a jar into an account pulled that account's earlier payments on the jar's line onto the jar.** The reader looked the line's jar up by today's account. At the move, the new account's payments on the jar's lines dated up to the move day are now written down as they read then, on its unallocated money (`JarMovements.KeepArrivalAttributionAsync`, the mirror of `KeepLineAttributionAsync`), and the day is recorded (`Jar.FundingAccountSince`). `JarLedgerReader.HeldOn` draws a line payment from the jar only when the jar was held in the paying account on its date, from the move day on; `ApplyOutgoingAsync` uses the same rule and keeps such a written-down payment on unallocated money while it stays dated on the move day. A payment on the jar's line dated before the move day is refused, from either account, and so is a date edit that moves one there (`TransactionInvariants`). `GetJarPayments` lists a payment as from the jar exactly as the ledger reads it in the jar's current account (a draw kept in an account the jar has left is listed as not from the jar; follow-up R2-P3-8). A move made before the day was recorded keeps its old reading. P51 is amended. The first version of this fix gave the move day to the old account; the review of this PR found that a payment recorded on the move day then never left the jar (P2-A below).
- **(P3-5) An out-of-range month was a 500.** New `GetJarsQueryValidator`: `Month` and `AsOf` must fall in 2000–2100, the years a budget month can have, so the pipeline answers 400.
- **(P3-6, P3-10) The ledger was loaded again for every date.** `JarLedgerReader` now loads the rows once (`LoadAsync`) and reads them as of any date in memory (`JarLedgerSource.At`; `ReadAsync` is the two together). `JarFigures` reads the month's two dates from one load, `JarPlanner.PlanMonthAsync` loads once and `BalancesAsync` and `ReplantAsync` take a loaded ledger, `UpdateJar` and `ArchiveJar` load once before the save for the zero check and the replant, and `JarCycles` loads once per settle or close, which replaces `BalanceBeforeAsync` (removed). One `GET /jars` for a past month now loads the ledger once (it was twice; still twice while a Recurring jar sits in Deadline missed), and a jar edit loads it twice (it was four times; five with an account change). Dismissing a missed cycle still loads it four times (review of this PR, P3-7, follow-up). `JarDepartures.SettleAsync` loads the departures for all the leg's jars in one query and skips a jar still held in the leg's account that never left it, so a leg on the account's own jars costs no query per jar.
- **(P3-7) Stale docs.** The P49 sentence and the TEST-PLAN clause were already corrected by #275's round 3 commit. This PR fixes JAR-103's expected result (the shelf shows no "to go"), the `JarDto` comments on `CurrentBalance` and `PlannedThisMonth`, and the comment on `Rule48_APastMonthsStatus_IsWhatItWasAtTheMonthsEnd` (a 300 target and 250 spent, not 1,200 and 900).
- **(P3-8) Moves made before departures were recorded could block the archive.** New `JarDepartures.BackfillAsync`, run at API start after the migrations (`Program.cs`, idempotent and non-fatal like the other backfills). For each jar with postings in an account it is no longer designated to and no departure there, it records one, settling what is left there. A move made before PR #274 (26 to 28 September) wrote nothing down, so the payments the jar's lines drew from the old account lost the jar at the move: the backfill writes them down first, as they read while the jar was held there, and dates the departure on the last day that account's ledger touched the jar (its last posting, on the day the ledger posts it, or its last such payment), so the dates before it read as they did before the move (review of this PR, P2-B). It is a startup backfill, not SQL in the migration, so the logic is unit-tested; production could not be checked from here.
- **(P3-9) The departure's read-modify-write had no lock.** `JarAllocationPosting.Amount` is now a concurrency token. A second writer that read a departure before another shifted it gets `DbUpdateConcurrencyException`, which the API answers with 409 ("Something else changed this at the same moment. Reload and try again."), instead of silently overwriting the first shift. This was cheaper than taking a second account's ledger lock inside writers that already hold one (lock ordering). Two writers that both create the same missing legacy departure are not covered; the startup backfill leaves none to create.

**Test maintenance** is itemised in TEST-PLAN (W05 follow-up). The P2-15 case extends the existing `Rule80_AJarMovedToAnAccountOpenedLater_…` test instead of adding a duplicate. `JarCycles.BalanceBeforeAsync` is dead once the settle and close paths read one loaded ledger, and is removed. Nothing else was obsolete: no existing test asserted the behaviour changed here.

**Validation (this machine, .NET SDK 10.0; no SQL Server or Docker daemon).** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors. Unit suite **1179 passed** (1166 before: 13 new). Client `vitest run` **807 passed** in 65 files (3 new). The integration project builds; its three new SQL tests (the backfill's cross-owner query, the concurrency token, the archive-period table) run in CI only.


**CI and adversarial review of [PR #276](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/276).** PR CI [run 36552578223](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36552578223) was green on `2ff1215` (all seven jobs, 09:58–10:05 UTC on 29 September). The independent review of that head confirmed P2-16, P2-17, P2-19 (dated archives), P3-5, P3-8, P3-9 and the P3-3 skip, and the #275 release record, and found one must-fix this PR introduced and one more that had to be fixed before the deploy runs the backfill. Both are fixed here, each with regression tests that fail without the fix:

- **(P2-A) A payment on the jar's line recorded on the move day never came out of the jar.** The first P2-20 fix gave the move day to the old account, but from the move on a payment on the jar's line can only be recorded from the new account. So a 300 deposit paid from B on Holiday's line the day Holiday moved to B left Holiday at 300, dropped B's unallocated money by 600, showed in the jar's payments list as from the jar, and, marked as a bill's payment, carried 300 into the next cycle. The move day now belongs to the new account. The new account's payments on the jar's lines dated up to the move day are written down at the move, so they stay on its unallocated money. A payment on the jar's line dated before the move is refused, from either account. For a payment from the new account, Christopher chose this on 29 September 2026 (14:05 UTC) over letting it draw from the jar, as `main` did. `GetJarPayments` reads the same rule (details in the P2-20 bullet). The tests: `P2_20_APaymentOnTheJarsLineFromItsNewAccount_DrawsFromTheJarFromTheMoveDay`, which replaces the test that pinned the old rule; `…ABillPaidOnTheMoveDay_MarkedPaid_LeavesNothingHeld`; `…APaymentOnTheJarsLineDatedBeforeTheMove_IsRefused_FromEitherAccount`; `…ThatAccountsPaymentRecordedBeforeTheMove_DatedOnTheMoveDay_StaysOnItsUnallocatedMoney`; `…ARoundTripTheSameDay_PaymentsFromTheJarsAccountDrawFromIt`; and `…APaymentTheJarMadeInItsOldAccount_CanStillBeRedatedBeforeTheMove`, which checks that the date-edit refusal leaves P51's kept entries alone after a one-way move (round 2 below extends this to a move away and back). The client's "paid from" picker is unchanged. It treats a jar line as drawing from the jar, which is now true of every payment the forms can save on it. The payee auto-categoriser (P2-E below) is the exception.
- **(P2-B) The backfill misdated moves made before PR #274.** It dated their departure on the last stored posting, before the old account's line payments, so July read 0 where the jar held 500. It now writes those payments down first and dates the departure on the later of the last posting (a transfer leg on the day it posts) and the last such payment. The tests: `P3_8_AMoveMadeBefore274_WritesDownTheOldAccountsLinePaymentsBeforeDatingTheDeparture`, `P3_8_TheBackfill_DatesATransferLegOnTheDayItPosts`, and on SQL Server `JarLedgerSqlTests.A_move_made_before_274_has_its_old_line_payments_written_down_before_its_departure`.

**Left for a follow-up PR (found by the review of #276).**

- **(P2-C, pre-existing)** After a move, a jar that never had an entry in its old account reads Unavailable on months before its new account opened. `RecordAsync` writes no departure there. The fix is to always record the zero departure and to have `JarUsage.EverHeldMoneyAsync` ignore departures, or a jar that was only moved becomes undeletable (rule 84).
- **(P2-D)** Creating a past month after a restore plants a line for a jar that was archived all through that month. `CreateBudgetMonth` should apply the archive periods to a month that is over.
- **(P2-E, pre-existing)** The payee auto-categoriser puts a learned jar line on a payment from any account, skipping `EnsureJarAccount`. This is left out because W13 (PR #277) is changing the same categoriser.
- **(H4)** A jar moved before this PR has no recorded arrival day, so its new account's earlier payments on its lines still draw from it.
- **P3s:**
  1. `CarriedJars` ignores when an account held the jar.
  2. A past month's `IsArchived` flag means two different things.
  3. A move to an account with no opening date replants from the balance before the edit.
  4. An archive made before archives were dated still reads live once restored. Count those rows in production before choosing a fix.
  5. The backfill re-reads every kept posting at each start, and two instances starting together could both write.
  6. The concurrency token does not cover inserts, so `UpdateJar` should lock both accounts.
  7. Not every path reads the ledger once yet: Deadline missed reads it twice, dismiss four times, `JarFigures.ReadAsync`'s ledger parameter is never passed, and the archive periods and lines are queried twice.
  8. A client answer that returns after the user moved to a later month is dropped without re-reading that month.
  9. A failed save rolls back a whole-month snapshot.
  10. A late answer can close a dialog the user opened on the new month.
  11. A late answer can show an error banner on the new month.
  12. 18 of the 20 new client guards have no test.

The same review's corrections to this PR's own docs are made here: the undated-archive sentence, the "loads once" claims, the testing map's P2-19 wording and the `JarDto.CurrentBalance` comment. **Validation (this machine, as above).** The build has 0 warnings. The unit suite has **1186 passed**: 1179 before, 7 new and 1 rewritten. There is no client change. The review's probes for P2-A and P2-B pass. The exceptions are Lead2 and H1b, which now meet the refusal they were written against, and H3 and H4, which are in the follow-up list. The new SQL test runs in CI.

**Merged with `main` after #277 (W13).** `main` (`8859322`) was merged into this branch with a merge commit, so the review of `4835df5` still applies to its own commits. The two PRs met only in four docs: both sides are kept, and this PR's jar cases are renumbered JAR-113–117 because W13 took JAR-112 (JAR-115–119 since the merge with #278, below). `JarArchivePeriodsAndArrival` (09:49:38) applies after `JointAccountsAndPrivacy` (09:41:02); the model snapshot merged cleanly with both, and `dotnet ef migrations has-pending-model-changes` reports none. W13's privacy filters and save guard act only when a login is signed in. The startup backfill runs in a scope with no request, so `CurrentUser.UserId` is null and it reads and writes every account, as before. The accounts it writes to hold stored jar postings, which W13 already keeps from being made private (rule 33). The one place the two PRs meet in code is a jar moved into its holder's private account with **Share the account and continue**: the account's payments on the jar's line are written down in the same save that shares it. New `JointAccountsW13Tests.MovingAJarIntoTheHoldersPrivateAccount_SharesIt_AndItsEarlierPaymentOnTheJarsLineStaysOffTheJar_Rule34` pins it (a 50 payment from Sam's account on Holiday's line dated the move day: Holiday 0 and Sam's account 950 unallocated afterwards; it reads −50 with the write-down removed). **Validation (this machine, as above).** The build has 0 warnings and no pending model changes.
