**Second round of the adversarial review of #276 (on `4835df5` and the merge `a4b946c`).** PR CI [run 36567714191](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36567714191) was green on `a4b946c`. The review found the merge clean and three must-fixes, and recommended three more for the same push because the code was changing anyway. All six are fixed here, each with a regression test that fails when its fix is reverted:

- **(R2-P1-1) The startup backfill left a phantom balance from payments dated before the old account opened.** It wrote down every unstored line payment from the old account, including ones dated before the account's opening date, which its ledger never counted, and it settled the stored postings rather than what the ledger reads. Such a jar kept that amount in the old account for good (Holiday 100 today, archive and further moves refused). The backfill now loads the account first. With no opening date it writes nothing down and settles the stored postings, since the ledger can't read that account yet. Otherwise it writes down only payments dated on or after the opening date, and each departure settles what the ledger reads the jar holding in that account on the departure's date. Taking the amount from the ledger was also meant to fix R2-P3-4 (a stored split on a transfer that hasn't left its account read the jar at −200), but it left that share out for good once the leg posts; round 3 found and fixed that (R3-P2-1 below). Test: `P3_8_TheBackfill_WritesDownNoPaymentDatedBeforeTheOldAccountOpened`.
- **(R2-P2-1) A move-day payment with a stored "paid from" answer, put on the jar's line, still stayed off the jar.** The keep branch in `ApplyOutgoingAsync`, meant for the arrival write-down, fired for any stored leg that didn't draw from the jar: "paid from Car" on a Groceries payment, or a draw kept from another jar that left the account that day. Moved onto Holiday's line, such a payment went to unallocated money; split onto it with the form's pre-filled answer, Holiday's slice came out of Car. The branch now fires only for a leg that draws from no jar, which only the arrival write-down (or the branch itself) stores. Tests: `P2_20_AMoveDayPaymentPaidFromAnotherJar_PutOnTheJarsLine_DrawsFromTheJar` (moved onto the line and its payee edited; split 50 + 30 with "paid from Car": Holiday 170, Car 70, B 600 unallocated) and `P2_20_AMoveDayPaymentKeptFromAJarThatLeftTheSameDay_PutOnTheJarsLine_DrawsFromTheJar`. Known limit: an arrival write-down re-split with a slice paid from another jar has its jar-line slice charged to the paid-from jar as well (in the review's K2, Car drops by 50 instead of 20); only P2-E data can reach that.
- **(R2-P2-2) After a move away and back, the jar's earlier payments in that account couldn't be re-dated or re-split, and the refusal gave a false reason.** `FundingAccountSince` holds only the latest arrival, so the arrival check refused every date before the return, including the jar's own payments from its first stretch there. A date edit that keeps the account, and a re-split, now skip that check for a jar the leg is stored as drawing from and that left that account on or after the new date (`TransactionInvariants.KeptDrawJarsAsync`, the stricter of the reviewer's two variants). Its departure governs such a leg, as P51 says, and a date past the departure is still refused. A new payment, or one whose draw is derived rather than stored, is still refused. Test: `P51_AfterTheJarMovesAwayAndBack_ItsEarlierPaymentsThereCanStillBeRedatedAndResplit` (re-dated 4 → 5 August and split 100 + 50: 300 on 4 August, 50 on 5 August, 0 today, A 600 unallocated).
- **(R2-P2-3) The backfill had no date window.** A payment from the old account dated after the move (the payee auto-categoriser can still put one on the jar's line, P2-E) was written onto the jar. The write-down now stops at the earlier of the run's date and 29 September 2026, the day #275 reached production, since every move from then on records its departure. `BackfillAsync` takes the run's date for the test: `P3_8_TheBackfill_WritesDownNoPaymentDatedAfterDeparturesWereRecorded`. The cap does not cover the review's other case, a payment dated before the jar ever reached the old account (only a mistaken legacy round trip leaves one), because no bound from the first posting is safe: it would drop real draws and bring P2-B back. The query below lists such payments for the owner to check.
- **(R2-P3-1) False or circular refusal messages.** A payment dated before the move from the old account was told to be recorded from the new one, which refused it too. It now gets one message: "'Holiday' is the jar Holiday, which moved to B on 29 Sep 2026. A payment dated before then can't be added to its line from any account. Record it on another line." The arrival refusal no longer says the payment "was made while its money was held elsewhere", which is false after a round trip; it says "A payment on its line from A can't be dated before then." HANDOVER P51 and the USER_GUIDE drop that reason. P51 records the old-account refusal as temporary: Christopher decided R2-D1 (below). `P2_20_APaymentOnTheJarsLineDatedBeforeTheMove_IsRefused_FromEitherAccount` now checks A's message.
- **(R2-P3-2) A failed or partial backfill went unnoticed.** The API logs the backfill's counts (departures recorded, line payments written down) at Warning, which reaches the Windows Event Log, and a failure at Error. The backfill saves once, so a failed run leaves nothing behind.
- **Doc corrections:** payments "recorded before the move" are the ones "dated up to the move day" (R2-P3-11: P51, USER_GUIDE, this log and the `UpdateJar` comment); "8 new and 1 replaced" above is 7 new and 1 rewritten (R2-P3-12); `GetJarPayments`' "exactly as the ledger reads it" is narrowed to the jar's current account (R2-P3-8).

**Before deploying.** Run these two read-only queries on production (and on staging, which runs the same backfill on its own data) and look at what the backfill will do. The first lists, for each jar with postings in an account it no longer names and no departure there, every line payment from that account that has no stored answer there, and says whether the backfill writes it down. A payment split across two such jars' lines is listed once per jar but written down once, and the Amount column is the whole payment:

```sql
WITH Legacy AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT j.Id AS JarId, j.Name AS Jar, a.AccountId
    FROM JarAllocationPostings p
    JOIN JarAllocations a ON a.Id = p.JarAllocationId
    JOIN Jars j ON j.Id = p.JarId
    WHERE a.AccountId <> j.FundingAccountId
      AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM JarAllocationPostings dp JOIN JarAllocations d ON d.Id = dp.JarAllocationId
                      WHERE d.Kind = 3 AND d.AccountId = a.AccountId AND dp.JarId = j.Id))
SELECT l.Jar, acc.Name AS OldAccount, acc.OpeningDate, t.Date, t.Amount, t.Payee,
       CASE WHEN acc.OpeningDate IS NULL THEN 'not written: no opening date'
            WHEN t.Date < acc.OpeningDate THEN 'not written: before the opening date'
            WHEN t.Date > '2026-09-29' THEN 'not written: after 29 Sep 2026'
            ELSE 'written down' END AS Backfill
FROM Legacy l
JOIN Accounts acc ON acc.Id = l.AccountId
JOIN Transactions t ON t.AccountId = l.AccountId AND t.Type = 0
WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM JarAllocations s WHERE s.TransactionId = t.Id AND s.AccountId = l.AccountId)
  AND (EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM BudgetItems bi WHERE bi.Id = t.BudgetItemId AND bi.SavingsId = l.JarId)
       OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM TransactionSplits sp JOIN BudgetItems bi ON bi.Id = sp.BudgetItemId
                  WHERE sp.TransactionId = t.Id AND bi.SavingsId = l.JarId))
ORDER BY l.Jar, t.Date;
```

The second lists every such group, including those with no candidate payment, which still get a departure (round 3, R3-P3-2). A payment in the first query dated before the group's `FirstEntryThere` is a candidate pre-arrival payment (B1, R2A2b above), worth a look. A non-zero `TransferLegsInTransit` means the jar will read that share below zero in the old account until the leg is confirmed (R3-P2-1 below):

```sql
WITH Legacy AS (
    SELECT j.Id AS JarId, j.Name AS Jar, a.AccountId,
           SUM(p.Amount) AS StoredThere, MIN(a.Date) AS FirstEntryThere, MAX(a.Date) AS LastEntryThere
    FROM JarAllocationPostings p
    JOIN JarAllocations a ON a.Id = p.JarAllocationId
    JOIN Jars j ON j.Id = p.JarId
    WHERE a.AccountId <> j.FundingAccountId
      AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM JarAllocationPostings dp JOIN JarAllocations d ON d.Id = dp.JarAllocationId
                      WHERE d.Kind = 3 AND d.AccountId = a.AccountId AND dp.JarId = j.Id)
    GROUP BY j.Id, j.Name, a.AccountId)
SELECT l.Jar, acc.Name AS OldAccount, acc.OpeningDate, l.FirstEntryThere, l.LastEntryThere, l.StoredThere,
       (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM JarAllocationPostings p2
        JOIN JarAllocations a2 ON a2.Id = p2.JarAllocationId
        JOIN Transactions t2 ON t2.Id = a2.TransactionId
        WHERE p2.JarId = l.JarId AND a2.AccountId = l.AccountId AND t2.Type = 2
          AND (t2.DepartedOn IS NULL OR (t2.TransferAccountId = l.AccountId AND t2.ReceivedOn IS NULL))) AS TransferLegsInTransit
FROM Legacy l
JOIN Accounts acc ON acc.Id = l.AccountId
ORDER BY l.Jar;
```

After the deploy, the release record takes the backfill's Warning line from the Event Log ("Jar-departure backfill: recorded N departure(s) and wrote down M line payment(s)."). Its payment count counts each payment once, so it can be lower than the first query's rows.

**Result on production.** Christopher ran both queries (`/mnt/project-files/reviews/pr-276/pre-deploy-queries.sql`) on production on 29 September 2026 at 16:50 UTC, after #278's deploy, and both returned no rows. No jar has postings in an account it no longer names without a departure there, so the backfill has nothing to settle: its first start should log "recorded 0 departure(s) and wrote down 0 line payment(s)".

**Left for a follow-up PR (found by the second round).**
- **(R2-D1, decided by Christopher on 29 September 2026, 14:05 UTC: "Accept and settle")** A payment the old account really made before the move and entered late is accepted on the jar's line from that account, as rule 49 asks, instead of refused. When a departure from that account covers its date, it is stored as a draw on the jar there and `SettleAsync` shifts the departure, the same way a correction of an existing entry is settled (P51). A new payment from the new account dated before the move stays refused (his other answer the same minute).
- **(R2-P2-4, pre-existing)** "Paid from", the income prompt, Move money and a transfer's source jar accept a jar in its new account on a date before it arrived, so a past month's shelf reads wrong. The fix must exempt answers the edit form echoes back unchanged.
- **P3s:**
  1. R2-P3-3: the lazy legacy departure in `CoveringAsync` records the stored postings only, with no write-down, and the backfill never revisits that group; fold it into the backfill's routine.
  2. R2-P3-5: nothing serialises the backfill against an overlapping recycle.
  3. R2-P3-6 (pre-existing): a kept draw can be re-dated to before the jar arrived in that account. Closed by the R2-D1 follow-up below (its review P2-1) when the jar's earlier stretch is recorded.
  4. R2-P3-7: whether an arrival write-down stays off the jar depends on the order of edits (P2-E data only). With it, the known limit above: an arrival write-down re-split with a slice paid from another jar has its jar-line slice charged to the paid-from jar.
  5. R2-P3-8 (pre-existing): `FromJar` is false for draws kept in an account the jar has left. Fixed by the R2-D1 follow-up below.
  6. R2-P3-9: the P2-E fix must also drop a learned jar line when the payment is dated before the jar arrived in the paying account.
  7. R2-P3-10 (pre-existing): client display gaps (the picker on kept old-account draws, an unlabelled write-down on a jar line, past months' jar lines counted as non-jar lines).
  8. R2-P3-13: the keep branch's `Date <= since` bound and `GetJarPayments`' arrival clause have no test.
  9. R2-P3-14: sub-points round 1 listed but this log dropped: P3-5's lazy duplication, P3-6's rollback and 409 test gap, P3-8's `DashboardPage.tsx` comment, P3-3's `UpdateJarCommandHandler` comment, and P49's false P2-C sentence.

**Third round of the adversarial review of #276 (on `fd3d510`).** CI [run 36578450531](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36578450531) was green on `fd3d510`. The review confirmed the round 2 fixes, the 29 September cap (right whichever day #276 deploys, since the window closed at #275's deploy) and the pre-deploy query (read-only, and it matches the code), and found one must-fix and three small fixes for the same push. All are fixed here:

- **(R3-P2-1) A jar share on a transfer leg still in transit was left out of the departure, then posted after the jar left.** Taking the departure amount from the ledger (round 2) left out a share stored on a transfer leg the ledger hasn't posted: one whose money hasn't left the other account, or, in the old account as the destination, whose receipt isn't confirmed. When the leg was confirmed later, the share posted in the old account for good, and re-answering it is refused because the jar has left. The backfill now adds such shares to the ledger's reading, so the jar reads that share below zero in the old account while the leg is in transit and 0 once it posts. The ledger's reading still leaves out entries dated before the opening date (R2-P1-1). `P3_8_TheBackfill_SettlesWhatTheLedgerReads_NotAStoredSplitOnAnUnpostedTransferLeg`, which asserted 0 in transit, is rewritten as `P3_8_TheBackfill_SettlesJarSharesOnTransferLegsStillInTransit_SoNothingIsLeftOnceTheyPost`: a receipt-first transfer and one awaiting its receipt, 300 into Holiday between them; −300 after the backfill, and 0 once both legs are confirmed (300 on `fd3d510`).
- **(R3-P3-1) After a move away and back, re-dating an earlier payment into another month was still refused.** A date edit that changes the month re-runs the whole line's checks, which didn't get round 2's kept-draw exemption. The exemption is now worked out once for any date edit that keeps the account and passed to all three checks. Test: `P51_AfterTheJarMovesAwayAndBack_ItsEarlierPaymentsThereCanBeRedatedIntoTheNextMonth` (a split payment re-dated 28 August → 1 September and a whole one 30 August → 2 September, both acknowledged: 350 on 31 August, 250 on 1 September, 0 on 2 September and today).
- **(R3-P3-2) The pre-deploy query** left out groups with no candidate payment and counted a payment split across two jars twice. The group census above is added, and the text says how the counts differ.
- **(R3-P3-3) Docs:** R2-D1 is recorded as decided (the follow-up list above, P51 and JAR-116), and the known limit now says what happens: the jar-line slice is charged to the paid-from jar.
- **(R3-P3-5) Comment:** the no-opening-date branch holds the jar at nothing once an opening date on or before its first entry is set; the comment says so.

**Left for a follow-up PR (found by the third round).**
- **(R3-P3-4, pre-existing)** After a one-way move, a kept payment in the old account can't be re-split keeping its jar slice or re-dated across a month; both are refused with "can't be added to its line from any account", though P51 says such entries stay editable. `EnsureJarAccount` refuses on the account before it looks at the kept jars. It belongs with R2-D1's follow-up, together with undoing a kept payment re-lined off the jar's line (K3), after a one-way move or a round trip.

**Merged with `main` after #278 (W08).** `main` (`4b549fe`) was merged into this branch with a merge commit, so the reviews of `c366905` still apply to its own commits. Six files conflicted:
- `UpdateJarCommandHandler`: W08 passes the identity service to `JarAccounts.LoadDesignatedAsync` (rule 63), and this PR reads the ledger it loaded once. Both are kept.
- `UpdateTransactionCommandHandler`: this PR's arrival check on a date edit is kept. W08 removed the block that cleared a waiting row's flag when its amount changed (A5), and it stays removed.

Migration `JarArchivePeriodsAndArrival` (09:49:38) is older than `ForeignAmounts` (13:46:03), which production already has. EF applies every migration missing from the history, whatever its place in the order, and the two touch different tables: this one adds `JarArchivePeriods` and `Jars.FundingAccountSince`, with no data change. The model snapshot merged cleanly, and `has-pending-model-changes` reports none.

**The backfill on data `ForeignAmounts` rewrote.** The migration restates only rows with no account, which no account's ledger or line-payment query reads. On an account it keeps the amount, and either stores the budget figure beside it (`BudgetAmount`) or sets the row waiting (`FxPending`). The ledger, the backfill's write-down and its departure all read `Amount`, so neither changes what the backfill writes. New `P3_8_TheBackfill_WritesDownALinePaymentsOwnAmount_AfterForeignAmountsGaveItABudgetFigure` pins this: it fails if the backfill skips a waiting payment or writes down the budget figure. On production the backfill has nothing to settle anyway: Christopher's two queries returned no rows after #278's deploy (above).

**Validation (this machine: .NET SDK 10.0, no SQL Server or Docker).** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx`: 0 errors and 1 warning, CS8620 in W08's `TransactionConfiguration.cs`, a file this PR doesn't touch. ## W07 Transfer and import identity (rules 7–8, 50–51, 87–91; HANDOVER O1, P68–P73) — 29 September 2026

**Scope.** Christopher answered O1 on 29 September 2026 (Keep, Show, Edit, Returned, Fee; rules 87–91). The import review now offers every recorded transfer whose leg on the statement account is still open, whatever its date, nearest first, including transfers recorded in the app or by the income allocation without a bank reference: before, those were never candidates, so importing the statement that showed them added the movement a second time (P68). Confirming such a leg stores the bank's reference and date (rule 69) and moves no money again. A row whose bank reference the account already knows is skipped only when date, amount and direction match too; otherwise it is listed, flagged and unticked (rule 88). Transfers are edited in place (rule 89), can be marked returned (rule 90) and can carry an explicit fee on a budget line (rule 91). Same-day import rows are judged on the day's closing balance, credits first (P69). The direct one-shot import endpoints are removed (P70).

**Schema.** Migration `TransferReturnsFeesAndAllocationKeys`: `Transactions.ReturnedOn` (date), `BankEntry` (40, what the bank printed for an imported row the user has since changed), `ReturnBankReference` (140, unique per owner and account where not null), `AllocationKey` (100, indexed per owner where not null), `FeeForTransferId` (self-reference, `Restrict`, unique where not null); check constraint `CK_Transactions_TransferReturn` (a return only on a transfer that departed, was not received, on or after the departure); `JarAllocations.ReturnOfTransferId` (the returned transfer a `Return` jar operation belongs to; foreign key with no action, unique where not null; rollback deletes those operations first). Data: the income allocation's run key moves from `BankReference` to `AllocationKey` for every transfer whose reference starts `alloc:` (and back on rollback). Nothing else is rewritten; no existing transfer becomes returned or gains a fee, so no `Return` operation exists at deploy.

**Domain.** `TransferPostingDates` carries `ReturnedOn` (`Of(transaction)`), validated as the constraint says; `TransferLedger.Calculate` reports `ReturnedAmount`, so a returned transfer leaves nothing in transit. `ActualAccountLedger` adds the money coming back as a second movement on the source under the derived id `TransferLedger.ReturnLegId(transferId)`. `TransferStatus.Returned`.

**Application.** `StatementReferences` is the one definition shared by preview and commit: `KnownAsync` returns each known reference with the entry it recorded (date, amount including any fee, direction), `KnownReferences.Resolve` answers duplicate, new, or reused (stored under `{reference}~{8 hex of date|amount|direction}`, within 140 characters); `TransfersAsync`/`CandidateFor` list the candidates — a debit confirms an open departure (a larger debit is a fee candidate), a credit an open receipt (a smaller one is a fee candidate once the departure is known), and a credit on the source of a departed, unreceived transfer for the same amount is a **Return** candidate. `PreviewImport` lists candidates nearest date first (rule 87), an exact amount before a fee only on the same date, and badges one carrying the row's bank reference without moving it up, and marks `recordedDate` for a leg the app recorded. `CommitImport` confirms matches (a credit takes the bank's receipt date and amount; a debit the departure date), confirms returns (`returnOfTransferId`: `ReturnedOn`, `ReturnBankReference`, the destination's jar answer cleared), records a typed fee on a new debit transfer as its own row, holds a match whose fee would overdraw the source, and evaluates rows by date with credits first; the collision recovery covers the return-reference index and fee rows. New `UpdateTransfer` (`PUT /api/transactions/{id}/transfer`, allowed for Limited like transfers): accounts, amount, the three dates, payee, fee and fee line, jar answers; a statement-confirmed departure keeps its account, date and amount plus fee, a confirmed receipt its account, date and amount, a confirmed return its date; rule 74 is checked on every account before and after. `TransferFees` writes the fee row (expense on the source, dated the departure, on the chosen line or uncategorised, drawing from the chosen jar unless the line is a jar's). `CreateTransfer` takes `Fee`, `FeeBudgetItemId`, `AcknowledgeCrossMonth`. `UpdateTransaction`, `SplitTransaction` and `DeleteTransaction` refuse a fee row ("Edit the transfer"); deleting or re-typing a transfer removes its fee. The return leg posts to unallocated; `Jars/TransferReturns` stores a zero-sum `Return` jar operation (jar +share, unallocated −share, dated the return) when the return is recorded or its date, account or jar split changes, giving each jar its share of the departure if it is still on the account and not archived, and removes it when the return is undone or the transfer deleted. A change that leaves the return's amount, source and departure split as they were (a statement confirming a return marked by hand, a corrected date) keeps that decision and only moves the operation to the new date (`TransferReturns.SyncAsync`). `AllocationPlanner`/`AllocateIncome` read and write `AllocationKey`, and a re-run never replaces or withdraws a transfer a statement confirmed (departure or return). `ImportStatementCommand`, its handler and validator are deleted; `ImportStatementResult` (now with `Returned`) and `ImportHeldRow` move to `Imports/Models/ImportResult.cs`. The P57 privacy guard's source-leg fields include `ReturnedOn` and `ReturnBankReference`.

**Client.** Transactions page: **Edit transfer** on every transfer row opens the transfer form pre-filled (jar answers and fee included); **Returned on** shows while the transfer has departed and has no receipt; fields a statement confirmed are disabled with "Confirmed by the bank statement"; an optional fee and fee line on create and edit (the departure month's lines, with the cross-month confirmation); a **Returned** tag, "+ fee" under the amount, and a fee row badged "Fee for transfer" that takes only a line. Import page: return candidates ("Returned: transfer to … on …") that hide the counterparty and jar prompts, fee candidates with a fee line, a typed fee on an unmatched debit transfer, the recorded-date note and same-reference tag, reused-reference rows unticked with the earlier entry's details, and a **Returned** tile on the result. Account detail: the **Returned** tag. `e2e/mocks.ts` answers `PUT /transactions/:id/transfer` and carries fee, returned and reused-reference fixtures.

**Review fixes (adversarial review at `6070119`, `/mnt/project-files/reviews/w07/`).** Each P1/P2 was reproduced by the reviewer's probe, now a regression test. **P1-1:** a candidate whose other side is private to another login dereferenced the hidden account, so after Sam's "Move money" into Sam's private savings every preview of the joint statement by Chris failed; `StatementReferences.CandidateFor` names the hidden side "Private account" with no id (`TransferMatchCandidate.CounterpartyAccountId` is nullable), a commit item that matches may leave `TransferAccountId` empty (the counterparty comes from the transfer), and nothing that changes the private side (a credit's fee, a return, moving its guessed date) is offered. **P1-2:** the return leg's jars were judged from the jar's state today, so emptying and archiving (or moving) the jar after the money came back counted it twice; `JarLedgerReader` now gives a jar its share back only if it was on the account and not archived on the return date (its Departure operations and `ArchivedOn`). **P2-1:** correcting an imported row made the next import offer the bank's own entry as a reused reference; `UpdateTransaction` keeps what the bank printed (`Transactions.BankEntry`, `date|amount|C/D`, set the first time the date, amount or direction changes) and `KnownEntry.Matches` compares against it. **P2-2:** a statement departure later than the arrival the app guessed (or an arrival earlier than the recorded departure) now moves that app-recorded leg to the bank's date, with rule 74 checked on its account. **P2-3:** a statement-confirmed return keeps its account and amount as well as its date. **P2-4:** a return is offered and confirmed when the receipt was only app-recorded; confirming it clears the guessed receipt, with rule 74 on the destination. **P2-5:** `UpdateTransfer` locks every account the edit touches (`BeginLedgerWriteAsync(IEnumerable<Guid>)`, in id order). **P2-6/7/8 (client):** deleting a transfer drops its fee row from the register; a transfer edit saved before the jar ledgers load keeps the stored jar answers; a fee candidate carries the current fee's line (`FeeBudgetItemId`) and the review pre-fills it. **Built on the reviewer's recommendations while Christopher decides:** a fee confirmed at import must name its line (validator), and a fee is proposed only while it is at most 10% of what arrives (`StatementReferences.MaxProposedFeeShare`), exact-amount candidates first (P3-1).

**Review fixes, round 2 (adversarial review at `1f182dc`, `round2-review-1f182dc.md`).** **R2-P1-1, R2-P2-1, R2-P2-2:** round 1's fix still re-derived the return's jars on every read from `ArchivedOn` and departures, so archiving or moving the jar on the return day counted the money twice, a return imported after the jar was archived left money in the archived jar, and restoring a jar pulled an earlier return back into it. The return's jar postings are now stored when it is recorded (`TransferReturns`, `JarAllocationKind.Return`, `JarAllocations.ReturnOfTransferId`), from the jars as they stand then, like every other jar movement; the reader no longer looks at jar state for it. The import's return, `UpdateTransfer` (when the return date, account, amount or departure split changes), `UpdateTransaction`'s retype, `DeleteTransaction` and the allocation re-run's removal of a stale transfer keep or remove it. **R2-P2-3:** the rule 74 check on a match's or return's other account read the database only, so two matches in one import could together take that account below zero; `CommitImport` now keeps one running state for the import account and every other account a row touches, loaded once and judged row by row. **R2-D1 (Christopher, 29 September, "10% or 10"):** a fee is proposed up to the larger of 10% of what arrives and 10 (`MaxProposedFeeShare`, `MaxProposedFeeFloor`), so a bank's fixed charge on a small transfer is offered; candidates are listed same reference first, then nearest date (rule 87), an exact amount only breaking a tie (round 1 listed exact amounts first). **R2-P3-2:** `StatementReferences.Entry` and `Derive` format with the invariant culture.

**Review fixes, round 3 (adversarial review at `6e776a3`, `round3-review-6e776a3.md`).** **R3-P1-1:** a statement confirming a return the user had already marked by hand decided the jars again from the jars as they were at import, so a jar emptied and archived in between read −100 on a 1,000 account; `TransferReturns.SyncAsync` (used by the import and by `UpdateTransfer`) keeps a stored decision and only moves it to the new date unless the amount, the source or the departure split changed. **Candidate order:** the same-bank-reference sort key is dropped (it departed from rule 87); the "Same bank reference" badge stays.

**Review fixes, round 4 (adversarial review at `3233809`, `round4-review-3233809.md`).** **R4-P2-1:** a statement showing a transfer leaving and coming back (a bounced transfer) couldn't be imported in one go, because the commit counted each transfer once per import; it now counts each leg (departure, receipt, return), so one import confirms the departure and then the return. **R4-P2-2:** matching a returned transfer's departure at import to another jar left the return giving the money back to the first jar; the import's debit match now calls `TransferReturns.SyncAsync`, and a stored return is kept only while it agrees with the departure split as it is (every jar it credits drawn by the departure on the same source, for the amount it credits), so a change made by an earlier row or an earlier write is caught too.

**Follow-ups from the review (not in this change).** P3-2: matching and returning take no concurrency token and the import takes no ledger lock (pre-existing), so an edit and an import of one transfer can interleave, and a double-submitted match that adds a fee fails on `IX_Transactions_FeeForTransferId` with a 500 instead of being skipped. P3-3: the destination still counts a transfer's source reference as known once its receipt is recorded (the legacy fallback for rows from before receipt references), so when both banks print one reference, the destination's credit is skipped instead of confirming the receipt; the legacy rows can't be told apart from a receipt confirmed by hand, so this needs a marker. P3-4: an income-allocation re-run keeps an unchanged transfer even when it came back, so the member's savings read as funded; it needs a second transfer beside the returned one. P3-6: a fee following a later bank departure into another month stays on the old month's line without the cross-month question. P3-7: the transfer edit form keeps both amount and fee of a departure-confirmed transfer, stricter than the server (which accepts any split with the same total). P3-8: re-saving an unchanged transfer whose fee sits on another month's line asks the cross-month question each time. P3-10: pre-filled jar answers naming an archived jar are sent on save and refused with a confusing message. R2-P3-1: `CreateTransfer` and `UpdateTransfer` still accept a fee with no line, so a fee entered by hand can be uncategorised while one confirmed at import can't (rule 91 on every path). R2-P3-3: rows edited before this change have no `BankEntry` (what the bank printed can't be recovered), so importing their statement again still offers them as reused references; this is expected, not a regression. R3-P3-1: the ledger reader applies a `Return` operation without checking it against its transfer; it should count it as unreconciled when the transfer is no longer returned on that date from that account (today every writer keeps them in step). R3-P3-2: the fee floor is 10 in the transfer's currency, which is Christopher's "10 in the budget currency" only for accounts kept in the budget currency; to be converted with W08's rates when W07 merges main after #278. #276 merge check: whichever of W07 and #276 reaches main second checks that `JarDepartures.BackfillAsync` treats `Return` operations (no `TransactionId`) as ordinary jar history on the old account. R5-P3-1: a same-day bounce that the app recorded a day late has no return candidate until its debit is imported, because the return candidate compares the credit's date with the recorded departure date; importing the statement again offers it, and no money moves wrongly. R4-P3-1: a return marked by hand and then dated later by the bank, past the day its jar was emptied and archived, moves the jar's share to the bank's date, so the jar reads −100 on the days between (the archive day included); today's figures are right. (Across a move this no longer applies: a later date typed by hand is refused and a bank's later date decides the return again, M2-P2-1 below.) Options: keep the jar's share at the hand-marked date when the new date passes the jar's archive, or refuse that later date for the jar.

**Merged with W08 (#278).** W07 was built beside W08 and merged main after it. `CommitImport` no longer converts (W08 removed `FxRateResolver`): new rows in another currency wait through `ForeignAmounts.MarkWaiting`, and a transfer's fee row, which is spending in the transfer's currency, waits the same way on accounts kept in another currency (`TransferFees`; a confirmed figure stands until the fee's amount or currency changes); `ImportStatementResult` carries both `Returned` and `AwaitingAmount`. W07's proposals are renumbered P68–P73 after W08's P61–P67, and its test cases IMP-096–IMP-100 after W08's IMP-091–IMP-093. The migration was regenerated after `ForeignAmounts` with the same schema and data steps. The real-backend allocation journey (ALLOC-058) now finds the allocation transfers by type and payee and checks their `BankReference` is empty, since the `alloc:` key moved to `AllocationKey`, which the API doesn't return (`AllocationCoverageTests` pins its format). **R3-P3-2 (fee floor currency), not done:** Christopher's floor is 10 in the budget currency; the code compares against 10 in the transfer's currency. They differ only for a transfer between accounts kept in another currency, which W08 allows only for legacy data. Converting the floor would need a rate at preview, and W08 made the app apply no rate the user hasn't confirmed, so the floor stays in the account's currency for such accounts until Christopher decides otherwise (a fixed figure per currency would also do).

**Merge pass (M-P2-1).** A fee row resolved the budget currency without the signed-in user, so before a household's first budget month a GBP household read as EUR and its fee on a GBP account waited for an amount it already had. `TransferFees.ApplyAsync` now takes the identity service from all five callers (create and update transfer, both import paths, update transaction), as every other budget-currency lookup does; a test covers a GBP household's fee before its first month.

**Merged with #276.** `main` (`fce70a5`) was merged into this branch with a merge commit. Four files conflicted. `JarLedgerReader`: #276 moved the per-date read into `JarLedgerSource.At` over rows loaded once, so the return legs are now worked out once in its constructor and the Return operations' unallocated leg is added in `At`, as before. `UpdateTransactionCommandHandler`: #276's arrival check on a date edit runs first, then W07 keeps the bank's printed entry. This log and the TEST-PLAN keep both sides, #276's first, since it merged first, then the record of its release above. The migrations still sort `JarArchivePeriodsAndArrival`, `ForeignAmounts`, then this PR's, and the model has no pending changes. **#276's startup backfill and Return operations:** a Return operation has no transaction, so the backfill dates it on its own date (the return) and never counts it among transfer legs still to post; `HasUnpostedLegAsync` only looks at legs with a transaction. It gives back only to jars still on the account, so it can't create a moved jar's history with no departure by itself. When a jar moved before departures were recorded, the backfill settles what a return gave back there too, dated on the return (`TheJarDepartureBackfill_SettlesAReturnsShare_OnTheReturnDay`). No code change was needed.

**Second merge pass (M2-P2-1).** A Return operation's jar share did not go through #276's departure settling, so undoing, deleting, retyping or changing a returned transfer after its jar moved left the jar at −100 in the old account, and a return re-dated past the move read −100 on the days between. `TransferReturns` now settles every change to a Return operation on the jar's departure (`JarDepartures.SettleAsync`), as `JarMovements.ReplaceAsync` does for every other jar share: removing one (`ClearAsync`, also on re-deciding), adding one, and moving its date. A kept share can't move past the day its jar left: a return date typed by hand (`UpdateTransfer`) is refused on `ReturnedOn`, as #276 refuses any other entry of the jar's stretch dated after it left; a bank's date at import is a fact, so the return is decided again as of that date, when the jar wasn't there, and the money comes back unallocated. A return also gives a jar its share only when the jar was held in the account on the return date (`JarLedgerReader.HeldOn`), so a jar that arrived later doesn't take it.

**Test maintenance** is itemised in TEST-PLAN (W07 review). **Validation after the merges:** unit 1277, SQL integration 95, vitest 848, stubbed e2e 103 (UX-005 fails as it does on main), real-backend journeys 48.

**CI and release of [PR #279](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/279).** The re-check of its merge pass was clean on `657ff75` (no P1 or P2). Christopher ran nothing before merging: the migration's data step only moves `alloc:` keys from `BankReference` to `AllocationKey`, and nothing on screen changes at deploy. **Released.** PR #279 was squash-merged by Christopher as `7008019` on 29 September 2026 at 19:13:00 UTC. CI [run 36617655842](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36617655842) on `main` succeeded (19:13:04–19:20:48 UTC). Deploy [run 36617655879](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36617655879) succeeded: staging 19:14:25–19:14:58 UTC, then production, which needs approval, 19:15:03–19:15:36 UTC. `Deploy.ps1` polls `/health` and throws on failure, and both deploy steps succeeded, so migration **`TransferReturnsFeesAndAllocationKeys`** applied at API start on staging and production. Recorded by the R2-D1 follow-up (#280), which merged `main` after it.

## Jar moves: accept and settle (R2-D1; R2-P3-8, R3-P3-4, K3; HANDOVER P51) — 29 September 2026

**Decision.** On 29 September 2026 at 14:05 UTC Christopher chose "Accept and settle" (R2-D1, the review of PR #276): a payment the jar's old account really made before the move, entered after it, goes on the jar's line and the move re-settles like a P51 correction. His other answer the same minute stays as #276 built it: a payment on the jar's line from the new account dated before the move is refused.

**What changed.**
- **Where the jar was held (`JarStretches`, new).** The jar's departures, from every account, are the record of its moves: a departure closes the stretch the jar spent in that account, and the stretch began on the jar's previous departure from any account (the move day belongs to both accounts), or has no recorded start when there is none. `LeftOn(jar, account, date)` is the covering departure's day when a recorded stretch held the jar there on that date; `HeldIn(jar, date)` names the account that did, when that stretch's start is recorded too (review P3-1, below). One query loads a jar's departures, and none is made when every jar line of a write is held by the paying account on its date.
- **The check (`TransactionInvariants.EnsureJarAccount`).** A payment on a jar's line is accepted when the paying account holds the jar on that date (`JarLedgerReader.HeldOn`, unchanged), or when a recorded stretch held it there (new). This replaces the kept-draw exemption (`KeptDrawJarsAsync`, removed): a stored draw of an earlier stretch is covered by that stretch, and so is a new entry, an entry moved there from another account, an existing one put back on the jar's line, and a re-split or re-date within the stretch, across a month too. Since the stretch is checked before the account, R3-P3-4 (a one-way move's kept payment could not be re-split keeping its jar slice, or re-dated across a month) and K3 (a kept payment taken off the jar's line could not be put back) are fixed. A refusal names the account that held the jar on the payment's date when a stretch says so ("'Holiday' is the jar Holiday, which was held in A on 10 Aug 2026. Record this payment from A so it comes out of the jar, or put it on another line."); otherwise the #276 messages stand. The whole-line check, the slice check, the date-edit check (`EnsureJarLinesArrivedAsync`) and the import's batch check all read the stretches.
- **Storing it (`JarMovements.ApplyOutgoingAsync`).** A line whose jar a recorded stretch held in the paying account on the payment's date is kept on the jar there, as a stored draw from an earlier stretch already was, and `ReplaceAsync`'s `SettleAsync` shifts the covering departure, so the old account holds nothing of the jar from the day it left, and the difference leaves its unallocated money that day. `CreateTransaction` now calls it for every expense on a line (it stores nothing when the reader derives the same, as before), and `CommitImport` for every row on a jar line its account did not hold on the row's date, since the reader can't derive a draw on a jar held elsewhere. Deleting the payment, or moving it off the line or to another account, clears the draw and settles the departure back (unchanged code).
- **The old account's ledger must read it.** With no opening date the payment is refused ("A has no opening date yet. Set A's opening date first, or record this payment on another line."): stored, it would count once a date is set, but not if the date set is later than the payment, while its departure's share would (R2-P1-1's phantom). Dated before the opening date, it is accepted and nothing is stored, as the ledger counts no entry before an account opened.
- **(R2-P3-8) The jar's payments list** (`GetJarPayments`) lists a payment as from the jar when its stored answer draws from the jar in whichever account paid it, so the old account's kept draws, the late ones included, read as from the jar there. A payment from the jar's own account with no stored answer is read as before.

**Known limits.**
- A move records a departure only when the old account's ledger carries the jar (`JarDepartures.RecordAsync`, unchanged): a jar that left an account with nothing in it (no money, no payments on its lines) has no stretch there, so a late payment from that account stays refused ("can't be added to its line from any account"). Recording a departure on every move would have fixed that for future moves, but an account with any jar operation can then neither be deleted nor made private (rule 33), which would block removing an account a jar was created in by mistake.
- **A stretch's start can be too early, and in that corner Christopher's "Refuse" for the earlier move does not hold.** A stretch is taken to begin on the jar's previous recorded departure. Two cases make that earlier than the day the jar really arrived: the jar passed through an account it carried nothing in (no departure is recorded there, as above), or it was moved before #275 and the startup backfill dated its departure on the old account's last entry (review P3-4, below). A payment on the jar's line from the later account, dated in the gap before the jar arrived there, is then accepted and kept on the jar in that account. Example: Holiday is in A with nothing in it, moves to B on 10 Aug, then leaves B for C today; a payment from B dated 5 Aug is accepted, and Holiday reads −50 in B on 5 Aug. When the unrecorded stretch is the jar's first, the refusal no longer names the later account (P3-1): a first recorded stretch, whose start is never known, is not named. When the unrecorded stretch is in the middle of the jar's history, it still does (review round 2, R2-N1): after X→A on 1 Aug (recorded), A→B on 10 Aug (A carried nothing, so not recorded) and B→C today, a payment from A dated 5 Aug is refused with "which was held in B on 5 Aug 2026. Record this payment from B", and B accepts it. Only the arrival day on each departure can tell these apart. A durable fix stores, on each departure, the day the jar arrived in that account (its `FundingAccountSince` at the move). That needs a new column, so it is left for the backend final review.
- **The auto-categoriser (P2-E, still open)** puts a payment on a jar's line on both paths that run it, and both now keep it on the jar in the old account when a stretch covers its date: a manual entry (`CreateTransaction`) runs it before `ApplyOutgoingAsync`, and the statement import suggests the line at preview, so the committed row reaches `CommitImport` on the jar's line like any other. (Before #279 merged, the one-step `ImportStatement` categorised rows after creating them and left such a payment on the old account's unallocated money; W07 removed that path.)
- R2-P3-10 is unchanged: the add form still shows the "paid from" picker on a payment from the old account on the jar's line (the client knows only where the jar is now). Leaving it on "Unallocated" is right; naming a jar is refused ("This payment is already on a jar's line; it draws from that jar.").

**Deploy.** No migration and no backfill. The ledger reads stored data exactly as before, so no balance changes at deploy. The only difference on screen is R2-P3-8: a jar that has moved lists the payments it made in its old account before the move as from the jar (the "From this jar" label in a recurring jar's "Mark cycle paid" dialog). A W07 fee row kept in an old account shows the same label; such rows exist only for moves made since W07 went live (29 September 2026, 19:15 UTC).

**Review, round 1** (the adversarial review of PR #280, `reviews/pr-280/` in the project files).
- **(P2-1) Fixed: a kept payment re-dated within its month to before the jar arrived.** The jar moved X→A on 10 Aug and A→B today. A's 20 Aug payment on its line, kept on the jar, could be re-dated to 5 Aug, when X held the jar; the jar then read −50 in A on 7 Aug. Entering it on 5 Aug is refused, and so is a re-date across a month, so only the same-month edit got through. `ApplyOutgoingAsync` now refuses to re-date a kept share to before its recorded stretch began ("This entry moved money in Holiday while Holiday was held in this account, and another account held Holiday until 10 Aug 2026. It can't be dated before that."), as it already refused one dated after the jar left. This also closes #276's R2-P3-6 for draws kept at a move, when the earlier stretch is recorded. Only a changed date is checked, so a legacy draw dated before its backfilled stretch (P3-4) can still be edited in place.
- **(P3-1) Fixed: the refusal could name an account that never held the jar.** `HeldIn` now names an account only when its stretch's start is recorded. Otherwise, a payment from the account the jar is in now gets #276's message, and one from any other account is told "A payment dated before then goes on its line only from the account that held the jar that day. Record it from that account, or on another line." The account that accepts it is still reachable (it is the corner above). The app no longer steers the user into it when the unrecorded stretch is the jar's first, but still does when it is in the middle of the jar's history (R2-N1, below).
- **(P3-2) Corrected:** the auto-categoriser limit above now names the paths (and, after the merge below, no path leaves such a payment off the jar).
- **Follow-ups for the backend final review (not fixed here):**
  - **(P3-3, pre-existing, also on `main`)** Moving an account's opening date later leaves a moved jar negative in its old account. `UpdateAccountCommandHandler` checks only rule 74; the ledger then drops the entries dated before the new opening date, but the departure's settlement stays. Holiday then reads −300 in A today on `main` (−250 with a late payment), which breaks rule 2 and blocks archiving it. The same gap leaves a late payment accepted before the opening date (stored as nothing) off the jar if the opening date is later moved earlier.
  - **(P3-4, pre-existing, inferred from the code)** Jars moved before #275 have no arrival date: only `UpdateJarCommandHandler` sets `FundingAccountSince`, so it is null for them, and their departure was backfilled on the last day the old account's ledger touched the jar. R2-D1 then covers only dates up to that day, and a later-dated genuine old-account payment is refused ("Record this payment from B"). The stretch after it starts too early (the known limit above). And B, with no arrival date, holds the jar on every date (`HeldOn`), so it also accepts a payment on the jar's line dated before the move.

**Review, round 2** (re-check and merge pass on `b8d18a0`).
- **(R2-N2) Fixed: a kept share re-dated to before the old account's opening date stayed on the jar.** The ledger counts nothing before an account's opening date, but the departure's settlement of that share stayed, so the jar read the share back in the old account after the move: +50 for a late payment re-dated from 10 to 3 August when A opened on 5 August, or +300 for the payment the move itself kept. Its card was too high and archiving it was refused. `ApplyOutgoingAsync` now keeps no share dated before the account's opening date, neither a kept draw on the jar's line nor one "paid from" a departed jar (`keepsFrom`), as it already stored nothing for a new entry dated then; the departure settles back. The move's own kept draw did this on `main` too; this PR's late payments were a second way in. `TransferReturns.StaysInItsStretchesAsync` gets the same guard for a kept return settled on a departure: dated before the opening date, the return is decided again and gives that jar nothing (inferred from the code; a return then must follow a departure also dated before the account opened, so no test reaches it).
- **(R2-N1) Logged, not fixed:** the refusal can still name an account that didn't hold the jar when the unrecorded stretch is in the middle of its history. The P3-1 text above is narrowed to say so.
- **(R2-N3, pre-existing, for the backend final review; inferred from the code):** a statement row that changes a transfer's fee re-validates the fee's line on the bank's date (`TransferFees.ApplyAsync` → `ValidateWholeLineAsync`, then `ApplyOutgoingAsync`), so a fee on a moved jar's line whose bank date falls after the jar left is refused, where `FollowDateAsync` would decide it again. On `main` that fee line is refused anyway.

**Merged with `main` after #279 (W07).** `main` (`7008019`) was merged with a merge commit. Only DEVELOPMENT-LOG and TEST-PLAN conflicted; W07's sections come first, then this one. The code merged cleanly, and #280's import hooks (`stretches`, `DrawsFromLeftJar`) survive in the merged `CommitImport`. Three changes make W07's operations follow the stretches:
- **Returns follow R2-D1.** A returned transfer's share went back only to a jar held in the account on the return date in its current stretch (`HeldOn`), so a return dated before a move but recorded after it, or one in an earlier stretch of a jar that came back, went to the old account's unallocated money. `TransferReturns.RecordAsync` now also gives the share to a jar a recorded stretch held there that day, when the account's ledger reads the date, and settles it on that stretch's departure (it already called `SettleAsync`). Today's balances are the same either way; the old account's history now reads the jar holding the money until the move. The reviewer's #279 probe `M3_AReturnInAnEarlierStretch_OfAJarThatCameBack` now gives the jar its share.
- **A kept return stays in its stretch.** `SyncAsync` checked only that a kept return wasn't moved past the day its jar left. `StaysInItsStretchesAsync` also refuses a hand re-date (or re-decides a bank's date) to before the jar arrived: for a jar still in the account, one that falls in neither its current stretch nor a recorded earlier one; for a jar that left, one before that stretch began (the return-side P2-1). Message: "This return gave money back to Holiday while Holiday was held in this account, and another account held Holiday until 20 Sep 2026. It can't be dated before that."
- **A fee and a departure dated by the statement (the #280 reviewer's merge check 1).** When a statement moved a transfer's departure date, `TransferFees.FollowDeparture` re-dated the fee row, and the transfer's own leg moved with `DepartedOn`, without re-deciding their jar draws or re-settling a departure. A fee on the jar's line, or a departure paid from the jar, could then draw from the jar in an account it had left, and the jar read the draw back in that account between its departure and the bank's date. `JarMovements.FollowDateAsync` now re-decides a leg's stored draws on the bank's date: each keeps its jar when the account held the jar that day (current stretch, or a recorded one it has since left), and otherwise draws from unallocated money, as a return decided on a bank's date does (a bank's date is not refused). `CommitImport` calls it for the fee (`TransferFees.FollowDepartureAsync`) and for the departure leg when the row gave it no new jar answer. This was already so on `main` for draws kept at a move; R2-D1 adds late fees on the old account's jar line.
- **Merge check 2.** `BudgetItemIds` now yields a transfer row's `FeeBudgetItemId`, so the fee line's jar is loaded into `jarHomes`, but `DrawsFromLeftJar` is consulted only for rows that aren't transfers. The fee is validated and kept inside `TransferFees.ApplyAsync` (`ValidateWholeLineAsync` with its own stretch load, then `ApplyOutgoingAsync`).
- **Probes on the merged head.** The #279 reviewer's `W07MergeProbes` and `W07Merge2Probes` and this PR's review probes: 13 pass. Three fail as expected: `M_ATransferRetypedIntoAnExpense_OnAnAccountKeptInAnotherCurrency_Waits` (M-P3-1, `main`'s, for the backend final review) and the two `PC_` probes (P3-3 above, pre-existing).

**Tests.** New `JarMoveAcceptAndSettleTests` (12): a late payment from the old account (created, on the move day, and imported), its deletion, the refusals after the move and from other accounts, R3-P3-4, K3, no opening date, before the opening date, a stretch's start, and review round 1's P2-1 (a same-month re-date before the jar arrived) and P3-1 (no account named for a stretch whose start isn't recorded). With the merge of #279, four more (16), and round 2's R2-N2 (17): a return dated before the move and recorded after it, a return in an earlier stretch of a jar that came back, a kept return re-dated to while the jar was away, and a transfer and its fee dated by the statement after the jar left; then kept payments (on the jar's line, a late one, and one paid from the jar) re-dated before the old account opened. `P51_AfterTheJarMovesAwayAndBack_ItsEarlierPaymentsThereCanStillBeRedatedAndResplit` now expects the late payment accepted (it was the refusal R2-D1 overturns). New SQL test `A_late_payment_from_the_jars_old_account_is_kept_on_the_jar_and_resettles_its_move`. Removed: `TransactionInvariants.KeptDrawJarsAsync` and `EnsureJarArrived` (folded into `EnsureJarAccount`), and the `keptJarIds` parameters.

**Validation (this machine: .NET SDK 10.0, SQL Server 2022 in Docker).** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx`: no errors; the one warning is W08's CS8620 in `TransactionConfiguration.cs`. On the first head the unit suite had **1247 passed** (1237 on `main` and 10 new). Reverting each piece fails a test: the stretch branch in `ApplyOutgoingAsync` (5 tests), its opening-date bound, the no-opening-date refusal, the stretch's start (either comparison), the import's and the create's call to `ApplyOutgoingAsync`, the payments list's clause, and the refusal that names the account. After review round 1 and the merge of #279 the unit suite had **1293 passed** (1277 on `main` and 16 new) and the SQL integration suite **96 passed** (95 on `main` and 1 new). After round 2: unit **1294 passed**; the R2-N2 test fails with either of its two guards removed. Each round-1 and merge change fails a test when reverted: the stretch-start refusal (P2-1), naming only a recorded stretch start and the unnamed message (P3-1, two tests each), a return's share in an earlier stretch (two), the kept return's arrival check, and the date following for the departure and for the fee.

**CI and release of [PR #280](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/280).** Round 3 of its review was clean on `69dc78f` (19:53 UTC), and PR CI [run 36622030131](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36622030131) was green on it (all seven jobs). Christopher ran nothing before merging: there is no migration or backfill. **Released.** PR #280 was squash-merged by Christopher as `5e36d91dc28e87b73c73f8ce81db888598d8978e` on 29 September 2026 at 20:01:21 UTC. The push to `main` ran CI [run 36623371416](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36623371416), green (all seven jobs, 20:01:25–20:08:18 UTC), and Deploy [run 36623371422](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36623371422), green: staging 20:02:52–20:03:25 and production 20:03:28–20:03:58 UTC, and the production `/health` check passed.

## Final review: test suites (W14; Christopher's 27 September request) — 29 September 2026

**What changed.** Tests only; no application code. Every suite (server unit, SQL integration, screen tests, mocked Playwright, real-backend journeys, deploy helpers) was read for obsolete and redundant tests. 26 server unit tests, 2 SQL tests, 31 screen tests and 2 journeys were removed, each because another test at the same layer covers the same thing or because the behaviour it guarded no longer exists; a few groups that differed only in input values became theories. TEST-PLAN ("Final review: test-suite tidy") lists every removal with the test that keeps its coverage. Shared helpers replace copies: one in-memory database helper (76 copies), one current-user stub (75 copies across the two .NET projects) and one mailbox reader for the journeys (3 copies). Unused `using` lines went.

**Fixed along the way (tests that could not fail).** `RoleGating`'s "limited access cannot manage accounts" looked for a field that exists only inside the closed add dialog; it now checks the Add account button. The mocked Playwright People page never received its spending figures, because `mocks.ts` answered `/members/spending` while the page asks for `/members/spending/{year}/{month}`; the route now matches. `DashboardPage.test`'s "spent amount as read-only" asserted only the absence of labels nothing renders and was removed (DASH-031 checks the figure).

**Left as is on purpose.** The screen tests still repeat their API mock and render wrapper in about 48 files. Extracting a shared `src/test` module would touch the same page test files the screens review is changing now, so it is left for after that PR merges (done: "The screen tests share one harness" below). `deploy/tests/Test-DeployHelpers.ps1` tests four functions that all still exist and are used; nothing to remove.

**Deploy.** Nothing changes for users: no application code, migration or backfill.

**Release of [PR #281](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/281).** Christopher merged it at 22:45 UTC on 29 September (`b502a09`). Neither its CI run ([36641411563](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36641411563)) nor Deploy ([36641411497](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36641411497)) ran any step: from about 20:57 UTC on 29 September GitHub Actions gave this repository's jobs no runner, and a re-run at 08:29 UTC on 30 September failed the same way. #281 changed tests and docs only, so production is unaffected; the next Deploy that runs ships `main` with it.

**Release of [PR #284](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/284).** Christopher merged it at 08:35 UTC on 30 September (`1cb2b31`); it changes the CI and Deploy workflow files, the journey Playwright config and this log, no application code. No CI or Deploy ran for it: GitHub Actions gave this repository's jobs no runner (see #281's record above). Production is unaffected.

## Final review: the app's screens (client) — 29 September 2026

Christopher asked for a full review and clean-up once the HANDOVER work was done (27 September). The review was split three ways: server, screens and tests. This section is the screens part. Each screen was read against AGREED-RULES, the HANDOVER decisions, and the server code that decides the same thing. Only client code changed here. Server-side findings went to the server thread. The test-suite thread (#281) owns removing and consolidating tests; this PR changes tests only where the behaviour changed.

**Logged follow-ups closed.**
- **#279 P3-7:** a departure the bank confirmed fixes only amount plus fee. The edit form now lets the amount move and fills the fee with the rest of the bank's total. It refuses an amount above that total. The amount is still locked once a statement confirms the receipt or the return, as the server keeps it.
- **#279 P3-8:** a fee left on the line and date it already had re-saves without the cross-month question.
- **#279 P3-10:** an edit whose stored jar answer names an archived jar is no longer sent. It says: "This entry moved money in or out of Holiday, which is archived. Restore Holiday on the Jars page to change this entry." Sending would have been refused, or, once every jar in the account was archived, the answer would have been left out and the money returned to unallocated. This covers transactions, splits, transfer edits and confirming a transfer's missing departure or receipt, where a receipt can still be pledged to a jar archived while the money was in transit (review of #282, P2-1; with every jar in the account archived, sending no answer would have let the server keep the pledge). The server half, refusing to archive a jar an in-transit receipt names or dropping its share at the receipt, went to the server thread.
- **#277 P3-5 (client half):** People shows "—", not "Private account", for a savings id when the accounts could not be read. A deleted account's dangling id needs the server to clear it; that went to the server thread.
- **R2-P3-1 (client half):** the transfer form requires a budget line for a fee (rule 91), as the import already did.

**Money shown wrong.**
- The Transactions hero counted every month under the viewed month's label. It now counts the month the top bar shows. The register still lists every month and doesn't refetch; TX-011 is updated.
- A jar in an account whose balance is unavailable read €0.00 in the jar prompts and in Move money. It now reads "balance not available" (rule 67). `readLedgers` nulls these balances where the ledger is read.
- Reports: money labels use the budget currency while the month breakdown loads. "Saved this month" takes the latest month up to today, not a budget already made for next month. A month with no budget shows its Variance, which the year's total already counted (rule 41).
- Allocation's projected balance no longer drops for a share of zero or less (nothing moves for it).
- A purchase's refunded total is re-read after a refund is added, linked, unlinked, deleted or its converted amount confirmed.
- The jar form's schedule no longer reads €0.00 a month when the current month is skipped. It shows no schedule for a completed goal (rule 55). The skip dialog says a fixed-monthly jar keeps its amount and finishes a month later, and no longer claims €0.00 is left unplanned.
- The new-month note counts only jars still planning.

**Money entered wrong.**
- Editing an in-transit transfer dropped the destination's jar answer: the €100 moved into Holiday landed unallocated on arrival. The answer is now kept and editable while in transit, and new transfers can give it too.
- "Confirm receipt" and "Add departure date" start from the stored jar answers instead of showing "stays unallocated".
- An edit saved before the jar ledgers loaded dropped the stored answer. It is now sent back unchanged, as the transfer edit already did.
- Jar amounts that don't parse, or that add up to more than the amount, are refused instead of left out silently.
- A transfer whose two statements put the receipt before the departure can be edited. Only a changed date is checked, as on the server.
- Category pickers offer only the lines the server accepts for the direction (income on income lines; an expense or a refund on expense or savings lines).
- A past month's jar line is read-only. Typing an amount there cleared its skip and rewrote a finished month's plan (rule 46). Past and current months are decided in UTC, as on the server.
- The jar form offers only accounts in the budget currency (rules 63/85); a jar's existing account stays listed.
- An empty date is refused with a message instead of the server's conversion error.
- Allocation and People show the server's reason when a save is refused.

**Dead code and lint.** Removed: the unused household switcher and its test, five unused icons, the allocation-rule and split-method label maps, a never-called amount helper and its test, and the Vite starter assets. `useJarLedgers` now has one fetch path. `eslint` had 34 errors; it is clean now (one warning in `AuthContext`), and CI's frontend job runs `npm run lint`. The React Compiler rule `set-state-in-effect` is off because the app doesn't use the compiler. `only-export-components` is off for the context files, which keep a provider and its hook together.

**Sent to the server thread.**
- #277 P3-5: clear `PersonalSavingsAccountId` and the allocation source when an account is deleted.
- #277 P3-6: a prior in-transit transfer to an account that has since gone private hits the 403 guard during Allocate.
- Rules 52/64: `MemberIncome` splits unattributed income at 4 dp with the remainder to the last member.
- `UpdateBudgetItem` un-skips a jar line without re-planning.
- The jar ledger sends 0 for an unavailable balance.
- Import results count skipped and awaiting rows.
- Accepting an unchanged archived-jar answer.
- New jar lines always land in the first Savings group.

**Follow-ups (not in this PR).**
- A Savings line on the Budget page shows spending from the jar as "€X of €planned" progress. Rule 14 wants contributed and still-to-go, which the Jars page reads from `/jars`.
- Budget lines don't show the server's `RefundedAmount`.
- A split purchase can't be picked as a refund's purchase, though the server allows it.
- The import's "done" totals include skipped rows (needs the server change above).
- The import asks the cross-month question for a fee on its own month's line.
- Move money's destination list offers accounts in another currency (the server refuses them clearly).
- Account detail rows dated before the opening date look like movements.
- Reports don't show how many rows are waiting for a converted amount.
- The Accounts "Owed" tile can't be non-zero under rule 74.
- Duplicate split-row helpers in TransactionsPage and ImportPage.
- Once the server accepts an unchanged stored jar answer that names an archived jar (the server final review, #283), the P3-10 check above is stricter than the server on an edit that leaves the jar answer alone; relax it to refuse only a changed answer. (Done in this PR instead if #283 reaches `main` first.)

## CI minutes (PR #284) — 29 September 2026

- Pull-request CI runs are cancelled when a newer push supersedes them; runs on main are never cancelled and never share a group. Every job has a timeout (journey 15, backend/integration/frontend/a11y 10, security-scan 8, deploy-scripts 5, deploy and provision jobs 15). Journey's Playwright `globalTimeout` is 11 minutes so a hang still leaves a report. Release zips for staging are kept 1 day, journey failure reports 3.
- Deploy no longer runs for a push that changes only `docs/**` or `*.md`. **A release record for a docs-only merge must say "no Deploy run" (CI still runs on main).** Run Deploy by hand (`workflow_dispatch`) to force one.
- Decided against skipping CI jobs for docs-only pull requests: a job-level check costs a billed minute on every run and only 1 of 33 September merges was docs-only; a workflow-level `paths-ignore` would leave any required status check pending on such a PR.

## Final review: server code (rules 2, 31, 42–44, 46, 52, 64, 79–80, 91; HANDOVER W01, O3a) — 29 September 2026

**Scope.** The final review of the server (`src/AllotJar.Api`, `Application`, `Domain`, `Infrastructure`) and `tools/`, started from the backend follow-ups the W PRs logged. Each fix below was reproduced first and has a test that fails without it. No migration, no backfill and no endpoint change; no response shape changes.

**Release of [PR #282](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/282)** (the screens PR, recorded here as the next final-review PR to merge). Christopher squash-merged it as `e0367f2` on 30 September 2026 at 08:39 UTC. **No CI or Deploy ran for it:** GitHub Actions refused every job for the account from about 20:57 UTC on 29 September, so the push's runs failed within seconds before any job started, and Christopher told the threads on 30 September to ignore CI for now. Its review was clean on its final head. The releases of #281 and #284, merged before it, are recorded in the two sections above. #282 is client code only, so nothing on the server changes with it.

**Fixed.**
- **(#279 M-P3-1) A transfer on an account kept in another currency counted 1:1 once retyped into an expense.** A transfer never waits for a budget figure, so it has none; `UpdateTransaction` now marks such a row waiting when it becomes an income or expense (rules 42, 44). Test: `ForeignAmountsTests.A_transfer_on_an_account_kept_in_another_currency_waits_once_retyped_into_an_expense` (the #279 merge-pass probe; a budget-currency transfer still counts its amount).
- **(#280 P3-3) Moving an account's opening date past a jar's money left the jar below zero.** Moved later, the date dropped the money put in before it but kept the payments after it (or the departure that settled them), so Holiday read −300 in A for good (rule 2). `UpdateAccount` now reads the account's jars today with the old and the new date and refuses the change when any jar's balance there would differ: "Holiday would change: the jar entries between the old and new opening date would no longer count in A. Change or remove those entries first, or keep the opening date." Test: `JarMoveAcceptAndSettleTests.P3_3_MovingTheOldAccountsOpeningDatePastTheJarsMoney_IsRefused_AndAMoveThatDropsNoJarEntryIsAccepted` (the #280 review's `PC_` probes). P3-3's second half (a late payment stored as nothing before the opening date, which stays off the jar if the date is later moved earlier) doesn't change a jar's balance, so this check doesn't see it; logged below.
- **(#276 P2-E, R2-P3-9) The auto-categoriser put a learned jar line on a payment from any account.** A learned line that names a jar is now given, on a manual entry and as an import suggestion, only to a payment from the account that holds the jar on its date (`AutoCategorizer.JarLinesFitAsync`, `JarLedgerReader.HeldOn`): not from another account, not from the jar's account before the jar arrived, and not a late payment from an account the jar has left (the user puts that one on the line, R2-D1). Tests: `AutoCategorizationTests.Apply_PutsAPaymentOnAJarsLine_OnlyFromTheAccountHoldingTheJarThatDay`, `ImportPreview_SuggestsAJarsLine_OnlyForTheAccountHoldingTheJar`.
- **(#279 R2-P3-1) A fee entered by hand could have no budget line.** `TransferFees.ApplyAsync` now refuses a fee without a line on every path ("Choose the budget line the fee is spent from."), as rule 91 asks; the import already required one on screen. The transfer form on the Transactions page is made to require it by the screens thread. Tests: `TransferIdentityW07Tests.ASmallerCredit_MeansAFeeTakenOnTheWay_AndTheSourceStillLosesWhatLeftIt` now asserts the refusal on import and on create and then confirms the fee with its line (it asserted an uncategorised fee).
- **(#277 P3-3) Deleting a budget line or group could take another login's private entry off the budget.** The database sets the entry's line to null, out of sight of the private-account guard. `DeleteBudgetItem` and `DeleteBudgetCategory` now refuse when the line (or one of the group's lines) holds an entry, or a slice of one, from an account private to another login (`PrivateLineEntries`); its holder can still delete it. Test: `JointAccountsW13Tests.OthersCannotDeleteALineOrGroup_HoldingAPrivateAccountsEntry_ButItsHolderCan_P3_3`.
- **(#276 P2-D) A past month created after a restore planted a line for a jar archived all through it.** `CreateBudgetMonth` now leaves out, on a month that is over, a jar whose archive periods cover the whole month, both from the appended lines and from the copy. Test: `JarW05FollowUpTests.P2_D_APastMonthCreatedAfterARestore_PlantsNoLineForAJarArchivedAllThroughIt`.
- **(#276 R2-P2-4) "Paid from", the income prompt, Move money and a transfer's source jar accepted a jar in its new account on a date before it arrived.** `JarMovements` (every jar answer on an entry) and `ReallocateJarMoney` now refuse it: "Holiday has been in B only since 29 Sep 2026, so money dated before then can't go into or come out of it there. To put earlier money in Holiday, record it without the jar and use Move money dated 29 Sep 2026 or later." The wording holds for a jar that moved away and back too (#283 review P3-2: it said "moved to A" for a date when A held the jar the first time). An answer the edit form sends back unchanged (same jar, same amount, same date) on an entry that had already posted is not judged again, as the #276 reviewers asked; a raised share is (#283 review P3-1). A statement that re-dates a receipt is covered below. Tests: `JarMoveAcceptAndSettleTests.R2_P2_4_MoneyDatedBeforeTheJarArrived_CantGoIntoOrComeOutOfItInItsNewAccount` and `R2_P2_4_AnEditEchoingAStoredPaidFromAnswer_IsAccepted_AfterTheJarMovesAwayAndBack`.
- **(Rules 52/64, found by the screens thread) Unattributed income was shared at four decimals, the remainder to the last person.** 100 across three read 33.3333, 33.3333 and 33.3334; 0.02 across four read 0.005 each. `MemberIncome.EqualShares` now shares whole cents, the leftover cents one each to the first people in display order (0.02 across four: 0.01, 0.01, 0, 0). Test: `MemberFiguresRule28Tests.TheUnattributedIncome_IsSharedInWholeCents_LeftoversToTheFirstInDisplayOrder_Rules52And64` (three cases).
- **(W07 P3-10) An edit that sent back an archived jar's stored share unchanged was refused.** Renaming an old income that put 50 into a since-archived jar, or a payment paid from it, is now accepted when the share is the one stored; a different amount is still refused, and so is a pledge whose receipt posts only now (next item). Test: `JarW05FollowUpTests.W07_P3_10_AnEditSendingBackAnArchivedJarsStoredShareUnchanged_IsAccepted`.
- **(#277 P3-5) A deleted account stayed as a person's savings account and the allocation's source.** Both are ids with no foreign key, so the People page read the deleted account as "Private account". `DeleteAccount` now clears them. Existing dangling ids are not cleared at deploy (that would change what the People page shows at deploy); logged below. Test: `JointAccountsW13Tests.DeletingAnAccount_ClearsItAsAPersonsSavingsAccountAndTheAllocationsSource_P3_5`.
- **(#282 review P2-1, server half; #283 review P2-1) Money on its way to a jar could land in it after it was archived.** Archiving checked only today's balance, and a transfer's answer while the money is in transit counts only once the receipt posts, so a jar could be archived with 100 pledged to it; confirming the receipt (by hand without an answer, or from a statement) then put the 100 into the archived jar (rule 26). This PR's echo exemption also let the same answer through when the receipt was confirmed on its departure day. Two changes, chosen together: `ArchiveJar` now refuses while an entry that names the jar hasn't reached its account by today ("Holiday has entries that haven't reached the account yet: 100 in from Everyday (not received yet). Archive it once they have and its balance is zero, or take Holiday out of them first."; a transfer is named by the account at its other end, and a receipt imported first reads "waiting for its departure", #283 round 2 P3-2), which keeps rule 26's "archive at zero" true of money on its way; and a receipt that posts now is judged like a new answer, while one that keeps its stored answer (`ConfirmTransferLegs` without an answer, a statement match) sends a share for an archived jar to the account's unallocated funds (`JarMovements.FollowReceiptAsync`); so does a receipt imported first whose departure the other account's statement supplies (#283 round 2 P3-1). The second only matters for pledges stored before this change. Tests: `JarMoveAcceptAndSettleTests.ArchivingAJar_IsRefusedWhileATransferPledgedToItIsInTransit_AndAcceptedOnceTheMoneyArrivedAndMovedOut` and `AReceiptPledgedToAJarArchivedMeanwhile_IsRefusedWhenTheAnswerIsSentAgain_AndGoesToUnallocatedWhenItIsKept` (by hand and by statement) and `AReceiptHeldForItsDeparture_PostsNothingIntoAJarArchivedMeanwhile_WhenTheOtherStatementSuppliesTheDeparture_R2_P3_1`.
- **(#283 review P2-2, the R2-P2-4 gap) A statement that re-dated a receipt kept a jar share dated before the jar arrived.** Holiday read 600 for 300 on those days. A statement match that moves a receipt's date without giving an answer now re-decides its shares with `FollowReceiptAsync`: a jar the account didn't hold on the bank's date gets nothing there, and its share goes to unallocated for the user to move (Move money dated on or after the arrival). Test: `AStatementThatReDatesAReceiptToBeforeTheJarArrived_SendsItsJarShareToUnallocated_P2_2`.

**Dead code removed.** `tools/AllotJar.Importer`, the one-off workbook importer: it is outside the solution and CI and no longer compiles against the domain (HANDOVER O6). It is removed in its own commit, so it can be restored from that commit's parent. `JarMovements.BacksJarsAsync`, which nothing calls. The docs that named the importer (`docs/testing/cases/13-jars.md`, `docs/testing/maps/import.md`, the integration project's comment) say it was removed.

**HANDOVER.** The W01 row is marked implemented (J1/J2 closed by #254, #255 and #258–#261; O1, O3a and O5 answered). O3a was checked against the code: registration seeds nothing, the templates carry no savings lines, and the jar form is the only path that creates a jar, with its account required (`Jar.FundingAccountId` is not nullable); it is marked resolved by rules 73 and 81. The register's intro now counts W01–W08, O6 says the importer is removed, O12 says the reset was performed on 26 September 2026, step 3 names W07, and the status line counts the PRs through #280.
