**Waiting on Christopher (asked in the thread on 29 September 2026).** R4-P3-1 (a hand-marked return the bank dates later, after its jar was emptied and archived: keep the jar's share at the marked date, or refuse the later date); R3-P3-2 (the import's fee floor of 10 is checked in the transfer's currency, which differs from the budget currency only on older accounts kept in another currency); #278 Q4 (refunds that exceed their purchase after W08's restating: leave or cap); P59 (an owner-side erase of an account private to a removed login). Each keeps its current behaviour until he answers.

**Logged, not fixed here.**
- #276 P2-C (a jar that never had an entry in its old account reads Unavailable on months before its new account opened) and #280's known limit (no stretch where the jar carried nothing): recording a departure on every move would block deleting or privatising any account a jar ever passed through (rule 33). #276 H4 / #280 P3-4 and R2-N1 (moves before #275 have no arrival date; a refusal can name an account that didn't hold the jar when an unrecorded stretch sits mid-history): the durable fix is an arrival-date column on each departure, a migration with a backfill that would need a pre-deploy census, not worth it pre-launch with no such moves to repair.
- #280 P3-3's second half (above); #280 R2-N3 (a statement that changes a transfer's fee re-checks the fee's line on the bank's date and can be refused, inferred).
- #279: P3-2 (no ledger lock on the import; a double-submitted fee match fails with a 500), P3-3 (the destination's legacy source-reference fallback needs a marker), P3-4, P3-6, P3-8, R3-P3-1 (the reader doesn't check a Return against its transfer; every writer keeps them in step), R5-P3-1.
- #278: the suggestion cache stores Frankfurter's latest published rate under the date asked for, and has no size bound (P3-2; a suggestion the user confirms, never money by itself); R2-P3-1 (a row taken off an account kept in another currency keeps its budget figure); P3-5 (`/auth/me` mixes the viewed household's budget currency with the login's own lock).
- #277: R2-P3-1 (inherent to P20 across logins); P3-6 (`AllocateIncome` replacing an earlier in-transit transfer into what is now another login's private account meets the guard's 403 rather than a validation message naming the person).
- From the screens thread: a jar line's planned amount can still be typed on a month that is over, and typing an amount on a skipped jar line clears the skip without re-planning later months (W04 chose the latter deliberately, test `Rule61_PlanningASkippedMonthAgain_…`; the screens thread makes past jar lines read-only); `JarAccountLedgerDto.Balance` is 0 rather than null when the account's balance is unavailable (the client reads `accountBalance === null`); `CommitImport` returns counts that include rows skipped as duplicates and rows awaiting an amount; `SavingsLines` always adds a new jar line to the month's first Savings group; dangling savings-account ids from accounts deleted before this change.
- #276 P3s 1–9 and R2-P3-3/-5/-7/-10/-13/-14 as that section lists them (performance, backfill serialisation, client-only and doc items).
- #283 review P3-4 (inferred): a transfer fee written without a line between #279 and this PR can be edited now only by giving it a line, and a fee dated in a month with no budget month can't be given one, so removing the fee is the only edit left. Nothing changes at deploy and production likely has none.
- **The screens' P3-10 check (#282) is stricter than the server.** With #283 the server accepts an edit that sends an archived jar's stored share back unchanged (same jar, same amount, an entry that had already posted), but the client still refuses any edit whose stored answer names an archived jar, with "Restore Holiday on the Jars page to change this entry" (`archivedJarNamed` in `TransactionsPage.tsx`, four sites: transaction edit, transfer edit, confirming a leg, split). #282 logged relaxing it "if #283 reaches `main` first", but #282 merged first. Not done here: relaxing it needs the client to compare each sent share with the stored one at four sites and to keep refusing the cases the server judges as new (a receipt that posts only now, a re-dated entry), which duplicates server rules on the client. The stricter client only blocks an edit on an entry whose jar was archived later, and "restore the jar, then edit" always works, so it is left for the screens thread to relax with the server's answer if Christopher wants it.
- The archive check reads the jar's current account only: a transfer still in transit to an account the jar has since left, pledged to it there, doesn't block archiving (the old account's departure settles that account's shares, rule 80; inferred, not run).

**Test maintenance.** 17 new tests (20 cases), two rewritten (`ASmallerCredit_…` above; `ALegConfirmedByAStatement_…` now gives its fee a line) and one fee test given a line. Removed: `ForeignAmountsTests.A_transfer_fee_before_the_first_month_counts_in_the_currency_chosen_at_sign_up` (review M-P2-1 of #279): a fee now needs a budget line and a line needs a budget month, so a fee before the household's first month can't be recorded; the owner's-currency path it covered is still covered by `Without_a_budget_month_the_owners_currency_decides_what_waits_not_EUR`. `R2_P2_4_MoneyDatedBeforeTheJarArrived_…` asserts the new wording. The rest of the suite's tidy-up is the test-suite thread's (#281).

**Deploy.** No migration and no backfill; stored data is read as before, so no balance changes at deploy. One figure changes on screen at deploy: where the unattributed income doesn't divide into whole cents between the people, the People and Allocation pages show cents (100 across three: 33.34, 33.33, 33.33 instead of 33.3333, 33.3333, 33.3334), as rule 64 asks. Everything else applies to new writes only.

**Validation (this machine: .NET SDK 10.0, SQL Server 2022 in Docker).** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx`: 0 errors; the one warning is W08's CS8620 in `TransactionConfiguration.cs` (fixing it changes the model snapshot, so it is left). Unit suite **1288 passed** after merging main with #281 and #284 (1269 on `main`, 17 new tests with 20 cases, 1 removed). Each fix was reverted on its own and its new test failed. SQL integration suite (SQL Server 2022 in Docker) **94 passed** after merging main (#281 removed two SQL tests; 96 before); `PrivateAccountsSqlTests` now also checks that another login can't delete the line a private entry sits on, so the new query is run against SQL Server.

## Final review: the screen tests share one harness (W14; the extraction #281 left for later) — 30 September 2026

**What changed.** Tests only; no application code. Each of the 46 screen test files that mock the API carried its own copy of the same three things: the `vi.hoisted` spies for `api.get/put/post/delete`, the `vi.mock('../lib/api')` factory that spreads the real module and stubs `api`, `getToken` and `setToken`, and a `renderPage()` that wraps the page in `MemoryRouter` and `AuthProvider` (with `MonthProvider`, `HouseholdProvider` or `FeatureProvider` where the page needs one). Two modules now hold them:
- `client/src/test/apiMock.ts`: `mockGet`, `mockPut`, `mockPost`, `mockDelete`, `resetApiMocks()` and `apiModule(importOriginal, overrides)`. A file mocks the API in one line, `vi.mock('../lib/api', async (importOriginal) => (await import('../test/apiMock')).apiModule(importOriginal))`, and imports the spies it drives. The factory imports the module lazily because `vi.mock` is hoisted above the file's imports; Vitest gives each file its own module registry, so the spies are still per file.
- `client/src/test/render.tsx`: `renderWithProviders(ui, { route, features, household, month })`, nested as `main.tsx` and `App.tsx` nest them (router > auth > features > household > month).

The 46 files lost 1,351 lines and gained 564 (82 of them the two modules). What each file still says for itself stays in the file: a signed-out screen's `getToken: () => null` and `refreshSession`, the import page's `previewImport`/`commitImport` spies, the pinned month or household of the Allocation, People and Dashboard files, the staging flag. No test body changed. `SharingReviewBanner.test` (no router) and `ConfirmEmailPage.catalog.test` (no `AuthProvider`; it mocks `useAuth`) keep their own render call and use only the API module. `api.test.ts` and the `AuthContext` tests mock nothing, and `App.catalog.test.tsx` mocks `./lib/api` together with every page, so those three are unchanged.

**Two small differences, on purpose.** Under the shared module `api` always has all four methods, where some files stubbed only the two they used; a call a page should not make now answers `undefined` instead of throwing, and the tests that guard those cases assert `not.toHaveBeenCalled()` on the spy, so they still catch it. `resetApiMocks()` resets all four spies (and `mockSetToken`, the `setToken` stub, after the review's P3-3) where a file reset only the ones it declared; nothing set a spy up outside `beforeEach`, so no answer is lost. The review's round 1 also found three tests that still built the provider tree by hand inside the test body (P3-1); they call `renderWithProviders` now.

**Found along the way.** `tsconfig.app.json` excludes `src/**/*.test.tsx` and `src/test`, so `tsc` never type-checks the tests and `npm run lint` has no type-aware rule: a missing import in a test shows up only when Vitest runs the file. Follow-up: a `tsconfig.test.json` (or including the tests in the app config's check) run by `npm run lint` or CI's frontend job. It is not only a config change: the #285 review (P3-4) type-checked the tests with a probe config and found 48 pre-existing errors in 13 files, the same 48 on `main` and none in the two new modules; mostly fixture objects missing DTO fields (`BudgetItemDto`, `JarDto`) in the Dashboard, Jars and Transactions files and `budgetModel.test.ts`, two `Element` vs `HTMLElement`, three `waitFor` calls with a fourth argument, and `src/test/setup.ts:45` under `erasableSyntaxOnly`; those are fixed first, then the check can gate. A second follow-up (P3-2): `renderWithProviders` builds its tree inline, so `PeoplePage.catalog.test` and `AllocationPage.catalog.test` keep a copy of the router-and-auth tree in their `rerender` calls, which must match the helper's or React remounts the providers; building the helper through Testing Library's `wrapper` option would let those two sites call `rerender(<PeoplePage />)`. Fixture builders are still copied within page families, about 290 lines in all: the Import page's `csvPreview`, `preview`, `previewWithTransfer`, `accounts`, `members` and `committed` (three files each), the Jars files' `jar`, `schedule`, `ledger`, `month` and `me`, Allocation's `profile`, ConsentModal's and AccountPage's `me`/`ownerMe`, and `axiosError` in three sign-in files. Left for a later pass; each family would get one fixture module next to its tests. In the full parallel run, `AccountsPage.test`'s "adds an account you hold alone as private, and a joint one as shared" once logged React's "two children with the same key, `a9`" from the Accounts list (the new account rendered twice for a moment); it doesn't show when the file runs alone and is not a test failure, but it points at the list's keys on the screen, so it is noted for the screens thread.

**Deploy.** Nothing changes for users: no application code, migration or backfill.

**Validation (this machine).** After merging `main` at `a8d175a` (#283): `npx vitest run` **823 passed** (64 files; 823 on `main` at `e0367f2`, no test added or removed); `npx eslint src` clean apart from the pre-existing `AuthContext` warning; `npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` clean (it does not see the tests, above). No CI run, per Christopher's "Ignore CI jobs for now" (30 September).

**Release of [PR #283](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/283)** (the server final review, recorded here as the next PR to merge). Christopher merged it as `a8d175a` at 08:54 UTC on 30 September. **No CI or Deploy ran for it:** its CI run ([36692632609](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36692632609)) and Deploy run ([36692632451](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36692632451)) failed without starting a job, as every run has since about 20:57 UTC on 29 September. Production still runs #280; the next Deploy that runs ships `main` with #281 to #283.

## Follow-up pass 1: the screens' figures (rules 14, 39, 40, 63, 74, 85; the #282 follow-ups) — 30 September 2026

Christopher approved the follow-up pass on 30 September ("go", 12:29 UTC) after the final review; the ranked plan is in the project's `plans/2026-09-30-next-steps.md`. This is its first PR: the screen items the #282 review logged, which show a wrong or incomplete figure or block an edit the server accepts. Client code, one additive server change (the import result names its skipped rows), and the tests that go with them. PR 2 (transfers, import and currency, with his four answers) is built in parallel; PR 3 (tests) follows.

**Money shown wrong.**
- **The Budget page's Savings line (rule 14).** A jar's line read "€30.00 of €125.00", where the 30 was spending paid *from* the jar's line, as an expense line reads. Rule 14 wants the plan, what went into the jar this month and what is still to go, which the Jars page already reads from `/jars`. `SavingsGroup` now takes the month's jars (the Dashboard's shelf read) and each line reads "€50.00 in of €125.00 · €75.00 to go", with the funding bar filling to what went in; the group header adds up what went in of what was planned, and shows only the plan when any of its lines' jars is missing from the read (review P3-3; a total counting that jar as 0 would read as complete). While the jars are loading or could not be read, a line shows only "plan €125.00" (the shelf says the read failed). Spending from a jar moves its Available figure and its plan, not what went in. **The figure itself (review P2-3):** `JarDto.ContributedThisMonth` was derived from two balances (`max(0, balance now − min(balance entering the month, balance now))`), so a bill paid from the jar was netted off what was contributed, and once the jar held less than it entered the month with nothing read as contributed at all (125 in and 30 paid out read "€95.00 in · €30.00 to go"; 125 in and 225 paid out read "€0.00 in"). The Jars page had read the same figure since W02. `JarLedgerSource.ContributionsBetween` now sums what was put into each jar over the month from the postings the ledger already loads — allocations and moves in, an income's or receipt's jar share — net of what was moved back out to unallocated money or another jar in the same month (a contribution undone: the jar emptied to travel between accounts still reads 0), never of what was spent or transferred from it; a returned transfer's share and a jar's departure (rule 80) count as nothing new in. `JarFigures` passes it to `ToDto` (`contributedThisMonth`; `RemainingThisMonth = max(0, planned − contributed)`), and `SavingsLines.CancelPlansAsync` cuts the current month's line to the same figure. The planning balance (rule 13, W03) is unchanged: a bill paid from the jar still counts at once in the plan. Tests: `JarContributionsRule14Tests` (a bill paid from the jar, the whole contribution spent in the month, money moved back out, the date range). Tests: `DashboardPage.jars.test` "reads a savings line as contributed and still to go from the jar" and "shows only the plan … while the jars could not be read".
- **Refunds on a budget line (rule 39).** The server has sent `RefundedAmount` since W12 and no line showed it, so "€100.00 of €90.00" after a refund of 20 read as a mistake. An expense line now adds "· €20.00 refunded" (with the explanation in its tooltip) when anything was refunded against it; `ItemVM` carries `refundedMinor`. A savings line shows contributions (above), not spending, so it has no refund note. Test: `DashboardPage.catalog.test` "says how much was refunded against an expense line".
- **Reports and the rows waiting for an amount.** A transaction in another currency waits for its amount in the budget currency and counts in no report, and the Reports page said nothing, so a total silently left it out. The page now says, under the hero, how many such rows the six-month window holds ("3 transactions in these months are in another currency, waiting for their amount in EUR, and not counted in these figures. Enter them on Transactions.") and, in Spending by category, how many the chosen month holds; nothing shows when there are none. The annual overview has no such count: `AnnualMonthDto` doesn't carry one (a later server change if wanted). Tests: `ReportsPage.catalog.test` "counts the rows waiting for a budget-currency amount" and "says nothing about waiting rows when there are none".
- **The import's done screen.** Its Money in / Money out summed every row the review sent, including the rows the server skipped as already imported (a re-import of the same file read as money moved twice). `CommitImport` now returns `SkippedReferences`, the skipped rows by the reference the request sent (the reference the row was sent under, not the derived one a reused reference gets, and for a row dropped in the concurrent-commit recovery the same), and the screen leaves those rows out of the sums. It also shows a "Waiting for EUR amount" count from the result's `AwaitingAmount` (sent since W08, never shown) with a line saying those rows count nowhere until their amount is entered on Transactions. Tests: `ImportPage.test` "leaves a row the server skipped out of the money sums and counts the rows waiting for an amount"; `ImportPreviewCommitTests.Commit_IsIdempotent_OnResubmit` asserts the references.
- **Account detail rows before the opening date (rules 40/74).** A row dated before the account's opening balance looked like a movement. It now carries a "Before opening" tag (tooltip: the opening balance already includes it; it doesn't change the balance) and its amount is struck through in grey. The date judged is the leg this account posts, as the ledger posts it (review P2-2): a transfer leaves its source on its departure and reaches its destination on its receipt (a receipt not yet confirmed is pending, never history), and one that left before the opening date but came back on or after it moved the balance on its return; the Date column shows that leg's date. Tests: `AccountDetailPage.test` "marks rows dated before the opening date as history only" and "judges a transfer against the opening date by this account's own leg".
- **The Accounts "Owed" tile.** Under rule 74 no account is ever below zero, so the tile could only read €0.00; it is removed and the hero keeps Total balance and Available. ACCT-001/002 and their catalog entries (12-accounts-reconciliation, 11-transfers, 24-currency-locale) now say so; a legacy negative balance still colours its card and stays out of Available.

**Edits the server accepts but the screen refused.**
- **The archived-jar check (#282 P3-10, relaxed after #283).** Since #283 the server accepts an edit that sends an archived jar's stored share back unchanged (same jar, same amount, an entry that keeps its date and had posted) as the entry's history, and refuses a jar newly named, a changed share, or one on a re-dated entry or a leg that posts only now. The client refused every edit whose stored answer named an archived jar. It now stops only what the server would: `archivedJarNewlyNamed` (`lib/jarLedgers.ts`) compares each share the edit sends with the entry's stored shares (`storedSharesOf`) and with whether the entry keeps its date, account and (for a transfer) amount and fee, at the three edit sites: the transaction edit (an income's jar amounts, or an expense's paid-from share, which is the part of the payment on lines that are not jars), the transfer edit (the source's draw, and the destination's answer once the receipt has posted) and the split editor. Confirming a missing leg still refuses an archived jar, as the server does, because that leg posts now. So that the stored share can be seen and sent back unchanged, the edit prompts now list an archived jar the stored answer names ("Holiday (archived)") after the account's live jars (`jarsForAnswer`); a new entry never sees it. Tests: `jarLedgers.test` "archivedJarNewlyNamed"; `TransactionsPage.catalog.test` "sends an edit that leaves an archived jar's stored share unchanged" and "explains, without sending, an edit that changes the share drawn from an archived jar". The edit prompts list the jars the entry's stored answer names (`storedJarIds`), not the current pick, so an archived jar stays offered after moving off it and can be picked back (review P3-2; test "keeps an archived jar's stored option offered after moving off it").
- **A split purchase as a refund's purchase.** The refund picker left out split purchases, which `RefundRules` accepts; it lists them now. A split purchase has no one line for the refund to follow (the server records such a refund unassigned; review P3-1), so the add modal's default option reads "Choose a category (the purchase is split)" and the save waits for one; the register's "Refund of" picker says the refund is recorded unassigned and its category is picked on the row. Tests: TX-099 lists the split "Ikea" purchase; "needs a category for a refund of a split purchase".
- **Move money's destinations (rules 63/85).** The list offered accounts kept in another currency, which the server refuses; they are left out (the jar's own account is always listed). Test: `JarsPage.move.test` "leaves an account in another currency out of the destinations".
- **The import's cross-month question for a fee (#279 P3-8 on the import; review P2-1).** A matched transfer's fee left on the line the recorded transfer already gave it was asked the cross-month question whenever the departure fell outside the loaded month. The first cut of this PR asked it for none of those, which the server refuses when the bank's departure date lands in another month than that line (every commit that carries a fee re-validates the line against the departure, and #287 adds the same check when a statement moves a recorded departure into another month). The screen now judges the fee as the server does: the fee's date (the bank's departure — for a credit, the recorded departure, or the credit's date when that is earlier and pulls the departure back) against its line's own month; the loaded month's lines are known, and `TransferMatchCandidate` now carries `FeeBudgetItemMonth` (the first day of the recorded fee line's month) for the line a match pre-fills. And a `crossMonth` 400 from the commit opens the same question with the server's reason instead of a dead-end banner; "Import anyway" re-sends with every row that names a line acknowledged. `crossMonthError` moved to `lib/serverErrors.ts`, shared with Transactions. Tests: `ImportPage.test` "asks the cross-month question for a fee only when its date leaves its line's month" (the May line, the June line, a recorded June line, a credit that pulls the departure back) and "turns the server's cross-month refusal into the question and re-sends acknowledged"; `TransferIdentityW07ReviewTests` asserts the candidate's month.

**Tidy.** The three copies of the split-row helpers (the register editor, the add modal and the import review each rebuilt "two blank slices", "patch a slice", "add a slice", "remove a slice, keep two") are one module, `client/src/lib/splitRows.ts` (`startSplitRows`, `patchSplitRow`, `appendSplitRow`, `removeSplitRowAt`, with `splitRows.test.ts`); the import's `SplitDraft` is the shared `SplitRowDraft`. The duplicate key "a9" the #285 run once logged came from the test posting the same id twice; the Accounts list now replaces a row that answers with an id it already holds instead of listing it twice, and the test gives its second account its own id.

**Follow-ups.** The contributed figure nets only reallocation postings (money moved back out within the account), so a jar emptied by a *transfer* to another account and refilled by a reallocation at its new account reads its whole refill as contributed that month, while the same move within one account reads 0 (review round 2, P3). Telling the refill of a moved jar from a contribution needs the move itself recorded (rule 80's Departure carries no amount), so it is left for the jar-move work.

**Left as it was.** The annual overview's waiting-row count (above). The Available tile now repeats the hero's figure whenever every account is in the budget currency; it still differs when a legacy account in another currency or below zero exists, so it stays.

**Deploy.** Client code, two additive fields (the import result's skipped references, the match candidate's fee-line month) and the contributed figure's new reading; no migration or backfill. Visible once deployed: the Savings line's wording, the refunded note, the Reports and import counts, the "Before opening" tag, no Owed tile, and the edit prompts listing an archived jar the entry still names.

**Review round 1** (on `ff20bdb`, write-up in the project's `reviews/pr-286/`): no P1; P2-1 (the fee question), P2-2 (the leg date) and P2-3 (the contributed figure) fixed above; P3-1, P3-2 and P3-3 fixed above. **Round 2** (on `28e97f0`): clean, one P3 logged under Follow-ups.

**Validation (this machine).** From `main` at `e1e9984` (#285): `npx vitest run` **843 passed** (65 files; 823 on `main`, 20 added, none removed); `npx eslint .` clean apart from the pre-existing `AuthContext` warning; `npx tsc -b` clean; `dotnet build` clean; `dotnet test tests/AllotJar.Application.Tests` **1,292 passed**. The SQL integration tests need SQL Server and were not run here (they don't cover the changed code). No CI run, per Christopher's "Ignore CI jobs for now" (30 September).

**Release of [PR #285](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/285)** (the screen-test harness, recorded here as the next PR to merge). Christopher merged it as `e1e9984` at 10:30:59 UTC on 30 September. **No CI or Deploy ran for it:** GitHub Actions has refused every job for the account since about 20:57 UTC on 29 September, so the push's runs failed before any job started. #285 changed tests and docs only. Production still runs #280; the next Deploy that runs ships `main` with #281 to #285 and whatever merges after them.

Christopher confirmed on 30 September that the first handwritten note means the label only ("yes tab and page label"). O10 is closed in the HANDOVER.

## Follow-up pass, PR 2: transfers and import (rules 74, 80, 87–91; HANDOVER O1, P59, P71; the #277, #278 and #279 reviews' P3s) — 30 September 2026

**Scope.** The second of the three follow-up PRs Christopher approved on 30 September ("go", 12:29 UTC; the plan is `plans/2026-09-30-next-steps.md` in the project files): the transfer and import findings the reviews of #277, #278 and #279 logged, and his four answers of 12:22–12:28 UTC. Built beside PR 1 (screens); whichever merges second merges `main` in and records the other's release.

**Christopher's decisions (30 September 2026).**
- **R4-P3-1 "Keep".** A return marked by hand that the bank later dates after its jar was emptied and archived keeps the jar's share at the hand-marked date. `TransferReturns.SyncAsync` re-dates a kept `Return` operation to the bank's date only when no jar it credits was archived before that date (`KeepsItsDateAsync`); the transfer's `ReturnedOn` still takes the bank's date, so the source has the money from the day the bank says and the jar's history is unchanged. The ledger reader accepts a `Return` operation dated on or before its transfer's return (below).
- **R3-P3-2 "10% only".** The import proposes a fee only while it is at most 10% of what arrives (`StatementReferences.MaxProposedFeeShare`); the flat floor of 10 (`MaxProposedFeeFloor`) is removed, and with it the open question of which currency it was in. A bank's fixed charge on a small transfer is entered by hand on the transfer. Rule 91, HANDOVER O1 and P71 and catalog IMP-099 say so.
- **#278 Q4 "leave".** A refund larger than its purchase after W08's restating stays as it is; nothing changed.
- **P59 "Add", then "Keep shared": owner-side erase.** The budget owner can erase, without seeing it, a private account whose login is no longer in the household (`GetUnreachableAccounts`, `EraseUnreachableAccount`; `GET /api/accounts/unreachable`, `POST /api/accounts/{id}/erase` with the typed name). An account is unreachable when its privacy belongs to a login with no membership in the owner's household and no open invitation back for its email (`UnreachableAccounts.UnreachableIdsAsync`; review P3-2). The list shows name, currency, holder, when it went private and the entry count, never a balance or an entry. Christopher chose "Keep shared" (13:53 UTC, on the review's P2-1/P2-2): only what nobody else could see goes, and what the household already saw stays and keeps counting as it did. The erase takes the account's rows off the budget, and its transfers with other unreachable accounts (with their fees, jar and return shares cleared through `JarMovements.ClearAsync` and `TransferReturns.ClearAsync`, so departures re-settle); a transfer that never posted on the shared side (still to depart, or returned before arriving) goes too. An entry on a budget line, or a refund of a purchase the household sees (P20), stays as an entry with no account, as `DeleteAccount` keeps them. The shared side of a posted transfer stays as a plain entry on the shared account: a receipt becomes income dated the receipt with the bank's reference for it ("Transfer from an erased account"), a departure an expense dated the departure with its reference ("Transfer to an erased account"), each keeping its jar postings, and its fee becomes a plain expense on its line (the source's fee on the erased account stays only when it is on a line). A departure from a shared account that came back before arriving keeps its return too, as an income on the shared account dated the return with the return's reference (its id is the ledger's `TransferLedger.ReturnLegId`), and the return's stored jar shares become that income's own leg (round 2, R2-P2-1). A receipt from before receipt references keeps the source's reference the destination knew it by (R2-P3-1). Every kept side is marked `Transaction.CounterpartyErased` (new column, round 2's R2-P2-2): it counts in the account's ledger as the transfer did and nowhere else, so `ReportFigures` (the month report, the annual summary and the trends) and the register's hero totals and unassigned count skip it as they skip transfers. So no household figure, no statement match (`KnownAsync`) and no budget line changes. It refuses when a refund elsewhere names an erased purchase ("Id"), or when a transfer links the account to one private to a member still here (the guard's 403 before; review P1-1), and checks the shared sides against rule 74 under the ledger lock as a safety net (a posted side keeps its date and amount and an unposted one never counted, so it can't fire). The privacy guard on `SaveChanges` is told every unreachable account the erase touches (`IApplicationDbContext.AllowErasing`), so a movement between two of the departed login's accounts goes without the guard refusing. The person's savings pointer and the allocation's source are cleared as `DeleteAccount` clears them (review P3-1). The Accounts page shows the owner a "Private accounts nobody can reach" card with an **Erase…** button per account and a dialog that enables **Erase for good** only once the account's name is typed, then reads the accounts again (review P3-4). Members who aren't the owner get an empty list and a 403 on the erase (`[OwnerOnly]`).

**Findings fixed.**
- **(#279 P3-3) A destination statement printing the source's reference now confirms the receipt.** The destination counted a transfer's source reference as known once its receipt was recorded, a fallback for rows from before receipt references, so when both banks print one reference the credit was skipped as a duplicate instead of confirming the receipt. Rows from before #279 can't be told from a receipt confirmed by hand since, so the new column `Transactions.ReceiptKnownBySourceReference` marks them: migration **`ReceiptKnownBySourceReference`** adds it (default false) and sets it on every transfer with a receipt date and a source reference but no receipt reference, which keeps today's behaviour for every existing row. A receipt confirmed by hand from now on is not marked, so its bank's credit is offered as a match and confirms it (`StatementReferences.KnownAsync`); the marker is cleared when a receipt gets its own reference (import, `ConfirmTransferLegs`) or the row stops being a transfer.
- **(#279 P3-2) The import takes the ledger lock.** `CommitImport` now locks the import account, every counterparty account and both sides of every matched transfer (`BeginLedgerWriteAsync`) before it loads the match targets, as the other ledger writers do. A double-submitted fee match therefore runs second, finds the reference known and skips the row, instead of failing on `IX_Transactions_FeeForTransferId` with a 500; an edit and an import of one transfer no longer interleave. (SQL Server only; the InMemory provider has no lock, as before.)
- **(#279 P3-6) A fee following the bank's departure into another month meets the cross-month question.** `TransferFees.FollowDepartureAsync` re-validates the fee's line on the new date when the month changes (`ValidateWholeLineAsync`), so the import refuses with the `crossMonth` key unless the row acknowledges it; the import page already asks its own cross-month question for a matched transfer whose fee has a line, so the user sees the usual dialog, and the server's refusal is the backstop.
- **(#279 P3-4) An allocation re-run keeps a returned transfer and records a second one.** `AllocateIncome` groups a member's earlier transfers by member (allocation keys `alloc:{month}:{member}`, then `alloc:{month}:{member}#2`, `#3`…, `AllocationKey` is unique), leaves a returned one alone, and records the movement that is still needed beside it; a returned transfer no longer counts as a member's savings funded. A second live transfer for one member (a return un-marked after the re-run recorded the next one; review P2-3) is stale like any superseded row: withdrawn while in transit, refused once a bank confirmed it.
- **(#277 P3-6) A re-run blocked by another login's private account is told so.** Replacing an earlier in-transit allocation transfer into an account that is now private to another login met the privacy guard's 403; the re-run now refuses with a validation message naming the person ("Sam's allocation transfer for this month went into an account that is private to Sam, so only Sam can change or remove it…"), and only when that member's movement changed.
- **(#279 R3-P3-1) The ledger reader checks a `Return` operation against its transfer.** One whose transfer is no longer returned, was returned from another account, or is dated after the transfer's return is set aside and counted as unreconciled instead of applied. (Every writer keeps them in step; the check guards the read.)
- **(Rule 80, from the server final review) A jar's in-transit check reads every account.** `JarDepartures.HasUnpostedLegAsync` looked only at the jar's current account, so a transfer in transit that names the jar in an account it has left didn't stop the jar changing account again. It now looks at every account, and the refusal names the jar.
- **(#278 P3-2) The rate cache.** Frankfurter answers a not-yet-published day with the latest published rate and names that day: the provider now caches it for good under the day it is for and only briefly (`FailureRetryInterval`) under the day asked, so today's rate is picked up once it is out; a day two or more days past that has no rate of its own (a weekend or holiday) keeps its stand-in for good (`StandInSettlesAfterDays`; review P3-3). The cache is bounded (`MaxCachedRates`, 10,000 entries; the oldest half goes when it fills).
- **(#278 P3-5) `/auth/me`'s currency label and lock.** `budgetCurrency` (the app-wide label) is the viewed household's; `currencyLocked` and the new `ownBudgetCurrency` are the login's own household's, the one its preference seeds and the one the preference save checks (review P2-4: the first cut judged the lock on the viewed household and disagreed with the save for a login in two households). The Account page's locked currency field shows `ownBudgetCurrency`, so the field, its note and the save agree.

**Deploy.** Two migrations. **`ReceiptKnownBySourceReference`**: a new bit column and one `UPDATE` that marks the legacy rows (transfers with a receipt date and a source reference but no receipt reference). Nothing changes on screen or in any balance at deploy: the marked rows behave exactly as before, only receipts confirmed by hand after the deploy differ. **`CounterpartyErased`** (round 2): a new bit column, default false, no data step; no existing row is marked, since only an erase sets it. No backfill runs at start. The erase and the unreachable list are new endpoints; the list is empty until a login with private accounts leaves a household.

**Known limits and follow-ups (not in this change).** The fee bound is judged in the transfer's currency, which is the budget currency for every account W08 lets a new transfer use. The erase leaves a refund on another account that names one of the erased entries, and a transfer with an account a member still keeps private, to those who can see them. A transfer from the erased account that came back before arriving in a shared account posted nothing there and goes with the account. "Keep" has a flip side (review P3-5): with the jar's share kept at the hand-marked date while the return itself takes the bank's later date, the account's unallocated money reads lower by the share on the days between; today's figures are right, and it is inherent in the answer. The InMemory tests can't exercise the import's lock; the SQL integration test does.

**Review round 1** (on `aa60d55`, write-up in the project's `reviews/pr-287/`; the migration, the import lock, 10% only, Keep, the reader's Return check, rule 80 across accounts and the rate cache held). **P1-1** (the erase met the guard's 403 on a transfer between two of the departed login's accounts), **P2-1** and **P2-2** (the erase removed the shared side of a statement-confirmed transfer, its fee on a budget line, and a refund of a shared purchase; Christopher's "Keep shared"), **P2-3** (two live allocation transfers for one member after a return was un-marked), **P2-4** (the currency lock followed the viewed household while the save checked the login's own), **P3-1** (dangling savings and source pointers), **P3-2** (an open re-invite didn't protect the account), **P3-3** (a past weekend's rate re-fetched every interval) and **P3-4** (the Accounts page kept old balances after an erase) are fixed above; **P3-5** is noted above; **P3-6** (the duplicate #285 record) is resolved by the merge with #286 below. The reviewer's F1 case (a credit row that pulls the departure earlier, into another month, asks the fee's cross-month question) held, and #286 brought the import page's own question in line with it.

**Review round 2** (on `a4f4a04`, `reviews/pr-287/round2/`): every round-1 fix held under its probes and the merge with #286 was right; no P1. **R2-P2-1** (a transfer from the joint account that came back was erased with its fee and both references: a shared source posted its departure when it was confirmed, so it is kept, and its return as a second row), **R2-P2-2** (a kept side counted as uncategorised income or spending in the month report, moving a household figure: the `CounterpartyErased` mark, skipped by the reports and the register's totals) and **R2-P3-1** (a legacy receipt lost the reference the joint account knew) are fixed above. The SQL integration count is 96 (the reviewer's figure; an earlier note of 98 here came from a test listing, not a run).

**Review round 3** (on `7994572`, `reviews/pr-287/round3/`, with the #288 merge pass): no P1 or P2; every round-2 fix held, the `CounterpartyErased` migration is safe, and nothing else sums loose rows. Three P3s, fixed: **R3-P3-1** (the erase re-dated a hand-marked return's jar share to the bank's date, undoing Christopher's "Keep": in the reviewer's probe an archived jar read −100 for three days; a `Return` operation dated before the bank's return date now stays a dated operation, jar +S and unallocated −S at the hand-marked day, and the kept income lands unallocated on the bank's date, as the transfer read before), **R3-P3-2** (the register's Unassigned filter, on the client and the server's `UnassignedOnly`, listed kept rows the count skipped) and **R3-P3-3** (a kept row put on a budget line kept its mark, so the line counted it while the totals skipped it: `AssignTransaction` and `SplitTransaction` clear the mark, and the row counts everywhere from then on).

**Tests.** New `Imports/TransfersImportFollowUpTests.cs` (9): the shared-reference receipt confirmed and the legacy row still skipped, the fee's cross-month question (refused, acknowledged, and within the month asked nothing), the 10% bound on both sides with no floor, Keep, the reader setting aside a `Return` whose transfer is no longer returned or is dated after the return, and the in-transit refusal across accounts. `FrankfurterExchangeRateProviderTests` (+3): a not-yet-published day, a past weekend's stand-in kept for good, the bounded cache. `AllocationCommitConfirmationTests` (+3): a re-run after a return records a second transfer, two live transfers for one member lose the later in-transit one, an unchanged member is left alone. `JointAccountsW13Tests` (+16): the erase keeping what the household saw (the budget-line entry with no account, the joint account's departure as an expense, its balance unchanged; and the wrong name refused), an account its holder can still reach is neither listed nor erasable, only the owner sees the list, an open re-invite keeps the account off the list, the shared balance unchanged and an unposted receipt gone (rule 74), a transfer between two of the departed login's accounts, the refusal naming a transfer with a member's private account, the statement-confirmed departure and its fee kept, a refund of a shared purchase kept, a returned departure from the joint account kept as an expense and an income with the fee on its line and the joint balance right on both days, the jar share a return gave back still with the kept income, the month report and the register's marked rows unchanged, a legacy receipt's reference kept, a hand-marked return's share kept at its date after the erase (Keep, R3-P3-1), a kept row put on a line losing its mark and off the Unassigned list (R3-P3-2, R3-P3-3), the savings pointer cleared, and the allocation re-run's message naming the person. Updated: `TransferIdentityW07ReviewTests` (the bound is 10% only: 20/22 offered, 20/22.01 not, 1,000/1,100 offered, 1,000/1,100.01 not; a small transfer with a fixed fee is not offered), `ImportTransferLegsTests` and `ImportPreviewCommitTests` (legacy rows carry the marker; an unmarked hand-confirmed receipt is offered). SQL: new `TransfersImportFollowUpSqlTests` (2): two contexts committing the same fee match at once (one posts, one skips, no 500), and the migration marks exactly the legacy rows; `TransferIdentitySqlTests` reads the W07 rows with raw SQL, since the entity now carries a later column. `TTransactionsCoverageTests.ServerUnassignedOnly_IncludesTransfersAndSplitParents` now also has a kept row, left out. Client: `TransactionsPage.test.tsx` (the Unassigned filter leaves a kept row out); `AccountsPage.test.tsx` (+3): the card and dialog, the erase call and the accounts read again after it, the card absent for a non-owner; `AccountPage.test.tsx` (the locked field shows the login's own household's currency, not the viewed one's).

## Follow-up pass, PR 3: the tests (W14; the #285 review's P3-2 and P3-4, the #276 review's P3-12, CS8620) — 30 September 2026

**Scope.** The third and last of the follow-up PRs Christopher approved on 30 September ("go", 12:29 UTC; `plans/2026-09-30-next-steps.md`): the test-suite leftovers the #285 review logged. Tests, test helpers and one server warning; no application behaviour, migration or backfill changes. It also records #287's release.

**The tests type-check, and the check gates (#285 review P3-4).** `client/tsconfig.test.json` extends the app config and includes every file under `src`, the tests and `src/test` helpers that `tsconfig.app.json` leaves out; `npm run lint` now runs `eslint .` and then `tsc -p tsconfig.test.json --noEmit`, so CI's frontend job (which runs `npm run lint`) fails on a test that would not compile before Vitest runs it. Against `main` the check found 51 errors in 14 files (the review's 48 plus three from #286 and #287): fixture objects missing DTO fields (`BudgetItemDto`'s `savingsId`, `savingsAvailable`, `dueDay`, `isPaid`; `JarScheduleMonthDto.skipped`; `JarScheduleDto.unplanned`; `TransactionSplitDto`'s `memberId`/`memberName`; `CategoryVM.excludeFromAllocation`; `FeatureFlags.analytics`), three `findByRole` calls with a stray fourth argument, two `Element` where `HTMLElement` was needed, two unused `url` parameters, and the parameter property in `src/test/setup.ts` under `erasableSyntaxOnly`. All are fixed; no test body changed.

**One fixture module per page family (#285 review's follow-up).** The builders the screen tests copied within a family now live in `client/src/test/fixtures/`: `budget.ts` (`budgetItem`, `budgetCategory`, `budgetMonth`: full DTOs whose `remaining` and totals derive from what the test passes), `jars.ts` (`jar`, `scheduleMonth`, `schedule`, `ledger`, `account`), `import.ts` (`accounts`, `members`, `month`, `preview`, `previewWithTransfer`, `csvPreview`, `committed`), `allocation.ts` (`jointCurrent`, `profile`, `preview`), `me.ts` (`me`, `ownerMe`, `joinedMe`, the `/auth/me` answers) and `http.ts` (`axiosError`). Thirty test files import them; a file keeps a thin alias where its defaults differ (the Dashboard's Holiday jar, the cycles file's Road tax, the catalog file's 60-a-month schedule) and anything only it uses. Every figure a test asserts on is passed explicitly where the builder's derivation would differ (a group's `totalActual` the page never reads, a month's `totalIncome` with no income line). The client test tree is 1,083 lines shorter and 1,198 longer, 314 of them the six modules.

**`renderWithProviders` through Testing Library's `wrapper` option (#285 review P3-2).** `providersFor(options)` returns the provider component (router > auth > features > household > month, as before) and `renderWithProviders` passes it as `wrapper`, so `rerender(<Page />)` keeps the same provider tree and its state. `PeoplePage.catalog.test` (PPL month change) and `AllocationPage.catalog.test` (ALLOC-007) drop their copies of the router-and-auth tree, which had to match the helper's or React remounted the providers.

**Every late-answer guard on the Dashboard has a test (#276 review P3-12).** `DashboardPage.lateAnswers.test.tsx` (DASH-082, 22 cases) pins each guard #276 added that DASH-081 left out: `refreshMonth` (the month found, and a 404 that must not say the viewed month is missing), `createFromTemplate`, the rollback of `commitItem`, `setItemMember`, `setBill`, `setPaid`, `renameItem` and `renameCategory`, the reconcile and the rollback of `addItem`, `deleteItem`, `addCategory`, `deleteCategory`, `moveCategory` and `reorderItems`, and `onJarLineSkipped`. Each starts its action on the current month with the request held open, moves to the previous month (Rent 900 against the current month's 1,100), then answers or fails the request and checks the previous month's grid is untouched; the two `refreshMonth` cases reach it through the jar line's Remove dialog (archive Holiday), and the blur-committed edits use DASH-078's trick of committing and changing month with one click. Each test was run with its guard removed and failed; with the guards in place all 22 pass.

**CS8620 is gone, with the model snapshot unchanged.** `TransactionConfiguration` gave `OriginalCurrency` (a `CurrencyCode?`) a `ValueConverter<CurrencyCode, string>` instance, which the compiler flagged for nullability. It now names the converter's type (`HasConversion<CurrencyCodeConverter>()`): EF instantiates it and handles the null itself, the column stays `nvarchar(3)` nullable, and `dotnet ef migrations has-pending-model-changes` reports none, so no migration. The solution builds with 0 warnings.

**Adversarial review (at `1ed6993`).** No P1 or P2. P3-1, fixed here: `budgetMonth` derived `isBalanced` as income equal to planned, so an empty month read as balanced where the server (and the client's own `isBalanced` selector, which the Dashboard uses instead of the flag) needs income above zero; the derivation now requires it. Nothing reads the DTO flag, so no test changed meaning. Two follow-ups: (P3-2) the module-level fixture objects in `import.ts`, `allocation.ts` and `me.ts` (`accounts`, `members`, `month`, `jointCurrent`, `ownerMe`) reach the API mock by reference and are mutable, so a test that edits one would leak into the next; factories or `Object.freeze` would stop it. (P3-3, pre-existing) the type-check gate covers `src` only: the Playwright specs under `client/e2e` and `e2e/mocks.ts` are type-checked by nothing.

**Found along the way (not in this change).** The skip-or-archive dialog opened from a jar line (`removingJarLine`) is not cleared when the viewed month changes: it unmounts while the new month loads and remounts holding the old month's line, titled with the new month, and a Skip from it would send the old month's line id. `onJarLineSkipped`'s guard keeps the answer off the new month's grid; clearing `removingJarLine` in the month-change effect would close the dialog with the month. A one-line follow-up for the next screens change.

**Deploy.** Nothing changes for users: no application behaviour, migration or backfill; the one server change is a compiler warning with the same mapping.

**Validation (this machine: .NET SDK 10.0, SQL Server 2022 in Docker).** `dotnet build AllotJar.slnx`: 0 errors, 0 warnings. `dotnet ef migrations has-pending-model-changes`: none. Unit suite **1324 passed** (1324 on `main`). SQL integration suite **96 passed** (96 on `main`). From `client`: `npm run lint` (ESLint and the new test type-check) clean apart from the pre-existing `AuthContext` warning; `npx tsc -b` clean; `npx vitest run` **868 passed** in 66 files (846 on `main`; the 22 new DASH-082 cases, no test removed). No CI run, per Christopher's "Ignore CI jobs for now" (30 September).

**Release of [PR #287](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/pull/287)** (transfers and import, recorded here as the next PR to merge). Christopher squash-merged it as `7261aee` at 19:26:06 UTC on 30 September. **No CI or Deploy ran for it:** GitHub Actions still refuses every job, per his "Ignore CI jobs for now"; production still runs #280. The next Deploy ships `main` with #281 to #288 at once, and at that deploy #287's two additive migrations run at API start: `ReceiptKnownBySourceReference` (one `UPDATE` that marks received transfers known only by their source reference) and `CounterpartyErased` (a bit column, no data step). Nothing needs running before it.

## End-to-end check, PR A: jar money is checked across its whole history (rules 68, 70, 77; app-2026-09-30 review JL-P1-1, JL-P1-2, JL-P2-3, JL-P2-4) — 1 October 2026

**Scope.** The end-to-end adversarial check of `main` at `46412e6` (report `/mnt/project-files/reviews/app-2026-09-30/REPORT.md`, Christopher's "Go, all five" on 1 October) found that every jar write dated in the past was checked on its own date only. Rule 74 already holds across the whole history (P10); these now do too. Each was reproduced on `main` by the review's probes (the two P1s also over HTTP on SQL Server) and each has a regression test that fails on `main`.

- **(JL-P1-1) A back-dated reallocation.** Moving 100 Holiday → Car "as of 2 January" passed because Holiday held 100 that day, although it spent the money on 15 January; from then on Holiday read −100 and Car's 100 was not backed by the account (rules 68, 70). `ReallocateJarMoney` now loads the account's ledger once and, after the existing checks on the chosen date, checks every later date on which the account's ledger changes, future-dated entries included (`JarHistoryChecks.FirstRefusal`): the source must still hold the amount, and no later day may show positive jars the account's money does not back, or a larger shortfall than it already had. The refusal names the day ("Holiday holds 0 on 2026-01-15, after what was paid or moved out of it since 2026-01-02, so it cannot give 100 as of 2026-01-02…"). A shortfall a payment already caused (rule 79 draws a no-line payment from unallocated) stays readable and does not block a move that leaves it as it is (rule 70).
- **(JL-P1-2) Money "already set aside" on a new jar, back-dated (rule 77).** The same check on the account's unallocated money, including an account that backs no jar yet (`JarLedgerReader.LoadAsync(…, includeUnbacked: true)`). A refused jar is still never saved.
- **(JL-P2-3) A transfer "from a jar" (Move money, the transfer form).** It was stored like a payment, so Move money could take 500 out of a jar holding 100, although `MoveMoneyModal` said the server checks it. The default taken when Christopher said go: a move of money is capped like a reallocation (rule 68); only a payment may overdraw a jar (rule 5). `CreateTransfer`, `ConfirmTransferLegs` (a departure confirmed later) and `UpdateTransfer` check the draw on the departure date and every later date, reading the ledger without the transfer (`excludeTransactionIds`), so an edit is checked against what it replaces. `UpdateTransfer` checks only a changed draw (jar, account, amount or departure date), so a new payee or receipt never trips on what the jar did since; a returned transfer holds the money only until its return (rule 90). A transfer still waiting for its departure takes nothing yet. The fee is not part of the cap: it is a payment.
- **(JL-P2-4) Lowering an account's opening balance below what its jars hold.** It was accepted with no word and left a backing deficit (rule 70). `UpdateAccount` now refuses it, naming the first day the account would hold less than its jars ("On 2026-01-01 Savings would hold 500 while its jars hold 800. Move money out of the jars to unallocated first, or keep the opening balance."), unless that day was already as short.

**Reader.** `JarLedgerSource` gains `ViewAt` (one account as of a date, without reading the others) and `OperationDatesAfter` (the days its balances change, the only ones a whole-history check needs to read); `At` is unchanged in what it returns.

**Deploy.** No migration, no data change. Rows already written by the old checks stay as they are and keep reading as before (a deficit they left shows on the Jars page, as today); only new writes are refused.

**Validation (this machine: .NET SDK 10.0, SQL Server 2022 in Docker).** Unit suite **1338 passed** (1324 on `main`: 14 new, one changed, below); the 8 regression tests fail on `main` with the fix stashed and the 6 controls pass there too. SQL integration suite **96 passed**. Real-backend Playwright journeys on SQL Server: see the PR. No client file changed.

**Review round 1** (on `e975e77`, write-up in the project's `reviews/pr-297/`): no P1, three P2s, fixed here.
- **(P2-1) The import still took more from a jar than it held.** A debit row recorded as a transfer "paid from" a jar, new or confirming a waiting transfer's departure, skipped the cap. Both import paths now run the same check (`JarHistoryChecks.TransferDrawRefusalAsync`) and hold the row with the refusal, as rule 74's shortfall does (P8), rather than failing the commit. Nothing is saved until the end of a commit, so the check is given what the rows accepted before it do to the statement account (`JarLedgerChange`): two rows that each fit the jar alone are not both accepted, and money a credit earlier in the same statement put in the jar can be moved on.
- **(P2-2) Editing a transfer refused corrections that left the jar no worse.** After a later payment overdrew the jar (rule 5), lowering the transfer or moving it a day was refused. When the edit keeps the same jar and account, a date is now refused only when the new draw takes more than the jar holds there and more than the old draw took that day (the reviewer's proposed fix).
- **(P2-3) Moving the opening date earlier** brought in payments recorded before it with no word and could leave the jars unbacked. The opening-balance check now runs on an opening-date change too.
- P3, done here: the cap default is recorded in HANDOVER P6 and P10.
- Validation: unit suite **1346 passed** (8 new; the three import tests fail without the import change), SQL integration suite **96 passed**, build 0 warnings.

**Review round 2** (on `8fb09a5`): round 1 confirmed; two P2s in the import's same-commit accounting, fixed here. (R2-P2-1) A payment on a jar's savings line earlier in the statement was counted as unallocated money, so a later transfer from that jar could still take money the payment had used. It now counts as rule 79 reads it back: the jar of its line, or of each slice's line, while the account holds it (`JarLedgerReader.Derive`). (R2-P2-2) A row that confirmed a transfer the app had already posted counted that leg twice, once in the stored ledger and once in the commit's own changes. That let a later transfer take money a confirmed receipt had already brought in, and held a valid one after a confirmed departure. The check now leaves the confirmed transfers out of the stored ledger and reads their leg from the commit's changes alone, keeping the stored jar answer when the row gives none. Unit suite **1349 passed** (3 new, failing on `8fb09a5`), SQL integration suite **96 passed**.

**Follow-ups (not in this change).** (Review P3-1) Removing a transfer's return, or dating it later, lengthens its draw unchecked: the jar may have given the returned money on since. Checking it as a changed draw breaks two Accept and settle tests (#280: undoing a return after the jar moved is accepted and settled), so it needs a design that keeps that decision. (Review P3-2) The whole-history walk rebuilds the ledger from the opening date for each later date: on three years of daily entries a back-dated reallocation takes about 1.5 s and lowering an opening balance about 1.8 s in memory. A single forward pass over the operations would make it linear. (Review P3-4) The opening-balance check reads without the account's ledger lock; that is PR B's (JL-P2-6).

## Fix plan D: privacy and sign-in (the end-to-end check's S-P2-1 to S-P2-4 and S-P3-2; P59) — 1 October 2026

**Scope.** PR D of the fix plan Christopher approved on 1 October ("Go, all five", 10:48 UTC; `reviews/app-2026-09-30/REPORT.md` in the project files).

- **S-P2-1, S-P2-2: a private account's id no longer reaches other members.** The People page's savings account and the allocation profile's source account come back without the id when the account is another login's private one, with a `savingsPrivate` / `sourcePrivate` flag instead; saving the form unchanged keeps the stored account.
- **S-P2-3 and the review's P2-1: a login someone else created chooses its own password from its own inbox.** A direct invite still sets a temporary password, but the login is marked (`MustChangePassword`, additive migration `LoginMustChangePassword`, no data step) and the app emails it a "Choose your AllotJar password" link. Until the link is used, every signed-in endpoint except `/auth/me` answers 403 `password_change_required`; `change-password` and the terms consent are refused too, so whoever knows the temporary password can neither pick the new one nor accept the terms for the person (review P3-1). Only the emailed reset link (or Google sign-in removing the password) clears the mark. Link invites are emailed to the invited address and the token is no longer returned to the inviter. Logins that already exist are not marked; whether existing invitees should be is Christopher's open choice.
- **S-P2-4: the sign-in rate limit counts each visitor.** The API reads `X-Forwarded-For` through IIS on loopback and Cloudflare's ranges only, so the limit is per visitor rather than one bucket for the whole site; refresh has its own, larger limit.
- **S-P3-2:** the front site sends `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000`.

**Deploy.** One additive migration, no data step. Invitations now depend on email: before relying on them, check the startup Event Log line that names the email provider (`Program.cs`) on production.

**Review round 2 (at `3e3e509`).** R2-P2-1: an invite whose email never went out was reported as sent. Outside Development an invite is now refused while the server can only log emails; otherwise the invite waits for the send and says whether it went out (`emailSent`), and the blocked login asks for its link again through `POST /auth/password-link`, which reports a failed send instead of claiming it. R2-P2-2: the inviter's name in the invite email's preview line is HTML-encoded like the rest of the email. Before merging, check the API's startup Event Log line on production ("Outbound email provider: …" means email is delivered; "Outbound email is NOT configured" means invites will be refused).

**Follow-ups (not in this change).** Send again (review R2-P2-1): a "Send again" on People for a pending invite, or a login still waiting to choose its password, so an email lost after the provider accepted it isn't a dead end for the owner (today the owner removes a pending invite and invites again, and a direct-invite login asks for its own link from the prompt). P3-2: the password-change gate reads the login's mark from the database on every signed-in request; a claim in the access token (re-issued when the mark clears) would avoid the read. P3-3: Cloudflare's ranges are a fixed list in `ClientAddress.cs` (overridable by `ForwardedHeaders:TrustedProxyNetworks`) while the server firewall fetches them live, so a new Cloudflare range would be keyed as one visitor until the list is updated; loading the same live list at start-up would keep them in step.

## Fix plan C: transfers and import identity (TX-P2-1 to TX-P2-4, CL-P2-1, TX-P3-5; rules 8, 38, 51, 88, 91) — 1 October 2026

**Scope.** PR C of the five-PR fix plan from the end-to-end check of 30 September (`reviews/app-2026-09-30/REPORT.md`, "Fix plan"), which Christopher approved on 1 October ("Go, all five", 10:48 UTC). No migration, no startup backfill, no stored row rewritten.

**A transfer's fee can't be refunded (TX-P2-1).** `RefundRules.ValidateOriginalAsync` refuses an original with `FeeForTransferId` ("A transfer's fee can't be refunded. Record the money back as income, or edit the transfer's fee."), which closes linking, creating and editing a refund onto a fee. A refund linked before this (none should exist; pre-deploy Q3) keeps the fee from going or shrinking below it: `TransferFees.EnsureRefundsFitAsync` runs in `TransferFees.ApplyAsync` (fee removed or lowered, from `UpdateTransfer`, `UpdateTransaction` retyping and import) and in `DeleteTransaction` for the transfer's fee, so these answer with a validation message instead of the SQL Server FK error (a 500).

**A camt.053 entry without a bank reference keeps one identity (TX-P2-2).** No `AcctSvcrRef` and no `EndToEndId` (or `NOTPROVIDED`) now gives `camt:<32-hex SHA-256 of date|signed amount|currency|payee|remittance text>#N`, N counting identical entries in the file from 0, as the CSV parsers do. A re-import or an overlapping statement skips it; the unique reference index protects it.

**A reference repeated within a statement keeps one identity across statements (TX-P2-3).** The parser no longer suffixes a repeat that differs in date, amount or direction: it keeps the bank's reference, and preview and commit store it under `StatementReferences.Derive` (`ref~hash`), as a reference reused across statements already was, so every statement showing that row reads it the same. Preview now claims each new row's reference as the commit does (`KnownReferences.Add`), so the second row of a pair is offered under its derived reference, not flagged as reusing a known reference. Only an entry identical in date, amount and direction gets `#N`. `KnownReferences.Resolve` looks for the entry under its derived reference and, for rows stored before this, under `ref#<digits>` (indexed by `ref`), even when the raw reference is free, as it is when the statement's first row with that reference was never imported (unticked, or held and discarded).

**A commit confirming a recorded transfer needs the statement's account (TX-P2-4).** `CommitImportCommandValidator` and the handler refuse a row with `MatchTransferId` (and the validator one with `ReturnOfTransferId`) when no account is chosen, instead of a 500.

**Rows are named by position (TX-P3-5, client CL-P2-1).** `ImportHeldRow` carries `Index` and the result `SkippedIndexes`, the row's place in the request. The import screen gives each preview row its own key and matches held and skipped rows by position (by reference only from a server that sends no index), so rows sharing a reference are no longer held, skipped, split or keyed together.

**Tests.** `TransferImportIdentityTests` (12; 11 fail on `main`, the identical-rows one records a guard that holds), `ImportPage.rowKeys.test.tsx` (4; all fail on `main`). IMP-088a/b now assert the content reference and the re-import skip; `ParserToleranceTests` adds a differing repeat that keeps the raw reference. Docs: IMP-088 and the import map.

**Deploy.** Rows imported from a camt.053 statement before this without a reference were stored with `BankReference = NULL`; the next import of a statement showing them offers them once more (unticked when the possible-duplicate hint finds them). Nothing in the data marks a row as imported, so `reviews/pr-299/pre-deploy-checks.sql` (project files, read only) lists the candidates for Christopher to judge: Q1a, reference-less rows (and recorded receipts) on accounts with a camt-looking reference; Q1b, accounts with rows but no reference at all (a card account whose bank prints NOTPROVIDED). Q2 lists legacy `#N` rows (information); Q3, refunds linked to a fee, must be empty.

**Adversarial review (round 1 at `a7c269c`).** No P1. P2-1, fixed: a repeated reference whose first row was never stored was offered again by the next statement (and so was a lone legacy `ref#1` row), because `Resolve` called an unknown raw reference new before trying its other forms; it now tries them first. P2-2, fixed: the pre-deploy Q1 looked only at accounts with referenced rows and between their first and last referenced dates; Q1a and Q1b replace it (all five of the reviewer's seeded candidates appear). P3-2, fixed: the `#N` lookup is an index by base reference instead of a scan of every known reference.

**Follow-ups (not in this change).**
- TX-P3-1: a row moved to another account by hand is offered again by its statement.
- #299 P3-1: two paths still delete a fee row without the refund check, so a refund linked to a fee before this change still meets the FK error there (`AllocateIncomeCommandHandler` re-recording a savings transfer's stale fee, `EraseUnreachableAccountCommandHandler`), and confirming such a refund's converted amount now says a fee can't be refunded. Moot when pre-deploy Q3 is empty; if it isn't, fix them here before merge.
- #299 P3-3: a reference-less camt.053 row gets a new identity if the bank rewords its remittance text between downloads, or if a statement boundary splits two identical same-day entries (as with the CSV parsers).

## End-to-end check, PR B: ledger locks and future-dated payments (rules 26, 74, 79, 80; HANDOVER P11, P51; app-2026-09-30 review JL-P2-1, JL-P2-2, JL-P2-5, JL-P2-6, MG-P2-1, TX-P3-4, JL-P3-3, JL-P3-4; #297 review P3-4 and R3-P3-1) — 1 October 2026

**Scope.** The second of the five fixes from the end-to-end check of `main` at `46412e6` (Christopher's "Go, all five", 1 October). Built on `main` after PR A (#297). Each finding was reproduced on `main` by the review's probes and has a regression test that fails on `main`.

- **(JL-P2-5) Archive, Complete and a jar's change of account took no ledger lock.** A reallocation into Holiday checked that it was not archived before taking the account's lock, so an archive in between left the archived jar holding 100 (rule 26); the same shape let a move re-designate a jar while money was on its way into it in the old account. Archive (and restore), Complete, Delete and the jar edit now lock the account holding the jar before they read it (`JarLocks.LoadLockedAsync`), re-reading which account holds it under the lock; a change of account also locks the account it moves to, in id order with the first, so the save's own lock there (P55) is already held. `ReallocateJarMoney` takes its lock before it reads the two jars, so a jar archived or moved meanwhile is refused. Splitting or re-assigning a payment now locks its account too (JL-P3-3: the jar it answers could be archived meanwhile).
- **(JL-P2-6, TX-P3-4, #297 review P3-4) Rule 74 was checked on accounts whose lock was not held.** Deleting a transfer locked its source only, so a payment from the destination relying on the receipt could commit alongside and leave the destination at −50. Delete now locks every side (and the fee's account), `ConfirmTransferLegs` both sides (confirming a receipt answers the destination's jars), `UpdateTransaction` the account receiving the row, the one losing it and a transfer's former destination, `CreateTransfer` both sides, and `UpdateAccount` its account before any check (the opening-balance check PR A added included).
- **(JL-P2-1, JL-P2-2) A payment on a jar's savings line dated after today.** It stores no operation (the reader draws it from the jar on its date, rule 79), so the zero balance today did not see it: archiving passed and the archived jar read −100 the next day, and a change of account passed and rewrote the next day (P51). Archive now lists such a payment among the entries that haven't reached the account yet ("a payment of 100 for Hotel on its line (on 2 Oct 2026)"), and the change of account refuses it as "an entry dated 2 Oct 2026" in the old account (`JarMovements.LinePaymentsAfterAsync`, used by `JarDepartures.EntryDatedAfterAsync`). Only the account holding the jar counts: a payment on the line from another account is not the jar's.
- **(MG-P2-1) Two API processes starting together wrote every legacy departure twice.** `JarDepartures.BackfillAsync` now finds the accounts with legacy moves, takes their ledger locks and reads again under them before it writes, so the second process finds the first one's departures.
- **(#297 review R3-P3-1) The import ignored a transfer's return when checking a later transfer from the jar.** A statement confirming the departure of a transfer that had already come back left the whole transfer out of the stored ledger, return included, and counted only the departure; a statement row confirming a return was not counted at all until saved. Either way a later row taking the returned money from the refilled jar was held with "holds 0". The import's running account of earlier rows now carries the return as the ledger will read it once saved: the return leg into unallocated money on its date, then the jars the return's stored operations refill (`RecordReturnAsync`). A return the app already counted (marked by hand before, its departure not confirmed in this statement) is left to the stored ledger.
- **(JL-P3-4) HANDOVER P11** listed the import commit as unlocked and the account edit as locked; both were the other way round. It now lists every locked path. `MoveMoneyModal`'s claim that the server caps the source jar became true with PR A.

**Deploy.** No migration, no data change. Writes take a few more row locks than before (one more account on transfers, deletes and confirmations), always in id order.

**Validation (this machine: .NET SDK 10.0, SQL Server 2022 in Docker).** Unit suite **1356 passed** (1349 on `main`: 7 new in `JarFutureEntriesTests`, 5 of which fail on `main`). SQL integration suite **106 passed** (96 on `main`: 10 new in `LedgerLockSqlTests`, 9 of which fail on `main`; the tenth, moving a jar while the new account is locked, already waited on `main` through P55's lock and is kept as a guard). Build 0 warnings. No client file changed.

**Review round 1** (on `17bf63f`, write-up in the project's `reviews/pr-300/`): no P1, one P2 and three P3s.
- **(P2-1) A payment on a jar's line checked which account holds the jar before taking that account's lock.** A move committing in between let the payment save from the old account on the jar's line, drawing nothing from the jar (JL-P2-1's end state). `CreateTransaction`, `AssignTransaction` and `SplitTransaction` now take the lock before the line or slices are validated (the reviewer's proposed fix), and `LinkRefund` takes its account's lock before it validates a chosen line, which had the same order.
- **(P3-2)** `JarLocks.LoadLockedAsync` now refuses ("try again") instead of carrying on under the wrong account's lock when the jar keeps moving.
- **(P3-3)** `UpdateTransfer` now locks every account the edit can touch before it reads the transfer and its previous draw, and refuses if the transfer's accounts changed meanwhile.
- Validation: unit suite **1356 passed**; SQL integration suite **110 passed** (4 new: a payment, an assignment and a split racing the jar's move, all three failing without the fix, and a transfer edit waiting for its lock, kept as a guard). Main merged in (#296, client tests only).

**Follow-ups (not in this change).** (Review P3-1, a question for Christopher) A payment entered on an archived jar's line after the archive is accepted and overdraws the archived jar (−30 in the review's probe). It predates this PR: rule 26 covers the archive itself, and P40 keeps the current month's line. Should such a payment be refused (record it on another line), or allowed as rule 5 lets a payment overdraw a jar? **Answered 1 Oct ("Refuse") and done by fix plan E (#301):** refused unless the archived jar can cover it, for the form, assign, split, edit and import.

## Fix plan E: schedules, cycles, pocket money and the late-answer guards (SC-P2-1 to SC-P2-4, MG-P2-2, RP-P2-1, RP-P2-2, CL-P2-2, CL-P2-3, SC-P3-3, SC-P3-7, CL-P3-2; rules 12–14, 19–23, 53–54, 60–61, 82, 83) — 1 October 2026

**Scope.** PR E, the last of the five-PR fix plan from the end-to-end check of 30 September (`reviews/app-2026-09-30/REPORT.md`, "Fix plan"), which Christopher approved on 1 October ("Go, all five", 10:48 UTC), plus two answers he gave at 13:43 and the client's "today" on UTC. No migration, no data change at deploy.

**Money set aside when a jar is created is the month's opening balance (SC-P2-1, SC-P3-7).** The preview planned 300 over three months from the 90 set aside (70/70/70), but once saved the ledger dated the 90 today, so every later read counted it as "contributed this month" (the card read "in 90, to go 0") and a month created early was planned at 105. `JarLedgerSource.SetAsideBetween` reads the `InitialAllocation` postings of a month, `JarPlanner.OpeningOf` adds them to the balance the month began with (planner, month creation, card), and `ContributionsBetween` leaves them out. The preview, the planted line, the card and a month created early now all say 70.

**A payment linked to a cycle that already rolled forward replants (SC-P2-2).** Marking a Funded (or missed) closed cycle paid only linked the payment, so the jar read "plan 0" while 550 short until some unrelated edit replanted. `MarkJarCyclePaid` now replants after linking, as it does when it closes the open cycle (not for an archived or completed jar).

**Archive and restore keep a skipped month skipped (SC-P2-3; his 1 Oct default).** Archiving removed every later line, flag included, so a restore planted the skipped month in full. `SavingsLines.CancelPlansAsync(keepSkipped: true)` keeps a skipped line at 0 with its flag on archive; completion still removes it. HANDOVER P40 records it.

**A month that is over is planted toward the definition it had then (SC-P2-4).** Back-filling last month after its Recurring cycle was dismissed planted it toward the next cycle a year on (7.69) while the shelf judged it by the cycle open then. `JarPlanner.PlanMonthAsync`'s past branch now plans with `JarHistory.DefinitionsOnAsync` at the month's last day (revision then, cycle open then, archived or live then), the one `JarFigures` reads: 100.

**Deleting the payment that marked a cycle paid reopens it (MG-P2-2; his 1 Oct answer "Reopen it").** The cycle stayed "Paid" with a null link (SET NULL). `JarCycles.ReopenPaidByAsync` runs in `DeleteTransaction`: when it is the jar's latest closed cycle and the jar still plans toward the next one, the row goes and the jar's open cycle returns to its deadline, replanted from what the jar holds without the payment (the next read settles it afresh, rules 20–21); an older cycle, or a jar archived, completed or re-dated since, keeps its row with no payment, judged Funded or Missed from the ledger without the payment, so another payment can be marked, closed on its deadline; one whose deadline is still ahead (paid early) waits unjudged and `JarCycles.SettleAsync` judges it when the deadline arrives (review P3-1). Erasing a departed login's private account and removing a transfer's fee reopen the cycles their rows paid the same way, and a transfer's fee is no longer offered or accepted as a cycle's payment (review P3-2). HANDOVER O3 records it.

**A payment can't push an archived jar below zero (his 1 Oct answer "Refuse"; the #300 review's open question).** A new payment, an assignment, a split or an edit that puts money on an archived jar's line is refused unless the jar holds the draw on its date and every later date its ledger changes ("Holiday is archived and holds 0, so it can't pay 30. Restore Holiday or put the payment on another line."). What the stored version of the payment already drew is not asked for twice. The check is in `JarMovements.ApplyOutgoingAsync`, which runs after the account's ledger lock in #300's order. An imported bank row on an archived jar's line (placed by hand or by the auto-categoriser) gets the same check, counting the import's earlier rows, and is held with the message rather than failing the import (`JarMovements.ArchivedLineRefusalAsync`, review P2-1). Confirming a foreign amount needs no check: the ledger draws a row's own amount from the jar whatever its currency, so the confirmation moves no jar money. A live jar may still be overdrawn by a payment (rule 5).

**Pocket money (RP-P2-1, RP-P2-2).** A refund on a person's line with less spent than came back read as a negative payment: the archive preview said 70 planned for a 50 line and Cancel wrote −20. `PocketMoneyLines` now counts paid as never below zero.
