After the "Pocket Money" group was renamed, the sync created a second group and a second linked line per person (rule 53); it now finds the person's linked line in any Expense group and adopts the flagged group holding linked lines whatever its name.

**`CreateBudgetMonth` reads today once (SC-P3-3).**

**Client.** (CL-P2-2) The Allocation page drops an answer for a month no longer shown (preview, commit banner, pocket-money sync), as the Dashboard does. (CL-P2-3) The People page's reload after a save reads the month on screen and drops an answer for a month left meanwhile. (CL-P3-2) Logout clears the login's person, sharing-review flag and own budget currency. **Today is the UTC day (rule 82, P14; not in the report):** the viewed month on opening the app (`MonthContext`), the bill due checks (`budgetModel.billStatus`), the getting-started checklist and the Reports year now read `currentMonth()`/`todayIso()` (`lib/dates.ts`), so from midnight to 01:00 or 02:00 Malta time the app no longer shows a new month the server still counts as the future; `TransactionsPage` and `AllocationPage` use `todayIso` instead of their own copies.

**Tests.** Unit: `SchedulesCyclesFixPlanETests` (9), `PocketMoneyFixPlanETests` (2), `ArchivedJarPaymentTests` (4), all failing on `main`; four tests that used an `InitialAllocation` to stand for a contribution now use a reallocation, and `ContributionsBetween_ReadsOnlyTheDatesAsked` asserts the set-aside money separately. SQL: `JarCycleReopenSqlTests` (2). Journey: `budget-lifecycle` copy-from-previous now expects Holiday's 100 set aside to open the month (91.66, then 81.81 next month). Client: `AllocationPage.lateAnswer`, `PeoplePage.lateAnswer`, `AuthContext.logout` (from the review's probes, failing on `main`), `MonthContext.test` and two `budgetModel` cases that set their own clock and the Malta time zone.

**Validation (this machine, .NET 10, SQL Server 2022 in Docker).** Unit suite **1388 passed**; SQL integration suite **109 passed**; client unit **all passed**, lint clean, build; mocked Playwright 103 passed, 1 failed (UX-005, the known flaky focus ring); journeys 46 passed.

**Follow-ups (not in this change).** A cycle reopened before its deadline is stored as "Funded" with no balance until the deadline arrives (review R2; it shows only if it becomes the jar's last cycle, which then reads "funded on time" early). The import adds two small queries per debit row on a line when the household has an archived jar (review R2). An archived jar keeps a skipped later month's line (0, skipped) on that month's budget while it is archived; "Plan it again" there is refused and typing an amount un-skips it (review P3-3, a side effect of his default). SC-P3-1, SC-P3-2, SC-P3-4 to SC-P3-6, RP-P3-1 to RP-P3-4 and the client P3s other than CL-P3-2 stay as listed in the report.

## Follow-ups F1: screens that answer late or lose a click (the end-to-end check's client P3-1, P3-4 to P3-6; #301 P3-4; the Dashboard dialog from follow-up pass PR 3) — 1 October 2026

**Scope.** The first of three follow-up PRs from the open list of 1 October (`plans/2026-10-01-open-follow-ups.md` in the project files, group 1). Client only; no server change, migration or data step.

- **The skip-or-archive dialog closes with its month.** Opened from a jar line, it stayed open across a month change, holding the old month's line under the new month's title, and a Skip from it sent the old line's id. The month-load effect now clears it (`DashboardPage`). Test: DASH-082 "the skip-or-archive dialog closes when the user changes month".
- **(Client P3-4) A row re-answered during an import keeps its answer.** When the commit held rows, the review was rebuilt from the rows as they were when Import was clicked, so a row ticked, unticked or put on another line while the commit was out came back as it was. It now filters the rows as they are (`setRows((current) => …)`). Test: `ImportPage.rowKeys.test` "a row re-answered while the commit is out…".
- **(Client P3-5) A network blip no longer signs you out.** Any failed `/auth/refresh` ended the session and broadcast a sign-out to every tab, so a laptop waking before its Wi-Fi was back landed on sign-in with a valid cookie. Only a refusal from the server (a 4xx other than 429) ends the session now; no answer, a 429 or a 5xx leaves the token as it was, the call fails as it would offline, and the next request tries again (`refreshSession` rejects with `SessionUnreachableError`; the interceptor and the focus check ignore it). A cold load with the server unreachable still shows sign-in. Tests: `api.test` (no answer, 503, 429; `refreshSession` keeps the token).
- **(Client P3-6) Jar ledgers: only the latest read lands.** Two reloads in flight (two saves in a row) could answer in either order and an older ledger overwrite a newer one. `useJarLedgers` numbers its reads as the Dashboard's `refreshJars` does. Test: `jarLedgers.reload.test`.
- **(#301 P3-4a) Allocation's "Pocket money added" note checks the month again** after re-reading the preview, so it never appears under a month the user moved to meanwhile. Test: ALLOC-068b.
- **(#301 P3-4b) Getting Started reads today on every check**, not once when the app loaded, so a tab left open over a month's end asks about the new month. Test: `GettingStartedChecklist.test`.
- **(Client P3-1) The register builds its line options once per render**, not once per row: a keystroke in a 300-row register called `buildItemOptions` 302 times, now twice per render. The full fix the review sketched (a memoised row component holding its own drafts) is a larger rewrite of the 2,700-line page and is left out. Test: `TransactionsPage.renderCost.test` (62 calls on `main` for 60 rows; under 20 now).

**Validation (this machine).** From `main` at `0bae18b` (#301): `npx vitest run` **900 passed** (75 files); each new test fails with its fix stashed. `npm run lint` clean apart from the pre-existing `AuthContext` warning; `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json` clean.

**Review round 1** (on `f76ced2`, write-up in the project's `reviews/pr-302/`): no P1.
- **(P2-1) Only no answer at all keeps the session.** `/auth/refresh` rotates the cookie before it answers, so a 5xx (or an answer lost on the way back) can leave the browser holding a cookie the server has replaced; kept signed in, the tab's next request replayed it outside the 30-second grace window and the theft response signed out every device. Any answer from the server, a 5xx or 429 included, ends the session again as on `main`; only a request that got no answer keeps it. The lost-answer case remains as on `main` apart from the delay before the replay; loading the user and building the answer before the rotation is saved belongs to the server (F2). Tests: `api.test` (no answer keeps it; 500, 503 and 429 end it).
- **(P3-1)** The review's rows and the bulk category and member controls are disabled while Import is out (the bulk member after round 2), so no answer can be given to a row already on its way (one the server took would lose it without a word). The test now checks the controls are disabled and the held row opens again.
- **(P3-2)** A failed call during an unreachable refresh is still counted by the API-error tracking.
- **(P3-3)** Getting Started's checks are numbered: a focus check and a navigation check answering out of order keep the latest. Test: `GettingStartedChecklist.test` "keeps the latest check".
- Validation: `npx vitest run` **902 passed**; lint and `tsc` as before.

**Deploy.** Client code only; nothing to run.

## Follow-ups F2: server fixes and hardening (the end-to-end check's SC-P3-1, SC-P3-4, RP-P3-1, TX-P3-2, S-P3-1, S-P3-5, MG-P3-2; #302 review P2-1's server half) — 1 October 2026

**Scope.** The second follow-up PR from the open list of 1 October (`plans/2026-10-01-open-follow-ups.md`, group 1). No migration, no data step.

- **(SC-P3-1, rule 58) The jar card estimates like the edit preview.** The card passed today's balance to the planner only when a month was skipped, so with money arriving this month it said "about 6 more months" where the preview said 4. `JarDto` always passes it now.
- **(RP-P3-1, rule 37) "Review N transactions" counts a split whose slice is on no line.** The slice's money was in Uncategorised but its transaction was not in the count (income likewise). A split counts once however many of its slices are loose. `ReportFiguresTests` pinned the old count and now expects 2.
- **(SC-P3-4) Two requests closing the same jar cycle get "reload and try again", not a 500.** Two pages settling a cycle on the first day after its deadline, or a double-clicked Mark paid or Dismiss, both added the cycle and the second save tripped the unique index. The exception middleware answers that collision (`IX_JarCycles_…`) with the 409 it already gives a concurrency conflict. SQL test: `FollowUpsF2SqlTests`.
- **(TX-P3-2) A camt.053 statement declaring a DTD is refused** ("a bank statement never does") instead of being expanded; nothing outside the document is resolved.
- **(S-P3-1) Expired refresh tokens are deleted** for the login on each sign-in and rotation. Rotated tokens that have not expired stay, so replaying a stolen one still meets the theft response.
- **(S-P3-5) The sign-in answer's role is the role in the household the login acts in** (its chosen household while it still belongs to it, else its own), as each request resolves it, not whichever membership came first (`ActiveHouseholdSelector.ActiveRoleAsync`, used by login and by refresh, Google and the other auth answers).
- **(#302 review P2-1) A refresh's rotation and its answer stand or fall together.** `AuthController.Refresh` saved the new token before loading the login and building the answer; a failure there gave a 500 with the rotation kept, and the browser, still holding the replaced cookie, met the theft response on its next refresh. The rotation now commits only with the answer.
- **(MG-P3-2) The start-up departure backfill asks the database for jars with no departure** instead of reading every posting a moved jar ever had and filtering in memory; once every moved jar has its departure, a start-up reads nothing.

**Left out (still on the open list).** S-P3-4 (answer 404 rather than 403 for another household's id) touches about 50 handlers and their tests for little gain, since ids are GUIDs. S-P3-6 (reset and confirmation tokens in the link's fragment) changes the email links, the two pages and about 15 tests that read the links. MG-P3-1 (backfill saved per household) would change deploy-time code that has nothing to do in production. The live Cloudflare list (#298 P3-3) would add a network call at start-up. Each is better as its own small PR if wanted.

**Follow-ups (not in this change).** (#302 review P2-1, accepted by Christopher on 1 October: "Accept") A renewal whose answer is lost on the way back (the server rotated the cookie, the browser never got the new one), followed more than 30 seconds later by the browser's replay of the old cookie, meets the theft response and signs out every device. Rotating and answering together (above) closes the server-error half; the lost answer would need a longer grace for an unused replacement, a security trade-off he chose not to make. (#303 review P3-2) The cycle collision's 409 also reaches the reads that settle cycles (GetJars, GetJarSchedule, the skip preview): two page loads at once on the first day after a deadline show "reload and try again"; SettleAsync could treat the collision as already settled. (#303 review P3-3) The Refresh transaction (rotation commits with its answer) has no test.

**Tests.** `FollowUpsF2Tests` (4: the card estimate, the split counted, the DTD refused, expired tokens pruned), all failing on `main`; `LoginTests` (+2: the role in the chosen household, and in the login's own when none is chosen); `FollowUpsF2SqlTests` (1: the second close recognised as a collision).

**Validation (this machine: .NET SDK 10.0, SQL Server 2022 in Docker).** From `main` at `7614994` (#302): `dotnet build` 0 warnings; unit suite **1401 passed**; SQL integration suite **124 passed**.

**Review round 1** (on `262d25c`, write-up in the project's `reviews/pr-303/`): no P1.
- **(P2-1) Pruning deletes set-based.** Two refreshes of one login that both read its expired tokens before either saved made the second delete affect 0 rows, so it failed with a 409 and signed that tab or device out. On SQL Server the expired rows are now removed with one `DELETE` (`ExecuteDeleteAsync`); a concurrent refresh that already removed them is no conflict. Tests: `RefreshPruneRaceSqlTests` (two devices, two tabs with one cookie, and a control), which fail on `262d25c`.
- Validation: unit **1401 passed**; SQL **127 passed**; build 0 warnings.

**Deploy.** No migration or data step.

## Follow-ups: jars and cycles (D13 to D18)

- **D13 (RP-P3-4):** archiving a person with "Cancel remaining" keeps the plan of a pocket-money line whose payment still waits for its converted amount, and the preview no longer offers that line for cancelling (`PocketMoneyLines`). Tests: `PocketMoneyFixPlanETests.Cancelling_keeps_the_plan_of_a_line_whose_payment_waits_for_its_converted_amount`; SQL `JarFollowUpsSqlTests.D13_…`.
- **D14 (SC P3-2):** typing an amount on a skipped month of a live jar (this month or later) un-skips it and re-spreads the other months around the typed amount (`UpdateBudgetItem`). Tests: `JarLifecycleW04Tests.Rule61_…AndTypingAnAmountAlsoUnskipsIt` (now 30 / 135 / 135), `D14_TypingAnAmountOnASkippedLaterMonth_RespreadsTheOtherMonths`.
- **D15 (SC P3-5):** changing a Recurring jar's date, or its kind, while a cycle is missed first records that cycle with the outcome Dismiss would give it then (Missed while short, FundedLate once the target is held; shared `JarCycles.UnpaidCloseOutcome`) and its balance the day before the deadline, then moves the date (`UpdateJar`). Tests: `JarCycleTests.D15_ChangingTheDateWhileACycleIsMissed_RecordsItAsMissed_ThenMovesTheDate`, `D15_ChangingTheDateWhenAMissedCycleIsNowFunded_RecordsItAsFundedLate_AsDismissWould`.
- **D16 (#297 review P3-1):** removing a transfer's return, or dating it later, is checked with `JarHistoryChecks.EnsureTransferDrawAsync` unless the jar has left the account since, which keeps #280's "Accept and settle" (`UpdateTransfer`). Tests: `JarHistoryChecksTests.D16_…` (3), the existing `TransferIdentityW07ReviewTests` move tests; SQL `JarFollowUpsSqlTests.D16_…`.
- **D17 (#301 review R2):** a cycle whose payment was removed before its deadline reads "waiting for the deadline" (outcome `Waiting`) until it is judged, and can still be given a payment (`JarDto`, `jarStatus.ts`, `JarsPage`). Tests: `SchedulesCyclesFixPlanETests.D17_…`, `jarStatus.test` "reads a cycle whose payment was removed…".
- **D18 (#301 review P3-3):** a planned amount or name typed on an archived jar's line is refused ("Holiday is archived and plans no contributions…"), so a skipped later month stays skipped for the restore; the Budget page shows the server's reason. Tests: `JarLifecycleW04Tests.D18_AnArchivedJarsLine_RefusesATypedAmount_AndKeepsItsSkip`, `DashboardPage.jars` DASH-083.

**Review round 1** (on `3b69232`, write-up in the project's `reviews/pr-304/`): no P1.
- **(P2-1, D14) A typed amount counts only inside the plan window.** Typing on a skipped month after the deadline's last contribution month (a Recurring jar's next cycle, a Goal past its date) made the replant count it as saved now, so the months before the deadline shrank and the cycle was missed. `UpdateBudgetItem` now replants around the typed amount only when the month is inside the window (`FundingCompletionMonth` of the current deadline, or the current month once that has passed); outside it the skip just ends. Test: `JarLifecycleW04Tests.D14_TypingAnAmountOnASkippedMonthAfterTheDeadline_LeavesThePlanBeforeItWhole` (Recurring and Goal).
- **(P2-2, D13) A waiting line is still asked about, and its kept plan ends once the amount is confirmed.** The preview and the archive count the line's plan in what they ask about again (`PocketMoneyMonth.Remaining`) and say a payment waits for its converted amount (`WaitingForConvertedAmount` in the preview, a sentence in the 400 and in the People page's dialog); a cancel still keeps that line's plan. `ConfirmConvertedAmount` on an archived person's pocket-money line in a month after the current one then cancels that month as the archive would (`PocketMoneyLines.TrimAfterConfirmAsync`). The current month's keep-or-cancel choice isn't stored, so a line there keeps its plan (follow-up below). Tests: `PocketMoneyFixPlanETests.Cancelling_keeps_the_plan_…` (now Remaining 50 and the question), `Confirming_the_waiting_payment_cancels_a_later_months_kept_plan_for_an_archived_person`, `Confirming_the_waiting_payment_keeps_the_archive_months_plan_while_the_choice_is_not_stored`; SQL `JarFollowUpsSqlTests.D13_Confirming_…`; client PPL-085 "a payment waiting for its converted amount is still asked about and named".
- **(P2-3, D15) A Recurring date with a recorded cycle is refused.** Setting the date back to a deadline the jar already has a cycle row for (missed by D15, paid or rolled over) reopened it, and every way out then hit `IX_JarCycles_JarId_Deadline` (409 forever). `UpdateJar` refuses it before anything is written: "A cycle due on {date} is already recorded for {jar}. Pick another date." Tests: `JarCycleTests.D15_ChangingTheDateToADeadlineWithARecordedCycle_IsRefused` (Missed, Paid, Funded); SQL `JarFollowUpsSqlTests.D15_Changing_the_date_back_to_a_recorded_missed_deadline_is_refused_and_the_jar_can_move_on`.

**Follow-ups (not in this change).** (P3-1) `PocketMoneyLines.HasWaitingPaymentAsync` runs one query per line in `ReadFromAsync` and `CancelAsync` (N+1); one grouped query over the month's line ids would do. (P3-2) D16's `JarLeftSinceAsync` skips the check whenever the jar left the account on or after the departure, including a jar that went A to B and back to A; the departure settle absorbs the draw on the day it left, so no wrong balance was found, but it matters if arrival dates come with the jar-move work. (P2-2 remainder) Confirming a waiting payment on the archive month's own line doesn't trim it after "Cancel remaining", because that choice isn't stored; trimming it needs the decision (or the archive date) recorded on the person.

## Follow-ups: open design answers (O3, O4, O9)

**Scope.** The HANDOVER's open design questions O3, O4 and O9, answered by Christopher on 1 October 2026 (21:47) with the suggested options; recorded in the HANDOVER's O3, O4 and O9 bullets and in P29 and P41. One migration, `JarLineTypedByHand`: additive (a `bit` column on `BudgetItems`, default false), no data change at deploy.

- **O3a** An overdrawn Goal still can't be completed (P41); `CompleteJar`'s refusal names the shortfall in the budget currency ("Sofa is 50.00 EUR overdrawn. Cover the 50.00 EUR shortfall … first, then mark it completed."), and the Jars card opens the dialog for an overdrawn Goal, which says the same with nothing to confirm. Tests: `JarLifecycleW04Tests.Rule55_Completing_IsRefused_…`, `LedgerLockSqlTests` (the racing completion), client `JarsPage.lifecycle` "O3a" and JAR-095.
- **O3b** Typing a jar line's amount sets `BudgetItem.IsPlannedByHand`; every replant (`JarPlanner.ReplantAsync`, and the planner's other readers through `JarPlanOptions.ByHandMonths`) keeps a later month's typed amount and spreads the rest over the other months, as around a skipped month; skipping or planning the month again, archiving or completing clears it; an amount typed on the current month is not marked and is replanted as before (P29), while one typed for a later month keeps it once that month arrives (review round 1). The edit preview flags those months (`ByHand`, `ByHandLater`) and the form lists them as "Kept as typed". Tests: `OpenDesignAnswersTests.O3b_*` (3), `OpenDesignAnswersSqlTests.A_hand_typed_later_line_keeps_its_amount_through_a_retarget`, client `JarsPage` "O3b".
- **O3c** `POST /jars/{id}/reopen` (`ReopenJar`) makes a completed Goal active again and replants this month and later ones from what the jar holds; an archived jar is refused ("Restore it first"); the card offers **Reopen** with a confirmation dialog. Tests: `OpenDesignAnswersTests.O3c_*` (3), the SQL test above, client `JarsPage.lifecycle` "O3c"; `client/e2e/mocks.ts` answers the endpoint.
- **O4** `IncomeAllocator` flags a by-income step that had no income to follow (`AllocationStep.FellBackToEqual`; it already split equally) and the allocation response carries `equalSplitNote`, shown on the Allocation page. Tests: `IncomeAllocatorTests.O4_…`, `AllocationIncomeFromBudgetTests.O4_…`, client `AllocationPage.catalog` "O4".
- **O9a** A refund may sit on its purchase's jar line from any account (`RefundRules.PurchaseJarsAsync`, passed to the line checks by create, link, assign and edit; `DefaultLineAsync` no longer leaves it uncategorised): it nets the line while its money stays where it arrived; P20 holds. Tests: `OpenDesignAnswersTests.O9a_*` (2), the SQL refund test.
- **O9b** A refund of a purchase paid from a jar that is now a completed Goal, or of a paid cycle's payment, reaching the jar's account with no jar named in the rule 78 prompt, goes back into the jar (`RefundRules.JarToRefillAsync` in create and link); the cycle stays paid; the prompt says so for a refund. Tests: `OpenDesignAnswersTests.O9b_*` (3), `OpenDesignAnswersSqlTests.Refunds_net_the_jar_line_from_another_account_and_refill_a_completed_goal_from_its_own`, client `TransactionsPage.jars` "O9b".

**Review round 1** (`reviews/pr-305`, adversarial review of 208e098). (P2-1, O3b) A month typed by hand lost its amount in the month it was typed for: once it became the current month the next replant rewrote it. `UpdateBudgetItem` now marks a jar line only when it is typed while its month is still a later one, and `SavingsLines.ByHandMonths`/`ByHandMonthsByJarAsync` keep marked lines from the current month on, so a figure typed for a later month survives its arrival while one typed on the current month is still replanted (P29); skip, plan again, archive and complete still clear the mark. (P2-2, O9b) Linking an already-spent receipt as a refund put money into the jar that the account no longer had: `LinkRefund` now refills the jar only when moving the receipt's amount out of unallocated funds on its date leaves unallocated at or above zero, and no jar unbacked, on that date and every later one (`JarHistoryChecks.FirstRefusal`, as a dated reallocation); otherwise the refund is linked and its money stays unallocated. A refund created as such (`CreateTransaction`) adds its money to the account and the jar together and can't leave unallocated short, so it is unchanged. (P3-4, O4) `FellBackToEqual` (and so the note) only when the step had something to share among more than one member. Tests: `OpenDesignAnswersTests.O3b_AnAmountTypedForALaterMonth_KeepsItOnceThatMonthArrives_UntilTheJarIsArchived`, `O3b_AnAmountTypedOnThisMonth_IsReplantedAsBefore_…` (now also checks the current month's line is not marked), `O9b_LinkingAReceiptWhoseMoneyWasSpentSince_LeavesItUnallocated`, `O3c_ReopeningAGoalPastItsDate_PlansTheWholeRemainderThisMonth` (pins P3-2's current behaviour), `IncomeAllocatorTests.O4_NothingToShare_OrOneMember_DoesNotFlagTheStep`.

**Validation (this machine, .NET 10, SQL Server 2022 in Docker, after review round 1).** Unit suite **1434 passed**; SQL integration suite **133 passed**; client unit **910 passed**, lint clean apart from the existing `AuthContext` warning.

**Follow-ups (not in this change).** (#304 review round 2 P3) `PocketMoneyLines.TrimAfterConfirmAsync` runs for the confirmed transaction's own line only, so a split payment with a slice on the pocket-money line is not trimmed. A Goal reopened after a month ended completed reads that month as live (completion keeps one date, no history of stretches, unlike archiving's `JarArchivePeriod`). Unlinking a refund that went back into a jar (O9b) leaves its jar share as stored; the edit form can change it. (Review round 1, P3-1, O3b) A rename from the Dashboard sends the client's copy of the planned amount with the name; when that copy is stale (the jar was edited elsewhere) the server stores it and marks it typed by hand, so the stale figure is kept; D11's version stamps cover this. (P3-2, O3c) Reopening a Goal whose date has passed plans the whole remainder this month (as an edit would); the Reopen dialog only says it plans from this month on, and could say the date has passed and point to Edit. (P3-5, O3b) Once marked, a line stays typed by hand even when the user types the schedule's own figure back; the only way back is Skip then Plan it again; a "Use the schedule" action, or clearing the mark when the typed figure equals the plan, would help. (P2-2 remainder) The same allocation made by hand on the edit form (an income's jar share in the rule 78 answer) is accepted even when the receipt's unallocated money was spent since, leaving the account's jars unbacked; it needs the same whole-history check. The build's existing nullability warnings in `CommitImportCommandHandler` (CS8714/CS8621) and `TransfersImportFollowUpSqlTests` (CS8604) are not from this change.

## Follow-ups: money entry, import and reports (D1, D8, D9, D10, D12)

Christopher's 1 October answers, each the recommended option (plans/2026-10-01-open-follow-ups.md, section 2).

- **D1 (CL-P3-7): a typed amount is whole cents.** 12.345 is refused with "Enter at most 2 decimal places.", never rounded: `MoneyInput` (`WholeCents` on every create validator, `EnsureAccepted` in the update handlers, slices in `SplitTransaction`) and `tooPrecise` in `lib/money.ts` on every money form. A stored amount with more decimals (P67, the jar planner) still saves unchanged (W10). Pinned by `MoneyImportFollowUpsTests.D1_*`, `money.test.ts` (tooPrecise), `TransactionsPage.test.tsx`, `DashboardPage.test.tsx`, ACCT-015 and JAR-028.
- **D8 (TX-P3-1): a row moved by hand to another account is skipped by its statement.** `UpdateTransaction` keeps the statement's account in the new `Transaction.StatementAccountId` (migration `TransactionStatementAccount`, one nullable column), and `StatementReferences.KnownAsync` knows the row there too; #299's identity rules are unchanged. Pinned by `MoneyImportFollowUpsTests.D8_*` and `MoneyImportFollowUpsSqlTests`.
- **D9 (TX-P3-3): a purchase with refunds linked can't be split.** `SplitTransaction` refuses as delete does ("This purchase has a refund linked to it. Unlink the refund before splitting the purchase."). Pinned by `MoneyImportFollowUpsTests.D9_*`.
- **D10 (JL-P3-1): deleting or lowering an income that leaves jars unbacked is saved with a warning.** `JarHistoryChecks.NewBackingDeficitAsync` compares the account's ledger before and after; `TransactionDto.Warning` and the delete's 200 `{ warning }` (204 otherwise) carry it, and the Transactions page shows it. Pinned by `MoneyImportFollowUpsTests.D10_*` and `TransactionsPage.test.tsx`.
- **D12 (RP-P3-2): Reports count calendar months.** Trends walk calendar months back from the latest budget (none before the first budget or recorded row; `HasBudget` per point), and the annual averages divide by `CountedMonths`, from the year's first month with a budget or a figure to the current UTC month (P14) or a later month with a figure (review round 1 P2-4; first written as "a later budgeted one"). Pinned by `RReportsCoverageTests.Trends_*CalendarMonths*` and `Trends_AndAnnual_CountTheSameCalendarMonths_*`, and the ReportsPage D12 tests.

**Review round 1** (`reviews/pr-306`, adversarial review of eba6697): no P1; each P2 was the same defect reached through a neighbouring path. (P2-1, D10) A transfer's receipt divided among jars is income to them (rule 78), yet lowering, re-dating or re-pointing a transfer after its jar money moved on saved with no warning: `UpdateTransfer` now reads the ledgers of the previous and new source and destination before the write and returns `NewBackingDeficitAsync`'s warning on the DTO (from the earliest of the old and new departure, receipt and return dates), and the Transactions page shows it after `PUT /transactions/{id}/transfer`. `ConfirmTransferLegs` is not the same defect: it only fills a leg that was missing, so a receipt it confirms adds money where none posted, and a departure it confirms is a payment (rule 70 lets that stand); it can't date a receipt later (that is an edit, now covered). (P2-2, D8) A row moved from the joint account into another login's private account, off the budget, was hidden from the other login by the privacy filter and offered again on the joint statement (and a commit would post it twice): `StatementReferences.KnownAsync` reads the moved-row branch (`StatementAccountId`) past the query filters, within the owner, projecting only the reference and the entry it recorded; the viewer gets nothing but the skip. (P2-3, D9) Moving a purchase with refunds linked to another line (or off every line, or folding its split onto one line) through `AssignTransaction` left the refund on the old line with no purchase; it is refused as a split is ("This purchase has a refund linked to it. Unlink the refund before moving the purchase to another line."; a refund in another login's private account counts, W13); re-saving the line it already has is not a move. (P2-4, D12) The annual `CountedMonths` counted future months that only had a budget, so creating next month's budget lowered every average: the span now ends at the later of the current UTC month and the last month with an actual figure (a budget-only later month doesn't count, O2); `RReportsCoverageTests` now expects 2 months (average 200) with a December budget read on 20 September. (P3-1, D1) The pocket-money amount (`FixedAmountPerMember`) is whole cents on the server too (`UpsertAllocationProfileCommandValidator`; the client already sends whole cents, so a stored profile re-saved from the page is unaffected). (P3-2) `UpdateTransaction` and `UpdateTransfer` skip the D10 ledger walk when the amount, dates, type and account(s) (and a transfer's fee and return) are unchanged and the jar answers are the stored ones (`JarHistoryChecks.JarAnswersMayChangeAsync`, conservative: an answer left to a default the stored one doesn't spell out is still checked), so a payee, member or note edit no longer pays for it while re-answering an income into a jar whose money was spent since still warns. Tests: `MoneyImportFollowUpsReviewTests.D10_LoweringATransferWhoseReceiptFundedJarsThatMovedOn_IsSaved_WithAWarningNamingTheDeficit`, `D10_RePointingSuchATransferToAnotherAccount_WarnsForTheAccountItLeft`, `D10_ATransferEditThatLeavesTheMoneyWhereAndWhenItWas_HasNoWarning`, `D10_AnEditThatOnlyReAnswersTheJars_IsStillChecked`, `D8_ARowMovedFromTheJointAccountIntoAnotherLoginsPrivateOne_IsStillSkippedForEveryLogin` (preview and commit), `D9_MovingAPurchaseWithARefundLinkedToAnotherLine_IsRefusedAsSplittingIs_AndAllowedOnceUnlinked`, `D1_ThePocketMoneyAmount_IsWholeCents` (the review's probes turned round; each fails without its fix), `RReportsCoverageTests.Trends_AndAnnual_CountTheSameCalendarMonths_*`, and `TransactionsPage.catalog.test.tsx` "shows the warning a transfer edit answers with (D10)".

**Follow-ups (not in this change).** (Review round 1, P3-1, D1) Jar amounts re-answered on an edit (`UpdateTransaction`, `UpdateTransfer`, `ConfirmTransferLegs`) have no cents check on the server, even a newly typed one (an API caller can store 33.333); the screens block it. It needs a per-jar compare with the stored posting (`MoneyInput.Accepts`), so a stored 4-dp share still saves (W10). (P3-2) The D10 check still loads each ledger twice and walks it for every operation date on an edit that moves money; folding it into one forward pass belongs with parked F3. (P3-3) `MoneyInput.IsWholeCents` applies two decimals to every currency, while W08 keeps accounts in other currencies and `CurrencyCode` takes any ISO code: a JPY account accepts 12.5 and a KWD/BHD account refuses 1.234; the precision should follow the currency's minor unit. (P3-4, pre-existing, next to D8) Retyping a transfer to an income or expense (`UpdateTransaction`) clears `ReceiptBankReference`, so the destination's statement offers the credit again; the destination should keep knowing it, as D8 does for a moved row. The build's existing nullability warnings in `CommitImportCommandHandler` (CS8714/CS8621) and `TransfersImportFollowUpSqlTests` (CS8604) are not from this change.

- **D12 (RP-P3-2): Reports count calendar months.** Trends walk calendar months back from the latest budget (none before the first budget or recorded row; `HasBudget` per point), and the annual averages divide by `CountedMonths`, from the year's first month with a budget or a figure to the current UTC month (P14) or a later budgeted one. Pinned by `RReportsCoverageTests.Trends_*CalendarMonths*` and `Trends_AndAnnual_CountTheSameCalendarMonths_*`, and the ReportsPage D12 tests.

## Follow-ups: people and sign-in (D2 to D7)

Decided by Christopher on 1 October 2026, all with the recommended option (`plans/2026-10-01-open-follow-ups.md`, section 2). One additive migration, `20261003120000_PersonLogins`: a new `PersonLogins` table (`HouseholdMemberId`, `UserId`, `LinkedUtc`, nullable `ReleasedUtc`; key person + login; cascade from `HouseholdMembers`), with no data step. Links made before it are recorded lazily: the current link always counts (read from the membership) and is written down the first time it changes or the login leaves or is removed.

- **D2 (client P3-8).** The browser no longer keeps the signed-in login's email, name or role (`zbb.email`, `zbb.displayName`, `zbb.role` are cleared on start); they come from the sign-in answer and `/auth/me`, and a cold load keeps the splash up until `/auth/me` answers, so the first screen never flashes signed out or with a guessed role. Onboarding waits for the login's id before it opens, and keys its progress by that id (review round 1, P3-1). Tests: `AuthContext.catalog` SESS-006, SESS-006b, SESS-006c; `LoginPage.catalog` REG-036; `AccountPage.catalog` PREF-005.
- **D3 (S-P3-3).** A signed-in accept needs a confirmed email ("Email first", #298); the invite page says so and offers the confirmation email again. Tests: `HouseholdAccessTests.Accept_invite_by_a_signed_in_user_whose_email_is_not_confirmed_is_refused_and_the_invite_waits`; `AcceptInvitePage.catalog` INV-040b.
- **D4 (S-P3-7).** Leaving asks first: `GET /api/households/leave-preview` counts the leaver's private accounts there, the Account page warns with that count, and `POST /households/leave` answers 409 with the count while the leaver holds more than it was told. Tests: `PeopleFollowUpsHttpTests` (D4 and D6); `AccountPage.test` (two new cases).
- **D5 (S-P3-8).** Only the person's own login may make their account private. A person remembers every login ever linked to it (`PersonLogins`, `PersonLoginRecords`), not only the first or the current one; while any other remembered login that hasn't released its link is still an active member of the household, no login can make the person's accounts private (Accounts page, sharing review, a new private account). Only that login can release its link ("This isn't me" on that person's row on People, `POST /api/access/people/{id}/not-me`, refused while it is linked to that person); the owner can never release another login's (Christopher, 3 October: "That login only"). A login that leaves or is removed stops counting while it is gone. Tests: `JointAccountsW13Tests.ReLinkingPeople_NeverLetsAnotherLoginMakeAMembersAccountPrivate_OnlyTheirOwnLogin_SP3_8`; `PersonLoginsTests` (four cases); `PeopleFollowUpsHttpTests` (D5).
- **D6 (S-P3-9).** The household switcher names another household by its owner's display name, else "Household", never the owner's email. Test: `PeopleFollowUpsHttpTests` (D4 and D6).
- **D7 (#298 R2-P2-1 follow-up).** "Send again" on People for a pending invite (a fresh link; the earlier one stops working) or a login still choosing its password (`awaitingPassword`, told to the owner only): `POST /api/access/members/{id}/resend`, owner-only, refused while email can't be delivered, on the auth rate limit and one send a minute per invite. Tests: `HouseholdAccessTests` (three `Send_again`/awaiting cases); `PeopleFollowUpsHttpTests` (two D7 cases); `PeoplePage.catalog` PPL-038b, PPL-038c.

**Review round 1** (`reviews/pr-307/PR-307-adversarial-review.md`, head 7c5869a):
- **P2-1 (D5).** The single `HouseholdMembers.FormerLoginUserId` kept only the first unlinked login, so an owner's login once linked to Sam blocked Sam and then, after the owner unlinked Sam and linked itself, let the owner make Sam's account private. Replaced by the `PersonLogins` record above (the migration was changed in place and renamed; `FormerLoginUserId` is gone from the PR). The review's probe `Gap_OwnerOnceLinkedToSam_BlocksSam_AndThenHidesSamsAccount` now fails; its regression is `PersonLoginsTests.OwnerOnceLinkedToSam_CanNeverHideSamsAccount_ByUnlinkingSamAndLinkingItself`, with `TheWronglyLinkedLogin_ReleasesItself_ThenSamsOwnLoginMayMakeItPrivate`, `ALoginThatLeavesOrIsRemoved_NoLongerBlocks`, `ACurrentLinkMadeBeforeTheRecord_IsWrittenDownWhenItChanges` and, over HTTP, `PeopleFollowUpsHttpTests.Relinking_Sam_to_the_owner_never_lets_the_owner_make_Sams_account_private_until_Sams_login_leaves_D5`. The SP3_8 case in `JointAccountsW13Tests` now has the owner's login say "This isn't me" on Sam before Sam's own login chooses.
- **P3-1 (D2).** Onboarding progress is stored under the login's id from `/auth/me` (`zbb.onboarding:<userId>`), not its email; a record kept under the email is moved across once and deleted. Tests: `Onboarding.test` (a new move-across case; the others now read the id key), `onboarding.catalog` (id key).
- **P3-2 (D7).** "Send again" saves the new invite link only once its email went out (`CommitResentInviteCommand` after the send), so a failed send leaves the old link working. Test: `HouseholdAccessTests.Send_again_on_a_pending_invite_issues_a_new_link_and_the_old_one_stops_working`.
- **P3-3.** The owner's member list reads which logins still wait to choose their password in one query (`IIdentityService.MustChangePasswordAmongAsync`), not one `FindByIdAsync` per login.
- **P3-5 (D2).** A cold load that never learns the login's role (no role in the session answer, and `/auth/me` failing or malformed) falls back to Read-only, never Owner, so no owner controls show on a guess; a role the sign-in or refresh answer gave is kept. Test: `AuthContext.catalog` SESS-006d (SESS-006c now checks the refresh answer's role is kept). A Viewer whose refresh gives its role was already safe; the server refuses either way.
- **People UI (D5).** A sign-in once linked to a person sees "Your sign-in was linked to Sam before." with a "This isn't me" button on that person's row. Test: `PeoplePage.test` (new case).
- **Follow-ups, logged:** P3-4, any editor can move Sam's shared account to the owner's person through the account's holders (`UpdateAccountCommandHandler.ReplaceHoldersAsync`) and then make it private; this was there before the PR, the holder change shows on the account, and the review's probe `Gap_OwnerReassignsHolder_ThenMakesSamsAccountPrivate` still passes. Ask Christopher whether to close it. P3-6, "Send the confirmation email again" always says it was sent (`resend-verification` answers 204 either way); check the startup "Outbound email provider" line before deploying.

**Review round 2** (head 54c08b7): clean. Two optional follow-ups, logged:
- **P3-7 (D2).** On a shared browser, another login's old email-keyed onboarding record (`zbb.onboarding:<email>`) stays until that login signs in there again; only the signed-in login's own record is moved across. Purging any leftover `zbb.onboarding:*@*` key after the move would clear it.
- **P3-8 (D5).** A blocked Sam reads "Only Sam's own login can make their account private" and isn't told that another sign-in once linked to Sam has to say "This isn't me" first (that login does see the note on People).

## Follow-ups: edit conflicts (D11)

- **D11 (two logins editing the same record; "Build it").** Transaction (both legs of a transfer are one row), Account, Jar, BudgetItem, HouseholdMember, BudgetCategory and AllocationProfile carry a SQL Server `rowversion` (`IVersioned.Version`, migration `20261003190000_AddEditVersions`, ordered after `PersonLogins`: seven new columns, no data change). Their DTOs return it (base64 `version`); the eight edit endpoints (UpdateTransaction, UpdateTransfer, UpdateAccount, UpdateJar, UpdateBudgetItem, RenameBudgetCategory, UpdateHouseholdMember, UpsertAllocationProfile) take it back, and `IApplicationDbContext.ExpectVersion` on the tracked entity actually saved (UpdateJar's and UpdateTransfer's are the ones read under the ledger lock) refuses a stale one with 409 "Someone else changed this. Reload and try again."; a change committed between that read and the save fails the UPDATE's own check, which the middleware words the same way (the ledger-row race keeps its wording). A save that only changes the rows under a record (a profile's rules, an income's jar split) writes the record back, so its version still moves. A missing version is no check, for a client loaded before the deploy. One-click actions send none. Client: each edit form sends the version it loaded and on a 409 shows the message and reloads the record into the open form; the budget grid keeps its lines' versions current from every write's answer. Pinned by `EditConflictSqlTests` (7, SQL), `editConflicts.test.tsx` (transaction, transfer, account) and `DashboardPage.test` "edit conflicts (D11)" (2); `JarLedgerSqlTests`' stub deletes now carry the row version. Built on `0bae18b` and rebased onto main after #307 (`c7c5370`): the handlers keep #302 to #307's logic (D1's `MoneyInput` checks, D14's unskip replant, O3b's typed-by-hand mark, D15's recorded missed cycle, D16's transfer-draw check, D18's archived-line refusal) with the version check beside it; the Dashboard's line save shows the server's reason (D18) or, on a 409, the conflict and a reload. No edit form added since saves one of the seven records without its version (the Allocation page's "link savings" sends the person's).

- **Side-effect audit (rowversion makes every UPDATE and DELETE of the seven tables a checked one).** Two concurrent writes to different columns of one row used to both land; now the later one fails. Where that can happen without an edit form:
  - **Cycles settled on a read** (`GetJars`, `GetJarSchedule`, `GetJarSkipPreview` call `JarCycles.SettleAsync`, which advances the jar and replants its lines): a page load racing an edit of the jar or of one of its lines, or another page load settling the same cycle, would now answer 409. They call `JarCycles.SettleOnReadAsync`: a `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` or a cycle-index collision is taken as settled by the other write, the context's unsaved settle is dropped (`IApplicationDbContext.DiscardChanges`) and the read answers from the database; the next read settles whatever is still due. This also closes #303 review P3-2 (two page loads closing one cycle). Tests: `SettleOnReadSqlTests` (2, SQL: two settles at once record the cycle once; a settle meeting a rename answers with the rename and the next read settles), both failing with the plain settle.
  - **Settles and replants inside a command** (UpdateJar, UpdateBudgetItem, Mark paid, Dismiss, Skip, CreateBudgetMonth, the jar and transaction commands that replant): read and written within one request; a concurrent change of the same row is a 409 "reload and try again" on a user action. Left as is.
  - **Start-up backfills** (`MembershipSeeder`, `OwnerMemberSeeder`, `JarDepartures.BackfillAsync`): insert only (memberships, a first member, departures and line write-downs as `JarAllocations`); the departure backfill reads transactions untracked under the ledger locks. Nothing to handle.
  - **Pocket-money sync** (an explicit button on Allocation), **allocation re-run** (deletes superseded in-transit transfers read before the source lock: a transfer confirmed or edited meanwhile is now refused instead of deleted) and **import commit** (match targets are read under the ledger locks; the accounts and lines it reads before the lock are not written): a race is a 409 on the user's own click. Left as is.
  - **Leaving, unlinking or removing a login** (writes the person's link and its `PersonLogins` record, D5): a one-click action; a race is a 409. Left as is. "This isn't me" writes only the `PersonLogins` row, which carries no version.
  - No raw `UPDATE`, `ExecuteUpdate` or stub attach touches the seven tables; the `catch (DbUpdateException)` blocks (a duplicate month, an import reference collision) rethrow anything else, a concurrency failure included.

**Review round 1** (`reviews/pr-308/PR-308-adversarial-review.md`, head 2e2a408): no P1.
- **P2-1 (the split editor saved last-write-wins).** `SplitTransactionRequest`/`SplitTransactionCommand` take the version the split editor loaded, and `SplitTransaction` calls `ExpectVersion` on the tracked transaction: a split saved over another login's split is a 409. A split rewrites only the slices and jar postings, so the transaction row is written back and its version moves; an edit form loaded before the split is refused too (its "paid from" answer, written for split rows as well, used to replace the split's). The split editor sends the row's version and on a 409 shows the message and reopens on the slices saved since. Audit of the other user commands that can change only a transaction's slices or jar postings: `AssignTransaction` (clearing a split from a row with no line; a new "paid from" on the line it already has), `ConfirmTransferLegs` (a confirmed receipt date re-sent with a jar division) and `LinkRefund` (linking the purchase it already follows can still refill a jar, O9b) now call `IApplicationDbContext.TouchVersion`, the same write-back with no check (one-click actions, which send no version). `ConfirmConvertedAmount` always writes the row, `DeleteTransaction` removes it, `CreateTransaction` makes a new one. Left alone, with no SaveChanges hook: jar postings written by settles, departure backfills, allocation runs and a jar's move to another account, and slices whose line is deleted (the database clears their line), so a page load or a jar edit never makes an open transaction form stale. Tests: `EditConflictSqlTests.A_split_saved_from_a_stale_load_is_refused_and_keeps_the_other_logins_split`, `A_split_moves_the_transactions_version_so_an_edit_form_loaded_before_it_is_refused` (the review's two probes turned round) and `One_click_writes_under_a_transaction_move_its_version` (clearing a split; a re-sent receipt with a jar division), each failing without its fix; `LinkRefund`'s touch is the same call and not pinned separately. Client: `editConflicts.test.tsx` "a split sends its version; a 409 shows why and reopens the split editor on the slices saved since"; the exact-body split PUT assertions now include `version: null`.
- **P2-2 (a second edit of a Dashboard line while the first save was in flight read as someone else's).** A line's writes on the Dashboard (amount, rename, who earns it, bill day, paid) go one after another (`DashboardPage` `saveLine`): a write made while an earlier one on the same line is still saving waits for that answer and sends the version it returned (kept per line from each answer, `budgetModel.itemVersionIn`), so the user's own correction is saved, not refused with "Someone else changed this" and reloaded to the first value. The line stays editable, and its saving dot stays until the last answer. A write that fails while a later one on the line waits is neither rolled back nor reported (the later one carries the latest value and settles the line); one that fails after an earlier write on the line also re-reads the month, since its snapshot need not be what the server holds. Test: `DashboardPage.test` "a second edit made while the first is still saving waits for it and sends the version it answered" (the review's probe turned round: one PUT until the first answer, then the second with that answer's version, no conflict message).
- **P3-1 (logged, no code).** `GET /jars` settles cycles on read in parallel with the month read, so on the first load after a Recurring deadline the grid can hold the jar line's pre-settle version and amount, and the first amount typed on it gets the 409 before the reload (self-healing after one reload). The jar form's schedule preview (`GetJarSchedule`) settling across midnight UTC can do the same to the form's own save. Later: re-read the month after a jars read that settled something, or word the 409 neutrally ("This changed since you opened it. Reload and try again."), which also suits a second tab of the same login.
- **P3-2.** A malformed or over-long `version` (not base64, or not a row version's eight bytes) answers 400 under `Version` ("This version is not one the app gave out. Reload and try again.", `EditVersions.Ensure`), not 409. Test: `EditConflictSqlTests.A_malformed_version_is_a_bad_request_not_a_conflict`.

**Parked (Christopher, 3 Oct; plans/2026-10-01-open-follow-ups.md section 4):**
- A consistent look. Start only after the current fixes are deployed and he's confident in the functionality. Use shadcn/ui (free, MIT; React 19, Tailwind v4, Vite) with its colour settings mapped to the brand tokens in client/src/index.css and dark mode kept on the existing `.dark` class. One PR adds the building blocks under client/src/components/ui with no screen changes, then one screen per PR. A paid kit (shadcn UI Kit Pro, $79 once) is optional later and needs the GitHub repo made private first, because paid kits forbid publishing their code; Tailwind Plus is closed to new buyers. Notes: plans/2026-10-03-site-template-shortlist.md.

**Validation (this machine: .NET SDK 10.0, SQL Server 2022 in Docker; after review round 1).** On main `c7c5370` (#307 merged) plus this change: `dotnet build` with only the two existing `CommitImportCommandHandler` warnings; unit suite **1459 passed**; SQL integration suite, `Http/` included, **151 passed** (a fresh database migrated through `20261003190000_AddEditVersions`); client `npx vitest run` **938 passed** (76 files), `npm run lint` clean apart from the existing `AuthContext` warning.

**Deploy.** One migration, additive (`rowversion` columns are filled by SQL Server as they are added); no data step. A client loaded before the deploy sends no version and is not checked.

## Release record: #289 to #302 deployed to production — 1 October 2026

GitHub Actions ran jobs again from 30 September about 20:23 UTC, and Christopher started a Deploy by hand ([run 36772323402](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36772323402), `46412e6`, #289). Production deploys one run at a time (the `production-deploy` group), so each later merge to `main` started a newer Deploy run and its queued production job replaced the pending one behind that approval; those superseded runs ended cancelled. Staging deployed every run.

**Merged since #289 (UTC, squash commits):** #289 `46412e6` (30 Sep 20:09:23); #295 `5413241` (1 Oct 10:43, tests only: the receipt test's date and the journey specs clicking Dashboard); the Dependabot bumps #290 to #294; #297 `6bb0e77` (12:35); #296 `670c441` (12:52); #298 `ad02b71` (13:28); #299 `0d84d22` (13:42); #300 `bfbe860` (17:02); #301 `0bae18b` (17:33); #302 `7614994` (21:47).

**Deployed (1 October).** Christopher approved production twice, a minute apart. Run 36772323402's production job (`46412e6`, #289) ran 21:55:47 to 21:56:20 UTC and succeeded, then [Deploy run 36931029061](https://github.com/maskit89/AllotJar/actions/runs/36931029061) (number 206, `7614994`, #302) ran 21:56:52 to 21:57:26 and succeeded; staging had it at 21:49. `Deploy.ps1` polls `/health` until it answers 200 and fails the job otherwise, so both successes mean the API started and answered; this record did not call `/health` itself. Production now runs `7614994`, moved from #280 (`5e36d91`): #281 to #302 shipped, including the Budget to Dashboard rename.

**Migrations that ran at API start:** `ReceiptKnownBySourceReference` (#287, one `UPDATE`), `CounterpartyErased` (#287, column only) and `LoginMustChangePassword` (#298, column only). All additive. #299's pre-deploy query (`reviews/pr-299/pre-deploy-checks.sql`) was a condition of deploying a run that contained it; its result is recorded in #299's thread, not here. #298's emailed invites need the production email provider: the API's startup Event Log line "Outbound email provider" says whether email is configured.

**Not in this deploy.** #303 to #307 merged afterwards (2 and 3 October) and are not in production: `JarLineTypedByHand` (#305), `TransactionStatementAccount` (#306) and `PersonLogins` (#307) migrate at the next Deploy.

At 3 October 22:30 UTC, Deploy run 207 (`ac4a790`, #303) is waiting for approval and run 211 (`c7c5370`, #307) is queued behind it; runs 208 to 210 were superseded and cancelled.
